# Design details

Decisions and rationale behind DESIGN.md (Renaud's spec and the core). One section per area: read the section of the area that you work on before you change it. Settled items are stated as facts. Items that still need a decision or a test are in "Still open". Facts about Claude Code were checked against the docs on 2026-11-09.

## Naming and files

- "Brain Fry" in prose, `brain_fry` in code, folders and the package name. `bf` is the command.
- Global, in `~/.brain_fry/` (`BRAIN_FRY_HOME` moves it): `projects.json` (projects and views), `inbox_prompt.md`, the hook script, `extensions/ublock-lite/`, `browser/session_cookies.json` and `browser/firefox_import.json` (see Browser).
- Per project, in `<project>/.brain_fry/`: one task file per session, `<session-id>.md`, or `archive/<session-id>.md` for an archived task (since 2026-11-14, see Task file, "The archive folder"); `events/`, the spool (one file per hook event, written by the hook, deleted by Brain Fry once applied); `events/failed/`, the events that could not be applied (see Hooks, "An event that cannot be applied"); `events.jsonl`, the log of applied events (written by Brain Fry only). `consume.lock`: the reader holds it (flock) while it applies the spool, so that two apps with different `BRAIN_FRY_HOME` (`bf.lock` is per home) that list the same project cannot apply an event twice. That case is a sandbox that points at a real project. The two apps would still write task files under two separate `taskfile.LOCK`s, so a sandbox must not list a real project (DESIGN.md, Testing).
- Why a spool and not one file that the hook appends to: jq may write a long line in several `write` calls, so two hooks that end at the same moment could interleave their lines. And consuming an append-only file needs a saved offset or a rotation, both racy while hooks append. A temp file plus a rename avoids both.
- Task files are not committed. Add `.brain_fry/` to `~/.gitignore_global`. A project that wants them committed negates the rule in its own `.gitignore`.
- `bf init` in a project creates `.brain_fry/` and registers the project in `projects.json`, so the hook condition (folder exists) and the project list never disagree.
- `bf install`, once per machine: adds the hook entries to `~/.claude/settings.json` next to the existing ones, which it keeps (since 2026-11-13, Renaud's settings have no other hooks and no `statusLine`: claude-carbon, Claude Status Bar and session_number are uninstalled). Safe to run twice, keeps a backup. Adds `.brain_fry/` to `~/.gitignore_global`. Writes a first `inbox_prompt.md`, and installs uBlock Origin Lite (see Browser). Planned with the reply trailer: `trailer_prompt.md`.

## Task file

YAML frontmatter, then the rolling digest, then one section per thread (TT).

- Frontmatter: `session_ids` (a fork gets its own file, see Split), `title`, `short_title` (2 or 3 words, the heading of the card, see Status, inbox, board), `cwd` (where the session started: `--resume` must run there), `transcript_path`, `project`, `prio` (working, next, planning, later, more_later, never), `rank` (the place in its column, see Status, inbox, board, "Order in a column"; since 2026-11-16), `status` (working, blocked, waiting, done), `live`, `inbox`, `unread`, `held` (a reply that waits for the digest's verdict, see Hooks, "Background work"), `parent` (the task that this one was forked from), `resume_note` (planned, see Resume note), `created`, `updated`, `opened` (last selected in Dev mode, see Project dropdown), `open_files`, `terminal_tabs`, `browser_tabs` (URLs, see Browser), `finder_tabs` (see Finder tab), `grid` (where each tab of column 3 sits, see 2a, 3a, 3b; since 2026-11-14).
- Thread section: timestamp, kind (review, answer, rule, FYI, wait, blocked), digest, the question verbatim if there is one.
- Layout: `# Notes` (only when the task has notes: see Modes, a new task in Organize mode; the other files stay as they were), `# Digest`, then `# Threads` with one `## n | timestamp | kind` section per thread, with the parts `**Digest**`, `**Question**`, `**Prompt**`, `**Reply**`, and, once the reply trailer is built, `**Sonnet kind**` when Sonnet disagrees with it. All free text is written as blockquote lines (`> ...`), so no content can be mistaken for structure, whatever headings, fences or `---` lines it holds. The parser reads only the lines without `>` as structure.
- A thread is born at UserPromptSubmit (kind `working`, with the prompt). Stop adds `last_assistant_message` as the reply and sets the kind `pending` (planned: the kind of its trailer, when it has one); the digest then sets the final kind. A new prompt before any Stop (Esc) marks the previous thread `interrupted`. A Stop without a prompt before it (a background task that finishes, or hooks installed mid-session) makes a thread without a prompt.
- The task file keeps the prompt and the full reply of every thread: the digest needs them, and the transcript may be gone after 30 days.
- Until the first digest, a task shows the first line of its first prompt as its title.
- The task file is the record. Claude Code transcripts are deleted after 30 days by default, and their JSONL format is documented as internal. A task must hold everything that Brain Fry needs; the transcript is read as little as possible.
- Per-task workspace: open file tabs, browser tabs and terminal tabs (the panes of column 3) belong to the task and are restored when the task is selected. The explorer root (2a) is the task's project.
- YAML (Renaud, 2026-11-14: "ok, use lib"): `taskfile.loads` reads the frontmatter with libyaml's safe loader (`yaml.CSafeLoader`), or with the pure-Python one when PyYAML was built without libyaml (`yaml.__with_libyaml__`). Never an unsafe loader. Checked on the 133 real task files: both loaders gave equal tasks. 18 broken frontmatters gave errors of the same kind with both. Reading all 133 files took 76 ms before and 17 ms after (medians of 9). The writer stays `yaml.safe_dump`: libyaml's dumper wrote the 133 frontmatters byte for byte the same, but it writes an emoji as `"\U0001F600"` and a NEL as `"\N"`, so a later title or path would change the text. It would save about 0.3 ms per save.
- The archive folder (approved by Renaud on 2026-11-13: "9 kiss, e.g. move them to an archive folder"; built 2026-11-14). The file follows the status: a task with `status: done` lives in `<project>/.brain_fry/archive/<id>.md`, any other task in `<project>/.brain_fry/<id>.md`.
  - Why: on 2026-11-13, 122 of the 130 task files were archived, with about 30 new tasks per day. The backend read all of them every 3 s (`_refresh_outside`), at every state push and every 60 s (the sweep): 139 ms per read, and a push of 400 KB, 90% of it archived tasks.
  - `taskfile.save` writes the task where its status puts it and removes the copy at the other place: an archive, an unarchive, the Stop of a `close` turn, a prompt or a Stop in an archived task's session (status working or waiting: the file moves back, and the event never makes a second task). Written first, removed second: a crash in between leaves two copies, and the top folder's wins (`taskfile.path_of`, and the migration below). An event that leaves the status at done (a SessionEnd, an idle notice) keeps the file in the archive.
  - Every lookup by id goes through `taskfile.path_of` (the top folder, else the archive): `actions.locate` and `load`, the top of `events._apply`, the digest's reload (`digest_task`, `_release`).
  - `taskfile.all_tasks(project)` and `actions.all_tasks()` read the top folder only: the periodic reads. `taskfile.archived_tasks(project)` and `actions.archived_tasks()` read the archive on demand. The first sweep of a run looks for pending threads in both (`Core._sweep`).
  - Migration: at each start of the app (`Core.start`, before the loops), every task file of a top folder that has `status: done` moves to the archive (`actions.migrate_archive`, a rename, which keeps the file's time). On Renaud's next start, 122 files. It also repairs the crash case above.
  - Measured on 2026-11-14 on a copy of the real files (132 task files, 124 archived; times are medians, on the scratch disk, so faster than the 139 ms of the real folders): before, one read of all tasks 81 ms and a push 84 ms and 441 KB; after, a read of the open tasks 5 ms, a lean push 6 ms and 36 KB; a push with the archive asked (the board with H, or a search) 84 ms and 441 KB, as before. The migration took 91 ms. The project order's archive times: 3.5 ms to compute, 0.1 ms cached.
  - Tested in `tests/test_archive_folder.py` (the moves both ways, by command and by events, the lookup, the migration, two copies, the project order, the lean state and `archive.show`, the sweep) and in a sandbox selftest (below, Modes, "Search on the board").
- A moved project (2026-11-14). The folder that holds a task file is the task's project, whatever the file's `project` says. `taskfile.load` compares the two (`paths.project_of`: the parent of `.brain_fry/`, also for a file of `archive/`). When they are not the same folder (`os.path.samefile`, so two spellings of one folder count as one), it sets `project` to the folder and moves a `cwd` that is inside the old project to the same place inside the new one (`taskfile._rehome`). A `cwd` outside the old project stays. The correction is made in memory at each load, and the file gets it at its next save. `bf.log` has one line per move and run. Not corrected: a text without a path (`taskfile.loads`) and a file that is not in a `.brain_fry/`.
  - Why: Renaud renamed two project folders, `serendipity` to `brain_fry` and `llm_dream_interpreter` to `personal_coaching` (reported on 2026-11-14). The task files moved with their folder, and `taskfile.save` wrote to `Path(task.project)`, the old place: `FileNotFoundError`. In `events.consume`, the first event of the spool (a SessionEnd of an archived task) failed every 0.5 s, and the 13 events behind it waited, so the new tasks showed no prompt and no reply. In `Core._refresh_outside`, `actions.set_live` failed every 3 s.
  - What a move needs from Renaud: the new folder in `projects.json` (`bf init` there, or File > Add New Project...). The old entry may stay: a project without a folder is skipped.
  - Not corrected by the loader: `transcript_path` (the transcripts stay where Claude Code filed them, see below), absolute paths in `finder_tabs`, and the `wrap:<project path>:<path>` keys of `window.json`.
  - Repaired by hand on 2026-11-14: `project` and `cwd` in the 107 archived files of `brain_fry` (these two lines of the frontmatter only, the file times kept), so that the running app applied its spool without a restart. The 3 files of `personal_coaching` were left to the loader.
  - What a move leaves behind outside Brain Fry (seen on 2026-11-14):
    - Claude Code files the transcripts and the memory of a project in `~/.claude/projects/<the project's path, with - for every / and _>/`, and a moved project gets a new, empty one. The old one kept 104 transcripts (384 MB) and 25 memory files. A session in the new folder starts without the memory. `claude --resume` of an older task runs in the new `cwd`, where Claude Code probably does not find the transcript (not tested). The memory files were copied to the new folder on 2026-11-14; the transcripts were not moved.
    - `.venv/bin`: the scripts of the dependencies (`pytest`, `websockets`, ...) and the `activate` scripts keep the old path in their first line. uv rebuilds only the project's own script (`bf`). `uv run pytest` then cannot start `.venv/bin/pytest`, runs the next `pytest` of the PATH (miniconda's) and fails with `No module named 'brain_fry'` in every test file. Repair: `uv sync --reinstall` while the app is closed (the app runs from this venv), or replace the path in these scripts (done on 2026-11-14).
  - Tested in `tests/test_archive_folder.py` (a renamed project: the open and the archived tasks, a `cwd` in a subfolder and one outside, the SessionEnd of an archived task and a prompt applied from the spool, nothing written to the old folder; a file outside a project).

## Hooks

Source: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md

- The hooks page on 2026-11-13 (read for the review of 2026-11-14, partly through a summary) lists 33 events. Facts that bear on known gaps, beyond the events that Brain Fry uses: `PermissionRequest` fires when a tool call needs a permission decision, before the dialog (payload `tool_name`, `tool_input`), so it would turn a task red about 6 s before the Notification and say what is asked; not used. No event fires when a dialog is answered by hand (`PermissionDenied` is for auto mode only, `PostToolUse` fires after a granted call succeeded) and none on Esc, so Claude Code's process files stay the source for the end of a block. `SessionStart` has the `source` values startup, resume, clear, compact and fork, and its stdout goes to the model's context, as the design of the reply trailer assumes. Notification types on the page: permission_prompt, idle_prompt, auth_success, auth_storage_failure, elicitation_dialog, elicitation_url_dialog, elicitation_complete, elicitation_response, agent_needs_input, agent_completed, quota_auto_resume_fired, quota_auto_resume_stale, quota_auto_resume_disabled, plugin_notification. In the events.jsonl files of 4 days only idle_prompt (276), permission_prompt (12) and auth_success (1) appear. Not checked: whether `elicitation_url_dialog` and `agent_needs_input` should be in `events.BLOCKING`.

- Stop fires every time Claude finishes a turn and waits for input. It does not fire on a user interrupt (Esc). SessionEnd fires when the CLI exits. The inbox trigger is Stop, not SessionEnd.
- Notification fires with `notification_type` `permission_prompt`, `idle_prompt`, `elicitation_dialog` (and others). `permission_prompt` and `elicitation_dialog` fire when Claude is stuck waiting, after about 6 seconds without typing: the blocked case. `idle_prompt` fires 60 s after a query ends, when nothing runs (see "Stale orange through `idle_prompt`" below).
- Every payload has `session_id`, `transcript_path`, `cwd`, `hook_event_name`. Stop adds `last_assistant_message` (use it: the transcript may not yet contain the final message). Notification adds `message` and `notification_type`. StopFailure adds `error`, `error_details` and `last_assistant_message` (see "A turn that ends on an API error" below). Session id = task id. The project comes from cwd, see the walk-up below.
- Hooks run synchronously, Claude waits for them. Default timeout is 600 s for command hooks.
- Registered once in `~/.claude/settings.json`, so they run in every project. Events used: UserPromptSubmit, Stop, StopFailure (since 2026-11-14), Notification, SessionEnd. Direct edits of the hooks in settings files are "normally picked up automatically by the file watcher" (hooks page), so a running session needs no restart after `bf install`. SessionStart is planned, to print the trailer instruction (see Reply trailer).
- The hook is bash plus jq, not `uv run`, because it runs at every turn end. It exits at once when `BRAIN_FRY_INTERNAL` is set. Otherwise it walks up from `$cwd` to the nearest folder that has `.brain_fry/`, and exits if there is none. The walk stops below `$HOME`, because `~/.brain_fry/` is the global folder, not a project. It writes the payload, plus `bf_ts` (epoch seconds, from jq's `now`), as one file in that folder's `.brain_fry/events/`. It prints nothing, except my editor selection on UserPromptSubmit (see Selection as context): on UserPromptSubmit and SessionStart, stdout is injected into Claude's context.
- Walking up, and not only checking `$cwd`, keeps the sessions that start in a subfolder or in a worktree under `.claude/worktrees/`. For nested projects, the nearest folder wins.
- Hook processes inherit the environment of the `claude` process (documented). That is what makes the `BRAIN_FRY_INTERNAL` guard work.
- Seen on 2026-11-09 (Claude Code 2.1.318): the Stop payload also has `prompt_id`, `background_tasks`, `permission_mode`, `effort`, `session_crons`, `stop_hook_active`. At the end of a `-p` run, SessionEnd has `reason: other`. Tested end to end with `claude -p --settings` that adds the hook: an event from a subfolder lands in the project's spool.
- `claude --resume <id>` keeps the session id and appends to the same transcript. `--fork-session` gives a new id with the full history. Resuming one id in two terminals interleaves both into one transcript.

Event --> status:

| event | status | color |
|---|---|---|
| UserPromptSubmit | working | orange |
| UserPromptSubmit of a background result while a turn runs | working; no thread of its own, nothing marked `interrupted` | orange |
| Notification permission_prompt, elicitation_dialog | blocked | red |
| Notification idle_prompt while working, the last thread `working` (no Stop came) | waiting, `inbox` and `unread` on, the thread `interrupted`. `live` stays on | green |
| the dialog closes (no hook: Claude Code's process file, see below) | working, or waiting after a refusal. `inbox` off | orange |
| Stop | waiting (needs me) | green |
| StopFailure (the turn ended on an API error) | waiting, `inbox` and `unread` on, the thread `interrupted` with the error as its reply | green |
| Stop while background agents or workflows run | working, `inbox` off (not done yet) | orange |
| Stop while a background shell or monitor runs | working, `inbox` off, `held` until the digest: kind `wait` keeps it so; any other kind, or a failed digest --> waiting, `inbox` and `unread` on | orange, then orange or green |
| SessionEnd | unchanged, but working or blocked --> waiting (nothing runs any more), `inbox` and `unread` on (the turn was cut off). `live` off | unchanged, or green |
| archive (my action) | done | gray |
| Stop of my `close` (or `x`) prompt, reply with `closed :-)` | done (archived), `inbox` off | gray |

- A task started from Brain Fry is born at once: `bf` runs `claude --session-id <uuid>`, so it knows the id before the first hook. Prio working, status waiting, `inbox` off. A session started elsewhere is born at its first UserPromptSubmit, Stop or Notification; SessionEnd or StopFailure alone creates no task. When its project was registered in the middle of the session, its earlier turns are imported from its transcript as `imported` threads (Renaud, 2026-11-10), and so does a session that started before `bf install` and is resumed since. The event's own turn is found by `prompt_id`, which is the `promptId` of its prompt in the transcript (checked on 2026-11-10), else by the prompt's text. A first Stop whose `prompt_id` is not the last prompt's (a background task) gets a thread of its own. Sessions with no `.brain_fry/` in `$cwd` or a parent are ignored. Quick questions and scripted `-p` runs inside a project do create tasks; archive them with one key.
- Background work (Renaud, 2026-11-10: "When the agent is still working in the background, it gives me a little message saying, 'Hey it's still working,' and then it pops into my inbox. I don't want it to pop into the inbox if it's not done."). A turn ends while background agents run: Claude Code shows "Waiting for 1 background agent to finish" and fires Stop. The Stop's `background_tasks` (one entry per task in flight: `id`, `type`, `status` `running` or `pending`, `description`, plus `agent_type` or `command`) is there to tell "session is done" from "session is paused waiting for background work to wake it" (its description in the binary of 2.1.322). When it holds an agent or a workflow, the task stays working (orange) and out of the inbox; the reply is kept in its thread, `pending`, and digested as usual. Each result comes back as a turn that Claude Code starts with a UserPromptSubmit whose prompt is `<agent-message from="<agent id>">` (a subagent) or `<task-notification>` (a shell): seen in events.jsonl, 2026-11-09 and 10. When it comes after the Stop, that turn is a thread of its own, and its Stop goes to the inbox once nothing holds it. When it comes while the turn still runs, it joins the open thread (since 2026-11-14, see "A background result during a turn" below). Code: `events.background_work`.
  - Agents and workflows only, as Claude Code's own "Waiting for N background agents and M dynamic workflows to finish" (it counts `pendingBackgroundAgentCount` and `pendingWorkflowCount`). A background shell or a monitor may never end (a dev server, a watch) and would hold its task out of the inbox for ever, so it does not hold its task by itself: the digest decides (next bullet).
  - A waiting agent out of the inbox (Renaud, 2026-11-11, on a reply of task `336ef1ec` that said "I'm waiting, and I'll be notified when the cleanup task stops working": "It's basically waiting and there's even the flag FYI. I don't need these tasks to show in my box."). A Stop whose `background_tasks` holds a shell, a monitor or an unknown type in flight, and no agent, is `held`: working (orange), out of the inbox, until the digest of its reply. The digest has a sixth kind, `wait`: Claude waits for something other than me and will go on by itself. `wait` keeps the task working and out of the inbox; any other kind puts it in the inbox (waiting, `unread`), as the Stop would have done. A failed digest puts it in the inbox too: a reply never waits for a digest that does not come. The next UserPromptSubmit, blocking Notification, Stop or session end clears `held`; an archive in the meantime stays (`actions.settle_hold`). The shell's result starts the next turn (`<task-notification>`), whose Stop goes to the inbox once nothing holds it.
    - Why not all FYI replies, as Renaud's words said: on 2026-11-11, 74 of the 211 digested threads were FYI. Most were `close` turns (archived anyway), but some answered my own questions ("What do you mean by a pin key?", "would it be a good idea to start with Theia", "how many lines of code"), which I want to read. The two replies that only said "I'm waiting" (`336ef1ec` and `d28fea7d`) both had a background shell in flight at their Stop.
    - Why both conditions, the shell and the kind: a shell alone may be a dev server (5 Stops with a monitor of an artifact's live updates had replies that needed me); the kind alone may be Sonnet's mistake on "I wait for your answer", and without anything in flight nothing can wake the agent. `wait` without a hold changes nothing.
    - Cost: a reply with a shell in flight reaches the inbox about 5 s later (the digest's time); 7 of 201 Stops had one (events.jsonl of all projects, 2026-11-11). A shell that never wakes the agent (a wait loop with a bug) keeps the task orange; the idle close after 4 h ends the session, and the task then goes to the inbox ("Turn cut off"). Crons (`session_crons`) do not count as something in flight: none seen yet.
    - The trailer (not built) would give the kind at once, without the 5 s.
  - The type test is `workflow`, or a type that contains `agent` (the hook's label is `subagent`; other agent kinds fall back to their raw name). An unknown type, or a status other than running or pending, goes to the inbox: a wrong guess then costs one inbox item, not a reply that never shows.
  - SessionEnd while the task waits for its agents: as for any working task, waiting (green) and in the inbox (see "Turn cut off" below). The agents died with the session, so their results will never start a turn: only my "Continue" restarts it. Until 2026-11-10 it stayed out of the inbox, because the last reply only said that it would wait.
  - An agent that hangs keeps its task orange. The stale orange through `idle_prompt` (2026-11-14, below) does not cure it: Claude Code sends no `idle_prompt` while a background agent runs, and the last thread is `pending`. The idle close after 4 h ends the session, and the task then goes to the inbox.
- Turn cut off (Renaud, 2026-11-10, after asking whether restarting the app to load new code is that bad): when the `claude` of a working or blocked task ends, the task goes to the inbox (`inbox` and `unread` on, status waiting, the last thread `interrupted` when no reply came). Its row says `interrupted: <prompt>`, or shows the reply when one came (background agents). I resume it from there: select it, "Continue". Before, it only turned green, and my prompt had cleared `inbox`, so it left my sight at every restart. One rule, `actions.end_session`, for every way a session ends: the SessionEnd hook, the app's quit (`set_live` right after the kill: the hook's SessionEnd is applied only at the next start and then changes nothing), the leftovers of a crash (`owned.json`), the idle sweep, and the process list for sessions outside the app. A session that I end myself in the middle of a turn (Ctrl-C twice) goes to the inbox too. A session that ends while idle stays where it was.
  - Is a restart in the middle of a turn bad? Not much. On 2026-11-10 (until 17:30), 6 restarts ended 44 sessions, and 2 of them were in a turn (counted from events.jsonl: a UserPromptSubmit with no Stop before the SessionEnd). The conversation survives: Claude Code appends each message to the transcript, and `--resume` brings back everything up to the kill. Lost: the reply being written and the tool call in flight. The real risk: the app sends SIGHUP to the process group (`ptys.py`), so a command that the agent runs dies with it (a git rebase or merge left in progress, a stale `index.lock`, a half-written file), and the resumed agent does not know how far it got. Background agents and workflows die too. SessionEnd fires on that SIGHUP, with `reason: other` (seen in events.jsonl).
  - Rejected for now: keeping the `claude` processes alive across a restart (a detached PTY holder such as dtach or tmux, reattached at start). It would remove the cut, but changes how the app owns its PTYs.
- A turn that ends on an API error (built 2026-11-14, picked by Renaud in the review of that day). Claude Code fires StopFailure instead of Stop (hooks page, read 2026-11-13; in the binary of 2.1.323 it fires at the error exits of the turn loop, which then returns without a Stop). Its payload: the common fields, `error` (rate_limit, overloaded, authentication_failed, oauth_org_not_allowed, account_on_hold, billing_error, invalid_request, model_not_found, server_error, max_output_tokens, cloud_credential_error, unknown; also the hook's matcher), `error_details` (for example "429 Too Many Requests") and `last_assistant_message`, the error text (for example "API Error: Rate limit reached"). No `background_tasks`. Until then the hook was not registered for it, so such a task stayed orange and out of the inbox. Seen on 2026-11-12 in task `ee8c522e`: a turn of more than two hours ended on "API Error: Your computer went to sleep mid-response", without a Stop.
  - Rule: status waiting, `inbox` and `unread` on, as after a Stop. The open thread (or a new thread without a prompt, when none is open) gets the error text as its reply, else "API error: <error> (<error_details>)", and the kind `interrupted`. So no digest runs on an error message (the digest takes only `pending` threads), and the inbox row shows the error (`views.inbox_line` shows the reply of an `interrupted` thread that has one). StopFailure alone creates no task, as SessionEnd. Code: `events.error_text`.
  - `bf install` registers it (`install.HOOK_EVENTS`). The banner asks for `bf install` while `settings.json` lacks one of these events, not only when the copy of `hook.sh` differs (`install.hook_state`, called by `Core._hook_state`). A `settings.json` that is not valid JSON counts as up to date: the check cannot tell.
  - Not seen yet: a real StopFailure payload. The field names come from the hooks page and the binary.
- Stale orange through `idle_prompt` (built 2026-11-14, picked by Renaud in the review of that day). Stop does not fire on Esc, nor on an API error before StopFailure was registered, so the task stayed orange and out of the inbox until my next prompt or the end of its session. The Notification `idle_prompt` ("Claude is waiting for your input") tells that the turn is over. In the binary of 2.1.323 (the idle notifier, the code that calls it with `notificationType:"idle_prompt"`), Claude Code sends it once per end of a query, after the idle threshold (60 s), and only while no query runs, no background agent runs, no dialog is open, no loop wake-up or automatic resume after a quota is armed, and I have not interacted since the query ended. In events.jsonl it arrived 276 times in 4 days, and 4 times while a turn was open (no Stop since the prompt): 60 s, 634 s, 691 s and 739 s after the last prompt. Agent turns longer than 60 s are common, and only 4 open turns got one: the 60 s run from the end of the last query, not from the prompt.
  - Rule: `idle_prompt` while the task is working and its last thread is `working` --> the turn is over. Status waiting, `inbox` and `unread` on, the thread `interrupted`: the same result as `actions.end_session` gives a turn cut off, but `live` stays on. Its row says `interrupted: <prompt>`.
  - Left alone: a task that waits for its background agents, and a held one (their last thread is `pending`); a blocked task (it is in the inbox already, and the end of its dialog or its Stop clears it; Claude Code sends no `idle_prompt` while a dialog is open anyway); a task after a refusal (`actions.end_block` made it waiting and took it out of the inbox, on purpose).
  - Self-correcting: a Stop or a StopFailure that comes while the last thread is `interrupted` without a reply fills that thread, instead of opening a thread without a prompt (`events._open`). This covers an `idle_prompt` that was wrong, and also a turn that the app marked cut at its quit (`set_live`) while its Stop was still in the spool, to be applied at the next start: that turn had ended after all. A later turn always starts with a UserPromptSubmit, which opens its own thread, so the only turn that a Stop can end is the last prompt's.
  - In the replay of all events (below), the 4 tasks go to the inbox at the `idle_prompt`. With the old code, 3 of them stayed orange for 10 to 12 hours, until their session ended, and 1 for 16 minutes, until my next prompt.
  - Not covered: an Esc followed within 60 s by a background result (the result joins the open thread, next bullet), and an agent that hangs (see "Background work").
- A background result during a turn (built 2026-11-14, picked by Renaud in the review of that day). Claude Code also delivers the result of a background agent or shell while the turn still runs: the UserPromptSubmit of `<agent-message from="...">` or `<task-notification>` arrives while the last thread is `working`, and the turn's Stop comes after it. Until then that prompt marked the open thread `interrupted` and opened a thread of its own, which then got the reply. In the task files of 2026-11-14, 36 of the 73 `interrupted` threads were followed by such a prompt; 16 of them were my own prompts, and for 7 of these the reply sat under a thread labelled `<agent-message from="...">`. Nobody had interrupted them: in task `ee8c522e`, two `<task-notification>` prompts arrived 69 and 138 minutes into one turn, and the transcript shows one turn from my prompt to its API error, with no end in between.
  - Rule: a prompt not typed by me (`events.typed_by_me`) while a turn runs (`events.in_turn`: the last thread is `working` and the status working or blocked) opens no thread and marks nothing. The flags change as for any prompt (working, `inbox` and `unread` off). The turn's Stop puts the reply in the open thread.
  - Unchanged: a background result after a Stop (last thread `pending` or digested), or after an `idle_prompt`, a StopFailure or a refusal, opens its own thread. A prompt that I type marks an open thread `interrupted` and opens a new one, as before. The label of a machine thread in 1b is unchanged (see Still open).
- Replay of these three rules (2026-11-14): the applied events of the 8 projects (events.jsonl, about 1,300 events) through `events.apply`, with the committed code and with the new code, into a scratch folder. Without the import of earlier turns (`transcript_path` blanked: today's transcripts also hold every later turn) and without the app's own actions (quit, refusal, archive, digest). Threads: 453 --> 416. `interrupted` threads: 75 --> 38. Marked by my prompt 28 --> 27, by a background result 37 --> 0, by a session end 10 --> 7, by `idle_prompt` 0 --> 4. Threads whose prompt is a background result: 66 --> 29. The 37 background results joined 18 open threads: 13 of my prompts and 1 thread of a background result got their reply; 4 ended `interrupted` (by an `idle_prompt`, my next prompt or a session end). No StopFailure in the data (the hook was not registered).
- Red clears when the dialog closes: I granted or refused the permission, or answered the question. No hook fires for that (PreToolUse and PostToolUse would fire on every tool call in every project). Claude Code's process file (`~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json`, see Claude Code's process files and transcripts) has `status` `waiting` while a dialog waits for me, with `waitingFor` (seen in the binary of 2.1.322: `input needed`, `dialog open`, a worker or sandbox request, or the dialog's own label), and `statusUpdatedAt` (ms). The scan every 3 s ends the block when a blocked task's process is `busy` (working) or `idle` (a refusal ended the turn: waiting), with a `statusUpdatedAt` after the Notification. That check rejects a snapshot of the files from before the block: the dialog opens about 6 s before the Notification fires. The task leaves the inbox, as after an answer, and the front end advances (see Auto-advance).
- Found on 2026-11-10 (Renaud: "I clicked on the right permission but the task did not disappear"): before this, red cleared only at Stop, so a granted task stayed red in the inbox for the rest of the turn. In that case the dialog was an AskUserQuestion, which also fires `permission_prompt`.
- Without Claude Code's process files (the folder is missing, or untrusted), red clears at Stop, as before.
- How to check an internal format of Claude Code: the binary (`readlink -f "$(which claude)"`, 320 MB) holds its JS source. A Python `mmap` scan for a key (here `statusUpdatedAt`, then `status:"waiting"`) prints the code around it in seconds. `grep -E` with a context pattern did not finish in 120 s, and ugrep refused it (complexity limit). Also fast: `LC_ALL=C /usr/bin/grep -a -b -o 'key'` for the offsets, then `dd` around each one. Minified names repeat across chunks: search for a literal string, not a function name.
- To see what Claude Code writes to a terminal (used for its links on 2026-11-10): a Python script that runs `claude "<prompt>" --model haiku --settings '{"disableAllHooks": true}'` in a PTY (`os.openpty`, `TIOCSWINSZ`, the variables of `CHILD_ENV_DROP` removed, `BRAIN_FRY_INTERNAL=1`, a scratch folder) and saves the raw bytes. Three traps: the prompt must come before `--allowedTools`, which takes several values and swallows it; the trust dialog of a new folder starts on "No, exit" (send Down, then Enter); at start Claude Code sends OSC 11, DA1 (`ESC[c`), XTVERSION, the kitty keyboard query and OSC 7501 (the probe answered OSC 11 and DA1, as xterm.js does). To see what Claude Code does with input (a paste, for the drops from Finder on 2026-11-14): the same PTY without a prompt, in a folder that is already trusted (the repo), then write to the PTY and read the screen text. Nothing is sent, so there is no turn and no transcript. Three Ctrl-C did not end it within 2 s, and the probe killed it: see the process file that this leaves (Claude Code's process files and transcripts).
- Sessions started outside Brain Fry (plain `claude` in a terminal) fire hooks and appear on the board, but have no terminal in Brain Fry. They are read-only until SessionEnd, then resumable. Brain Fry also reads Claude Code's list of running processes, so it knows where they run, and when they end even without a hook (see Claude Code's process files and transcripts).

- An event that cannot be applied (2026-11-14, "fix it" by Renaud). When `events.apply` raises, the reader logs the error with its traceback, moves the event file to `<project>/.brain_fry/events/failed/` (`events._set_aside`, `paths.failed_dir`) and goes on with the next event. This happens at the first failure: no retry.
  - Why: before, the file stayed in the spool. The reader failed on it every 0.5 s, wrote a traceback each time, and every later event of the project waited. Seen on 2026-11-14 with a task file that named a folder that was gone (Task file, "A moved project"): 14 events waited, and the tasks showed no prompt and no reply.
  - Nothing reads `events/failed/`, and such an event is not in `events.jsonl`. To apply it again once the cause is gone, move its file back to `events/`: the reader sorts by `bf_ts`, so it is applied at the next pass. An event of the same task that was applied meanwhile came later, so the order is then wrong for that task.
  - Not covered: a failure after `apply` (the line of `events.jsonl`, the delete) still ends the pass, and the event is applied again at the next one. An unreadable file is renamed `*.bad`, as before.
  - The cost of "at the first failure": a cause that passes (a full disk) also sets its events aside. A retry would need a count per file. Not built.
  - Tested in `tests/test_events.py` (`test_an_event_that_cannot_be_applied_is_set_aside`: the event behind it is applied, no second try, the file moved back is applied).

## Digest (Sonnet via claude -p)

- Input, rolling: the task's current digest plus the last thread (the user prompt and `last_assistant_message`, tool calls reduced to tool names and file paths, tool outputs dropped). Bounded cost; has goal, question and answer. Weakness: summary errors compound over many turns. Accepted.
- Output, fixed structure through `--json-schema`: title, short title (2 or 3 words), digest of three to five lines, kind (review, answer, rule, FYI, wait, blocked), question verbatim or null. `wait` (added 2026-11-11) is also described in the schema, so that an `inbox_prompt.md` older than it still gets it; see Hooks, "Background work". The kind field is what makes the inbox an owner inbox and not a notification list. Planned: when the reply has a trailer, the trailer's kind wins (see Reply trailer).
- One Stop = one turn = one thread. Normally one digest call per turn. The backlog at startup is batched: one call per task covers all of its turns that were not digested yet, and still returns one thread section per turn.
- Title: the first digest sets it, later digests do not change it (a card title that changes every turn is hard to follow). A rename: Rename in the right-click menu of an inbox row (2026-11-15, see Modes). From 2026-11-10 to then the UI had no way to rename a task: the only one was a double-click on the title in the header, which was removed (see Status, inbox, board).
- Short title: set once, as the title. A rename sets both, so that my name shows on the card, whatever its length. When Sonnet returns none, the first three words of the title stand in.

```
BRAIN_FRY_INTERNAL=1 claude -p --model sonnet \
  --output-format json --json-schema "$SCHEMA" \
  --no-session-persistence \
  --settings '{"disableAllHooks": true}' \
  "$PROMPT"
```

- Recursion guard: the digest call is itself a Claude Code session and would fire the Stop hook. Primary guard is the `BRAIN_FRY_INTERNAL` variable. `disableAllHooks` is a second layer: the docs say that it overrides project settings and imply that it overrides user settings.
- `--bare` would skip hooks and CLAUDE.md, but in bare mode Claude Code does not read the OAuth login and needs an API key. Not usable with the subscription.
- Without `--bare`, every digest call loads `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`. Accepted. Running the call with cwd `~/.brain_fry/` avoids at least the project's CLAUDE.md.
- `--no-session-persistence`: no transcript for digest calls. `-p` sessions are hidden from the picker and from `--continue` anyway.
- `structured_output` is missing from the JSON when the run failed. Check before reading.
- Red items get no Sonnet call. The inbox shows "blocked: <notification message>" plus the existing digest.
- Placeholder: the Stop event carries `last_assistant_message`. Show it in the inbox at once and replace it when the digest arrives. The inbox keeps working when Sonnet fails.
- The consumer runs only while Brain Fry runs, in a background thread. On start it digests the backlog. No daemon.
- The prompt goes on stdin (no limit on argument length). Each turn sends the prompt (cut to 4,000 characters), the tools used (from the transcript, best effort: name plus file path or the first line of the command) and the reply (cut to 8,000 characters).
- The call runs without the task-file lock. Afterwards the task is reloaded and only the threads that were sent are updated, because events may have changed the task in the meantime. A thread that the model skipped becomes FYI, so that it never loops. A failed call leaves the thread pending.
- Measured on 2026-11-09: about 3.4 s per call. Tested on a real session: kind `answer`, the question copied word for word, a 3-line digest.
- To check a change of the prompt on real replies without changing any task (2026-11-11, kind `wait`): load a task file, deep-copy it, keep the threads up to the one to test, set that one `pending` and `transcript_path` empty, then `digest.build_prompt` and `digest.call_sonnet`; save nothing. Four calls in parallel take about 10 s. Result for `wait`: 4 of 4 as expected (`336ef1ec` #7 and `d28fea7d` #3 --> wait; `336ef1ec` #4 "pin key" and `341020a6` #1 "Theia" --> FYI).

### Early summary of a new task (discussed 2026-11-10, parked by Renaud)

- Problem: a new card stays empty during its whole first turn, which can last 10 min or more, because the digest runs only on Stop. Until then the card shows the first line of the first prompt and no digest. Seen on 2026-11-10 (task `26e6084d`): the prompt was `[Image #1]` plus "Don't build it but let's spec it out.", so the card showed only `[Image #1]`.
- What is known, and when:
  - 0 s: the prompt text (hook). Useless when the topic is in a screenshot.
  - 0 s: the pasted image. The transcript's user message holds it as a base64 `image` block (53 KB here, same timestamp as the prompt), followed by a text entry `[Image: source: /private/tmp/...]` that gives a file path.
  - A few seconds, before the first reply: Claude Code writes `{"type":"ai-title","aiTitle":"..."}` into the transcript (internal format; the entry is appended again at each turn, with the same value). It costs us nothing. It sees only the text: "Organized mode tab summaries" for a text prompt (good), "Image-based feature spec" for the screenshot prompt (useless). Some transcripts also hold `custom-title` and `agent-name` entries.
  - 10 s and later: tool calls with their descriptions. In `26e6084d` the agent wrote no visible text for minutes, and the descriptions gave only weak hints.
- Proposal (not decided):
  1. No model call: until the first digest, the card shows the prompt in gray in place of the digest, and the fallback title skips `[Image #N]` lines.
  2. An early digest: the first UserPromptSubmit of a task without a title queues one Sonnet call on the prompt plus its images (from the transcript). It returns title, short title and a digest of the goal, in about 4 s. Stop still rewrites the digest. One call per new task, not per turn. A fork gets its call at its own first prompt.
  3. Do not use `ai-title`: it fails on screenshots, and it is one more internal format.
- Open for Renaud: whether the early title is final (the "set once" rule; recommended, since the prompt plus the image name what was asked) or replaced once by the Stop digest; whether one more Sonnet call per new task is acceptable.
- Not tested: images in `claude -p`, probably through `--input-format stream-json`. Fallback: give the image's file path and let the call read it (a few seconds and one tool turn more).

## App

Brain Fry is one Mac window. Chosen on 2026-11-09 instead of the TUI, see Rejected.

- One Python process. pywebview opens a native window that uses the system WebKit (WKWebView), so no browser is bundled. The front end is plain JS: xterm.js for 4a and 3b, CodeMirror 6 for 3a.
- A local websocket in the same process carries the terminal bytes between xterm.js and the PTYs, and pushes board and inbox updates to the front end. Jupyter's terminal works the same way (terminado + xterm.js).
- The app owns the PTYs. Each live session is a `claude` child process in a PTY, with `TERM=xterm-256color` and `COLORTERM=truecolor`. Each terminal tab (3b) is a shell in a PTY.
- Children get a cleaned environment. When Brain Fry is started from inside Claude Code or VS Code, it inherits about 30 variables that they set for their own children (`CLAUDECODE`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT`, `VSCODE_*`, `TERM_PROGRAM`, ...). A `claude` that sees them may believe that it is nested or attached to an IDE. The app removes a precise list of them (`CHILD_ENV_DROP` in `util.py`) and every `VSCODE_*` variable, not every `CLAUDE*` variable, because a user may set `CLAUDE_CODE_*` variables on purpose.
- The front end keeps one xterm.js instance per live session and shows the selected one in 4a. Each instance keeps its own scrollback. The backend keeps the last few MB of each PTY's output, so a reloaded front end can replay it.
- Quitting the app ends all live sessions: a turn in progress is killed, its task goes to the inbox (Hooks, "Turn cut off"), and `--resume` brings the conversation back. Quitting asks one question when it would lose something: the tasks that are working and the files with unsaved edits (2a, 3a, 3b, "Unsaved edits at quit"). Planned: closing the window keeps the app running. Built in step 3: closing the window quits (with the same question).
- Quitting, checked and fixed on 2026-11-14 (Renaud: the real bf.log had 23 starts since 2026-11-10 and one "main: quit"). Two causes, both seen in a sandbox:
  - Cmd+Q, Quit in the app menu (and in the Dock, a logout) and Ctrl-C all call `NSApp.terminate_`: Cmd+Q is the key equivalent of the menu item, it never reaches pywebview's `WebKitHost.keyDown_` (whose Cmd+Q would call `app.stop_`), and pywebview's Ctrl-C handler (`AppHelper.machInterrupt`) calls `terminate_` too. Once pywebview's delegate has said yes (`applicationShouldTerminate_` --> `closing`), Cocoa posts `NSApplicationWillTerminateNotification` and calls `exit()` inside `webview.start()`, which never returns: the `finally` of `run()` never ran. So no cookie save, no `core.shutdown()`, `owned.json` left behind (the next start logs "its session died with the last run of the app"). The sessions died anyway, because closing a PTY's master sends SIGHUP to its child. Only the window's close button returned from `webview.start()` (the one "quit" line was a window close).
  - The question deadlocked: `on_closing` runs on the main thread (inside `windowShouldClose_` or `applicationShouldTerminate_`), and pywebview's `create_confirmation_dialog` queues the alert to the main thread (`AppHelper.callAfter`) and then waits on a semaphore. So Cmd+Q or the close button while a task was working hung the app (beachball) until a force quit. Seen with a stack dump: the main thread waits in `create_confirmation_dialog`, called from `on_closing`. The real log cannot tell this hang from the first cause: both end a run with no line.
  - SIGTERM and SIGHUP (the terminal of `bf` closed) killed the process at once, with no line: Python's default action.
  - The fix (`app/main.py`): `_Quit` runs the shutdown exactly once (a lock that is never released): it logs "quitting: <why>", saves the browser's session cookies, calls `core.shutdown()`, kills the `claude --version` probe if it still runs, and logs "quit". Three callers, all on the main thread: the `finally` of `run()` (the close button, a selftest), an observer of `NSApplicationWillTerminateNotification` (Cmd+Q, the Quit menus, Ctrl-C), and the handler of SIGTERM and SIGHUP. Python runs a signal handler only between bytecodes of the main thread, which sits in Cocoa's run loop, so the handler goes through PyObjC's `MachSignals` (as pywebview does for Ctrl-C): it calls `_Quit`, then `NSApp.terminate_`, and `on_closing` answers yes without a question since the quit has begun. A signal is delivered only while the run loop runs in its default mode: during the question it waits until the alert closes, and after the window closed it is ignored (the shutdown runs already). The question itself is an `NSAlert` run directly on the main thread (`_confirm_quit`: "Quit Brain Fry?", the text of `quit_question`, buttons Quit and Cancel; Escape is Cancel). `--browser` keeps Python's default: Ctrl-C runs the shutdown, a signal kills it.
  - Children that outlived the app (Still open since 2026-11-12): the `claude --version` that `_log_start` runs at start, not a session. A sandbox's fake `claude` that exits only on `-p` sleeps on `--version` too, outside any PTY; the probe's timeout is 20 s, and a selftest quits earlier, so the daemon thread died with the app and the child stayed with PPID 1. Seen in every quit of the sandbox before the fix, the close button included; the sessions' children were always gone (`ptys.kill`, and the SIGHUP of the closed PTY). A real `claude --version` answers in about a second, so a real orphan was rare. Now the probe is a `Popen` kept in `main._probes` and `_Quit` kills it. A fake `claude` should still exit at once on `--version`, else every sandbox start waits 20 s for it and logs "claude --version failed".
  - Which way asks: the close button, Cmd+Q, the Quit menus and Ctrl-C ask (someone is there: Ctrl-C is typed in the terminal of `bf`, so the question appears in the window). SIGTERM and SIGHUP never ask: nobody can answer. A selftest logs the question and answers yes. The log has "quit: asking: <text>" and "quit: confirmed" or "quit: cancelled".
  - Also in `core.py`: once `shutdown()` has begun there is no state push and no `notify` (`request_broadcast`, `_broadcast`), and a session that ends then is not marked by `_on_pty_exit` (the shutdown marks it and removes `owned.json`; before, a late `mark` could write `owned.json` again). This removes the asyncio traceback "cannot schedule new futures after shutdown" in `Core._broadcast`: a push scheduled 50 ms after a session ended ran while Python shut down, after its executor had stopped. Not reproduced in 3 runs on 2026-11-14, so fixed by construction only.
  - Tested in a sandbox, with the real window and real `NSEvent`s (the method of "A key equivalent of the window" below): a script runs `main.run()` itself (not a selftest, so the question path is the real one) with `webview.start` wrapped to start its driver thread, `_fill_screen` replaced (1600x1000) and `_confirm_quit` replaced by a stub that logs the text and answers from a list. Each run has one task with a live session of a fake `claude`, then quits one way. Before the fix: Cmd+Q, the menu's Quit and Ctrl-C logged nothing and left `owned.json`; the close button and Cmd+Q with a working task hung (stack dump after 15 s); SIGTERM and SIGHUP killed the process. After the fix, each logs "quitting: ...", "shutdown: ending 1 process(es)", "quit", and leaves no `owned.json` and no child: Cmd+Q and the menu's Quit and Ctrl-C ("quitting: Cocoa quits the app (Cmd+Q, a Quit menu, Ctrl-C)"), the close button ("quitting: window closed"), SIGTERM and SIGHUP ("SIGTERM received", "quitting: SIGTERM"), a real `bf --selftest` ("quitting: window closed"). Cmd+Q with a working task and two unsaved files: the question named both, Cancel kept the app, a second Cmd+Q and Quit quit. With a browser tab open, Cmd+Q and SIGTERM logged "browser: quit (session cookies saved)". The real alert too, without the stub: the driver read its texts from `NSApp.modalWindow()` ("Quit Brain Fry?", then both sentences) and posted Escape (Cancel: the app stayed) and, at the second Cmd+Q, Return (Quit). `AppHelper.callAfter` runs during the alert's modal loop. Renaud has not seen the alert; the app must be restarted. The scripts (`harness.py`, `runner.py`, `st.py`) stayed in that session's scratchpad.
- Spawning a PTY child: the app has threads (Cocoa, asyncio, digest worker), so `pty.fork()` (Python code between fork and exec) is unsafe. `subprocess.Popen` forks and execs in C; a 3-line Python wrapper then takes the PTY as its controlling terminal (TIOCSCTTY, so that Ctrl-C and SIGWINCH reach the child) and execs `claude`. Tested: size, SIGWINCH, Ctrl-C.
- IUTF8 is set by its value, since Python's termios on macOS lacks the constant (2026-11-11). Before, the flag was never set, so Backspace could leave half of a UTF-8 character (`ptys.spawn`, checked in `test_ptys.py`).
- Code: `app/core.py` (state, commands, PTYs, the reader every 0.5 s, the digest worker thread, a sweep every 60 s), `app/server.py` (one port: static files, `/file/<task id>/<path>` for the images and PDFs of 3a, `/ctl` websocket for state and commands, `/pty/<task id>` websocket for terminal bytes), `app/main.py` (window, single instance through a lock on `~/.brain_fry/bf.lock`), `web/` (plain JS; the libraries are vendored in `web/vendor/`, see 2a, 3a, 3b).
- The server listens on 127.0.0.1 only, but a web page that I open in a browser can still reach it: a browser opens a websocket to any address (no same-origin rule for websockets), and DNS rebinding lets a site point its own name at 127.0.0.1. Without a check, such a page could find the port, send `shell.new` and type a command into the shell. So every request must carry `Host: 127.0.0.1:<port>` or `localhost:<port>` (a rebinding page sends its own name), and a request with an `Origin` header must come from the app's own page. Clients that are not browsers (tests, scripts) send no `Origin` and pass; they run as me anyway. Built 2026-11-10, tested in `test_app_backend.py`.
- The backend pushes the whole state after every change, coalesced over 50 ms. Simple, and cheap for dozens of tasks.
- What the state holds (2026-11-14, with the archive folder, see Task file): the open tasks; plus the archived tasks that this run of the app touched (`Core.touched`: a task id that any command names, `id` or `from`, or that an event changed; and the selected task); plus every archived task while the front end asks for them. Why the touched ones: after an archive in Dev mode, the front end still shows that task's tabs (the idle screen) and Cmd+Up goes back to it to unarchive it; an archived card opened from the board stays selected. The front end asks with `archive.show` (`on`, and `ids`: the selected task, the idle screen's and the one that Cmd+Up goes back to, so that they stay when `on` turns false) while the board is on screen and H is on or the search has text, and stops asking when that is no longer true (`askArchive` in `app.js`, called at each render; sent again at each new connection). So the lean state is the normal case in Dev mode, where the hook events arrive. A task that a command named but that no longer exists is forgotten at the next push.
  - The touched set has no cap: a run of several days keeps every task archived during it (about 30 a day), which is at worst the cost before the archive folder. A restart empties it. A cap would drop tasks that the front end still holds: their terminal tabs would then lose their shells (`shells.js` drops the tabs of an owner that leaves the state).
  - Accepted losses (2026-11-14): `live` of an archived task no longer follows Claude Code's list of processes every 3 s (`_sync_outside` reads the open tasks; Renaud runs his sessions only in Brain Fry, where the app itself keeps `live` right); the idle screen at start picks the task opened last among the open ones; Cmd+N O lists the open tasks and the archived ones that this run touched, not every archived task; a new task in a project whose tasks were all archived in an earlier run starts without the tabs of the last one (newTask finds no source; the project tabs still show).
- The sweep (every 60 s) closes an idle session through the live sessions of the app, not through the open tasks: archiving does not end the `claude` process, so an archived task may still run, and its close after 4 h must still happen (`Core._sweep`). Digest retries: the pending threads of the open tasks, plus the tasks whose digest failed (`Core.failed`), which may be archived, since a `close` turn leaves its thread `pending` on an archived task; the first sweep of a run reads the archive too (a digest that was pending or failed when the app quit). A failed task that no longer needs a digest leaves `failed`.
- Resume: `claude --resume <id>`. A task that was never prompted has no transcript to resume, so it gets `claude --session-id <id>` again; a fork that was never prompted is forked again.
- `~/.brain_fry/owned.json` lists the tasks whose `claude` runs in the app. After a crash, the app marks them not live at the next start (those processes died with it).
- Window settings (2026-11-14, approved by Renaud: they survive a restart): `~/.brain_fry/window.json` (`paths.window_file`, moved by `BRAIN_FRY_HOME`), a plain key --> value map: `layout` (the sizes of the panels and the shown columns, layout.js), `autoAdvance`, `showHidden` (the hidden columns of the board), `rvAll` and `reviewAgent` (Review mode), and `wrap:<project path>:<path>` per file. The front end keeps its helpers `stored(key, fallback)` and `store(key, value)` (app.js), so the call sites did not change; `store(key, null)` forgets a key. `stored` is synchronous and runs while the scripts load, so the server serves the map as the script `/window.js` (`window.__windowSettings = {...}`, `window_settings.script`), which index.html loads before app.js. Each `store` sends `window.set` (one key; logged at DEBUG only, no state push); a `store` before the connection opens is sent when it opens. No debounce: every caller stores once per action (the splitters at mouseup). A missing or broken file gives the defaults. The grid of a task is not there: it belongs to the task file (2a, 3a, 3b, "Where the grid is kept"). Why not WebKit's localStorage: pywebview's `private_mode` is on by default and wipes WebKit's data store at each start, and the server takes a new port at each start, so the page's origin changes anyway (found on 2026-11-10). At start 4a still takes a third of the window (`startWidths`, layout.js): the saved width of the middle column is replaced, as before. Tested: `tests/test_window_store.py`, and the sandbox selftest of the grid (2a, 3a, 3b), whose run 2 showed the explorer 60 px wider and auto-advance off, as run 1 left them.
- A session that runs elsewhere (live, but not a process of the app) shows an overlay in 4a that says where it runs (VS Code or a terminal, idle or busy): close it there, then resume it here. A button resumes it anyway.
- The project picker (Cmd+T, Task > New Task; Cmd+P, see Project dropdown) asks the backend for `projects.json` each time it opens, and a change of `projects.json` pushes the state: a `bf init` in a terminal shows at once (bug seen by Renaud on 2026-11-10).
- The picker shows the projects in the order of the dropdown (see Project dropdown): the most recently used first. Each row marks the current project and says how long ago I last opened a task there.
- Picker keys: Enter opens the highlighted project (Cmd+T: the first; Cmd+P: the first that is not the current project; until an arrow key moves the highlight), 1 to 9 open the n-th one. Each row shows its key: Enter on the highlighted row, its digit on the others.
- When a Stop arrives for the selected task, it is marked read at once (I am looking at it). It stays in the inbox.
- `bf --browser` serves the same page without a window; `bf --selftest` lets Claude check the window without a human (page text, terminal text, and snapshots taken by WebKit itself, because `screencapture` needs the Screen Recording permission). The WebKit snapshot holds the page only, not the native parts of the window (title bar, traffic lights). To see those, render the window's frame view (`window.native.contentView().superview()`) with `bitmapImageRepForCachingDisplayInRect:` and `cacheDisplayInRect:toBitmapImageRep:` on the main thread: no permission needed, and it includes the page. Used on 2026-11-11 for the title bar: a scratchpad script set the sandbox variables, replaced `main._screenshot` with that render and called `main.run(selftest=1.0, selftest_shots=DIR)`.
- A selftest that selects tasks would resume real sessions. Run it on a sandbox instead: `BRAIN_FRY_HOME` (projects.json and the logs), `BRAIN_FRY_CLAUDE` (a fake `claude` script) and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (fake transcripts) point Brain Fry away from the real files. `--selftest-js` then drives the page: set a select's value and dispatch `change`, or focus an element and dispatch a `keydown` on it (see Keys). Used on 2026-11-10 for the project dropdown and Cmd+P. The selftest also prints the menu bar (`=== menu bar:`, each menu with its items), read on the main thread. Events sent by JS do not move the focus (a synthetic click on a row of 2a leaves it where it was): focus the element by hand before a `keydown`. A right-click is a `contextmenu` MouseEvent with `clientX` / `clientY` (used for the menu of 2a). The Task menu of the menu bar: call `taskMenu(name)` in the page (`new`, `archive`, `fork`, `pin`, `auto`), and read what the menu shows in the menu bar printout, where a checked item starts with ✓ and a disabled one ends with `[off]` (used on 2026-11-15). The output shows the page at the end only: to read the steps of an async check, end it with `uiLog('SELFTEST ' + JSON.stringify(r))` and read the sandbox's `logs/bf.log` (used for the empty 4a). macOS has no `timeout` command. A selftest of an animation with the window covered (used for the fades of 4a on 2026-11-11): animations and frames stop and `onfinish` never fires, but the timings can still be checked. Right after the change, read each layer's `el.getAnimations()` (`effect.getTiming()`, `effect.getKeyframes()`), set `a.currentTime = t` on all of them for a few values of t and read the computed opacities, then call `a.finish()`, which resolves `a.finished` at once. To check a transition (used for the veil, 2a, 3a, 3b), sample the page at every `requestAnimationFrame`: push a line only when a signature changes (computed opacity, the panes on screen with their tab names, the rows of 2a, `B.placed`), then `uiLog` each line at the end. The backend selects a task at start and restores its tabs, so a check of "the first select after a start" must pick a task whose `S.work[id].restored` is still false. To check what paints on top (a z-index), take `document.elementFromPoint` at the center of the element that may show through, then `closest(...)` with the candidates: the hit is often a child, and an SVG's `className` is an object, not text (used for the modals on 2026-11-11). `--selftest-shots DIR` writes the WebKit snapshot `2_app.png` at the end of the run; `sips -c H W --cropOffset Y X` crops it to read a detail. DIR must exist: else no file is written, with no error (2026-11-11). A sandbox task that was never opened is not selected at start: call `select(id)` at the top of `--selftest-js`, then wait about 1.5 s for its tabs. Its task file keeps the terminal tabs of earlier runs, so a second run restores those shells first: find a new terminal by its pane, not as the first shell of the task (2026-11-11, the play button). A quick sandbox from real tasks (2026-11-11, the switch card): `taskfile.loads` each real task file, `dataclasses.replace` with `project` and `cwd` set to the sandbox project, `transcript_path=""`, the four tab lists empty, `live=False`, `inbox=True`, then `taskfile.dumps` into the sandbox's `.brain_fry/`. With tasks left in the inbox, `selectNone()` selects one again at once (an arrival), so a check of the photo needs an empty inbox. Auto mode refused a selftest command that contained `rm -rf` (2026-11-12): let the sandbox builder clear its own folder (`shutil.rmtree` in Python), or give each run a new folder. The fake `claude` of the sandbox makes the app log "claude --version failed: ... timed out after 20 seconds": harmless. Found on 2026-11-12 (the column toggles): the selftest window is 1600x1000 only on a screen that fits it (1470x895 on Renaud's laptop), so a check of widths must read `innerWidth`. A synthetic Cmd+Option key needs `code` (`Digit1`) as well as `key`, since the handlers read `e.code` for Option combinations; a key forwarded by a browser page has no `code`. To read a synchronous or async check without `bf.log`, append a `<pre>` that starts with `SELFTEST` to `document.body`: the page text printed at the end holds it. `focus()` on an element inside a `display: none` column does nothing, and an element that has the focus keeps it in WebKit when its column becomes `display: none`.
- To see the board with real-looking cards, copy some real task files into the sandbox project and rewrite their `project` and `cwd` to the sandbox path (also `transcript_path: ''`, `live: false`). The backend writes to the file under `task.project`, so a copy that keeps the real path would change the real task. Used on 2026-11-10 for the short titles; the copies with a digest but no short title then start their calls to the fake `claude` (they fail, as expected).
- The screenshots of README.md (`screenshots/`, 2026-11-12) show demo data only, since the repo is public. How they were made: a sandbox with three made-up projects (`load_forecast`, a git repo with 6 commits, a branch and an uncommitted change in `features.py`; a clone of this repo; `nlp_course`) and 13 task files written with `taskfile.save`. The fake `claude` is a bash script that exits 1 on `-p`, finds the id after `--resume` or `--session-id`, prints a canned ANSI screen for that id (a conversation that ends on a permission dialog) and then runs `exec sleep`. A driver script replaces `main._selftest`, so that one run takes the three shots with `main._screenshot` (Dev, Organize, Review), then calls `main.run(selftest=1.0)`. Three things to know for the next time. Every sandbox shows the "hook not installed" banner, because `_hook_state` looks for the sandbox's hook path in the real `~/.claude/settings.json`: the driver sets `core.Core._hook_state` to return `(True, False)`. A run that selects a task ends its session at quit, which marks the task interrupted, so restore the task files from a copy before each run. And `goToLine` right after the tabs are restored does not scroll: call it about 1.5 s later. Pictures taken at 1600x1000 (3200 px wide on Retina), cut to 1600 px with `sips -Z 1600`. The scripts stayed in the scratchpad and are lost.
- Links in a terminal, without moving a mouse: a fake `claude` prints OSC 8 links (`printf '\033]8;;%s\a%s\033]8;;\a' target text`), and the JS asks xterm.js's providers for the links of a buffer line, `term._core._linkProviderService.linkProviders` (internal), each with `provideLinks(y, callback)` (y counted from 1). Each link's `activate(new MouseEvent('click', { metaKey: true }), link.text)` then runs Brain Fry's handler. Used for the file links of 4a on 2026-11-10.
- Another agent may edit the same front end while a selftest runs. When its unfinished edit calls a function that does not exist yet (on 2026-11-10, `isCut` from the explorer's cut and paste broke every draw of 2a), define a stub at the top of `--selftest-js` (`if (typeof isCut === 'undefined') window.isCut = () => false;`) instead of waiting or changing its code. A browser tab in a selftest needs a page that loads: a failed load becomes `about:blank`, which is never saved. The app's own files work (`location.origin + '/icon.png'`).
- While the app runs, the selftest needs its own `BRAIN_FRY_HOME` (the single-instance lock), its own projects and a fake `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`. With the real projects it would apply their spools in place of the running app. Keys can be tested with `--selftest-js` by sending `KeyboardEvent`s to the element that has the focus in real use (usually xterm.js's textarea in 4a, not `document`), with `newTask` replaced by a function that records its argument, so that no session starts (done for the project picker on 2026-11-10).
- A key equivalent of the window (Cmd+W, Cmd+Q: pywebview's `WebKitHost.keyDown_` acts on a Cmd key that the page leaves unhandled) cannot be tested with a JS `KeyboardEvent`: it never goes through WebKit's native path. Post real events instead: a scratchpad script sets the sandbox variables, replaces `main._selftest` with its own function and calls `main.run(selftest=1.0)`; the function builds `NSEvent.keyEventWithType_...` (key down and up, `NSEventModifierFlagCommand`, the window's `windowNumber()`, key code 13 for W) and sends them with `NSApp.postEvent_atStart_` on the main thread (`AppHelper.callAfter`), after `NSApp.activateIgnoringOtherApps_` and `makeKeyAndOrderFront_`. `window.events.closing` tells that the window closed. A control run proves the check: a `window` capture listener that calls `stopImmediatePropagation` blocks the page's handlers, and the window must close. Used on 2026-11-11 for Cmd+W. Cmd+Q (key code 12) never reaches `keyDown_`: it is the key equivalent of the app menu's Quit (`terminate:`), see "Quitting" above (2026-11-14). To test a quit, run `main.run()` without `selftest` and wrap `webview.start` to start the driver: a selftest skips the question. The app menu's Quit is `menu.performActionForItemAtIndex_(i)` on the item whose `action()` is `terminate:`; the close button is `window.native.performClose_(None)` (not `window.destroy()`, which closes without `closing`). An observer of `NSApplicationWillTerminateNotification` in the script tells a Cocoa quit apart from a return of `webview.start()`. `faulthandler.dump_traceback(all_threads=True)` from the driver thread shows where a hung main thread waits. Two traps: `window.S` is undefined (a top-level `const` is no property of `window`: test `typeof S`), and the page's `select(id)` waits for a frame while the window is covered, so the driver sends `select` to the backend itself (`asyncio.run_coroutine_threadsafe(core.command(...), core.loop)`).
- Copy path and Copy relative path (and Ctrl+C in 2a) write the real clipboard of macOS with `pbcopy`, also in a sandbox: save it with `pbpaste` to a file in the scratchpad before the selftest and put it back with `pbcopy` after (done on 2026-11-10 for Ctrl+C). `pbpaste` after the run is also the simplest check of what was copied.
- A drag of a card between columns of the board works from `--selftest-js` (2026-11-11): one `new DataTransfer()`, then `DragEvent`s with it as `dataTransfer`: `dragstart` on the card, `dragover` and `drop` (both `cancelable`) on the column, `dragend` on the card; read the prio from `S.state` about 800 ms later.
- A selftest is valid only while its window is on screen. When other windows cover it, WebKit reports the page as hidden (`document.visibilityState`) and pauses `requestAnimationFrame`, and everything that waits for a frame stops: PDF.js then stops tracking the scroll position. On 2026-11-10 this made a PDF test fail in about half of the runs; every run with the window visible passed. Put `document.visibilityState` in the selftest's output and ignore the runs where it is `hidden`. `--selftest-js` runs once, about 3 s after the start; write the results into a `<pre>` appended to the page, since the selftest prints the page text. Shorter: push them into `window.__errors` (`"R: ..."`), which the selftest prints under `=== JS errors:` (used for the digit keys of the board on 2026-11-10). These `R:` lines also reach the sandbox's `bf.log` (as ERROR lines of `brain_fry.ui`): a check that some text is never logged must ignore them (seen on 2026-11-10, the notes of a planned task). A sandbox does not need copies of real tasks: task files written from scratch work (the frontmatter, `# Digest` and an empty `# Threads`), with `transcript_path: ''` and `live: false`. Simpler for a card without a session: `actions.plan_task(project, title, prio)` run with `uv run python -c` and `BRAIN_FRY_HOME` and `HOME` pointing into the sandbox (used for the × of Cmd+P on 2026-11-10). While the picker (Cmd+P, Cmd+T) is open, it takes every key and ignores the ones it does not know: send Escape before the next Cmd key, or that key does nothing. Write them with a Python script in the scratchpad: auto mode refused a Bash heredoc that wrote them. Create the sandbox and start the selftest in two separate Bash calls: auto mode also refused one command that removed the old sandbox (`rm -rf`), built a new one and started the app (2026-11-10, the Wrap test).
- A covered window can stay live (tested on 2026-11-14, not built). WKWebView has a private setting, `_setWindowOcclusionDetectionEnabled:` (pyobjc: `wv._setWindowOcclusionDetectionEnabled_(False)`), that exists on macOS 26.7. The test was a standalone pyobjc script, not the app: a window with a page that counts frames, covered after 2.5 s by an opaque window of a second process that also took the focus. macOS reported the window as not visible and the app as inactive in both runs. Detection on (the default): 35 frames per 0.5 s, then 0 and `hidden` while covered. Detection off: 35 to 36 frames per 0.5 s and `visible` while covered, and JS timers kept their 500 ms period (once per second with detection on). Not tested: a minimized window, a window on another Space. To use it: in `_selftest` only, on the page's WKWebView and on each WKWebView of `app/browser.py`, after checking that the selector exists (a private API may disappear in a macOS update); in normal use detection stays on, since it saves CPU and battery. Offered to Renaud on 2026-11-14, not answered: build it only if he asks.
- Hook events during a selftest (2026-11-10, for the selection of an arriving reply): a Python script in the scratchpad builds the sandbox, puts the first events in the project's spool (`<project>/.brain_fry/events/<bf_ts>-<sid>.json`: `session_id`, `hook_event_name`, `cwd`, `transcript_path: ''`, `bf_ts`, plus `prompt` or `last_assistant_message`; temp file, then rename, as the hook does), starts `bf --selftest` with `subprocess.Popen`, and drops more events at fixed times with `time.sleep`. Bash's foreground `sleep` is blocked, so the timing must live in the script. Events alone make the tasks: no task file to write. A fake `claude` that exits 1 when `-p` is among its arguments makes the digest fail at once, and sleeps otherwise (the PTY). A probe of the focus taken right after `select()` reads `BODY`: `showTerm` focuses the terminal in a `requestAnimationFrame`, so read it 100 ms or more later.
  - Fixed times drift: `--selftest-js` starts about 3 s after the window, plus the start of `uv run`. More reliable (2026-11-11, the auto-advance switch): the JS runs its steps in an `async` function and logs `uiLog('SELFTEST MARK <name>')` when it is ready for an event; the script polls the sandbox's `logs/bf.log` for that line, then drops the event. The script is `switch_test.py` in that session's scratchpad (not kept); rewrite it from this note.
- A sandbox with a fake `HOME` (2026-11-11, Cmd+N O): the bash of the terminal tabs then reads the sandbox's `.bash_profile` and writes its history there, not in Renaud's. Run `.venv/bin/bf --selftest ...` directly: `uv run` would look for its cache under the fake `HOME`. Task files from `actions.new_task`, `rename`, `set_open_files`, `set_browser_tabs`, `set_terminal_tabs`, `archive`, then `mark_opened` one second apart (the order of the list). A page for the browser tabs: `python3 -m http.server 8765` started in the background by the runner script, killed after. A hover is a `mousemove` dispatched on the row. Selecting another task (`select(id)`) builds its tabs and starts its shells; `wsSend(S.terms.get(key), {t: 'in', d: 'cd sub\r'})` types in one. The scripts were `ct_setup.py`, `ct_scenario.js` and `ct_run.sh` in that session's scratchpad (not kept).
- The smallest sandbox for a terminal tab (2026-11-12, Cmd+K): a fake `HOME` whose `.bash_profile` sets only `PS1`, a `projects.json` with one project that has an empty `.brain_fry/`, no task file, `BRAIN_FRY_CLAUDE=/usr/bin/true`. In `--selftest-js`, `await newShell('project:<path>', 1)` starts a project shell; read its lines with `term.buffer.active.getLine(i).translateToString(true)`; a new `WebSocket` to `/pty/<key>` gets the backend's replay as its first message. The permission check refused one long Bash call that built the sandbox and ran the selftest; files written with Write, then one `bash run.sh`, passed.
- A sandbox for Review mode (2026-11-10): a repository built by a script in the scratchpad, with `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` pointing to a sandbox file (else Renaud's git config applies: signing, hooks, excludes) and `.brain_fry/` and `.claude/` in the repository's own `.gitignore`. Without the global excludes, `git add -A` committed a worktree under `.claude/worktrees/` as an embedded repository. A task in a worktree: `actions._update(id, cwd=...)`. A task made with `actions.new_task` starts `live`, so 4a shows "runs in another terminal" in the selftest: expected. Save the selftest's output to a file and read the probe lines with `grep -E`: macOS's sed has no `\|`, and a first run lost its results that way.
- Icon: `src/brain_fry/web/icon.png`, cropped around the brain from the original image (1.2 MB, kept outside the repo), 256 px, 64 colors: 23 KB (32 colors show rings in the glow). The Dock and Cmd+Tab show `web/dock_icon.png`, the same brain without the dark background and the glow (transparent; Renaud, 2026-11-10: "an ugly black background"), built from `icon.png` by `frontend/dock_icon.py`: the bright pixels plus the dark outline that touches them, holes filled. The splash and the header keep `icon.png`, whose glow matches their background. The Dock icon and the app menu name ("Brain Fry", not "Python") are set through AppKit (`_mac_identity()` in `app/main.py`). The name of Cmd+Tab, the Dock and Activity Monitor is "Brain Fry" too (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-15; it was "python3"): LaunchServices names a process that is not an .app after the file that started it (`.venv/bin/python3`, the shebang of `bf`), and CFBundleName changes only the app menu, inside the process. `main.set_display_name` calls LaunchServices' private `_LSSetApplicationInformationItem` with `_kLSDisplayNameKey`, through ctypes, after `NSApplication.sharedApplication()` (which registers the process) and before the app becomes regular. A macOS without the call leaves "python3" and logs one line ("Cmd+Tab name not set"). Rejected options: starting through a symlink named "Brain Fry" in `.venv/bin/` (a restart at each start, a link that `uv sync` removes, and `sys.executable` would name every terminal wrapper "Brain Fry"); a real `Brain Fry.app` (a compiled launcher, about half a day, worth it only to start the app from Spotlight or the Dock); py2app or PyInstaller (a rebuild after each change). Tested: `tests/test_mac_name.py` reads the name back with `lsappinfo` (a background child, no Dock icon), and a sandbox window registered as "Brain Fry". What Cmd+Tab itself shows needs Renaud's eyes, as for the icon. pywebview's docs say that its `icon` option works only with GTK and Qt, but in pywebview 6.2.1 it also sets the icon on macOS (`webview/platforms/cocoa.py`, `BrowserView.__init__`), after the app is regular.
