The app must be a regular app (activation policy) before the icon is set: an icon set earlier never reaches the Dock or Cmd+Tab, which show the blank document of the bare python binary. pywebview makes the app regular only when it loads its Cocoa module, after `_mac_identity()`, so `_mac_identity()` sets the policy itself (found with test apps in Cmd+Tab, 2026-11-10). To check the Cmd+Tab icon, only Renaud's eyes work: `NSRunningApplication.icon()` read from another process returns the icon that LaunchServices has for the executable (the blank document), never the icon set at runtime, and the Dock is hidden (autohide, on the left), so a screenshot does not show it. What worked: small AppKit apps that live 3 minutes (one per variant, told apart by their icon), and a question to Renaud about what Cmd+Tab shows. Unexplained: the black-square icon showed in Cmd+Tab before this fix, with the same order. Splash: the icon on `#040510` (the image's background) until the first state arrives, at least 0.9 s; the window background has the same color, so there is no white flash.
- Window size (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-12: "maximum window size, but NOT full screen"; this replaces the full screen asked on 2026-11-11): the window opens as a normal window that fills its screen's visible frame (menu bar and Dock excluded), as Window > Fill does, not in macOS full screen (no Space of its own). `_fill_screen` in `app/main.py` sets that frame from `before_show`. pywebview's `maximized=True` is not used: it takes the whole screen, under the menu bar. A selftest stays 1600x1000, so that it does not cover Renaud's screen. Checked on 2026-11-12 in a test window: the frame equals `visibleFrame` and the window is not in full screen. The app must be restarted to see it.
- Title bar (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-11: "make it dark"): the bar of the window has the header's color (`--panel`, #232428), so the two join. macOS painted it light gray because the system is in light mode. `_dark_titlebar()` in `app/main.py`, from `before_show`: the title bar's view (`NSTitlebarContainerView`) gets the dark appearance (light title, the traffic lights as in dark mode), the window a transparent title bar, and the view the color. pywebview paints that view with the system's window color, which hid the transparent bar (internal to pywebview 6.2; if its view hierarchy changes, the bar stays gray). The dark appearance is on that view only, not on the window: the page, the dialogs and the browser of 3a keep the system's appearance, so websites do not switch to their dark theme. During the splash the bar is #232428 over the #040510 of the splash. Checked by rendering the window's frame view to a PNG (`cacheDisplayInRect:toBitmapImageRep:`, no Screen Recording permission needed; the WebKit snapshot of the selftest has no title bar), in a sandbox: bar and header pixels both #232428, window appearance still Aqua. Weighed with Renaud on 2026-11-11, not built: the header inside the bar, with the traffic lights over its left end (VS Code's look, about 28 px of height won). It needs a native window drag from the empty parts of the header, through pywebview's internal `WebKitHost.mouseDown_`, and a double-click that zooms: about 2-3 h. Renaud has not seen it yet; the app must be restarted.
- Dark scroll bars (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-15: "the scrollbars in the same dark style"; he picked this over own `::-webkit-scrollbar` CSS in VS Code's look, which WebKit always shows and which takes about 10 px of width): `color-scheme: dark` on `:root` in `app.css`. Before, the app styled no scroll bar, and WebKit drew the light ones of the system's appearance (macOS is in light mode; only the title bar is forced dark, above). macOS still decides whether they show always or only while scrolling. The same property turns dark the native controls that have no colors of their own (the project dropdown, the PDF zoom list, the checkboxes, the caret and placeholder of text fields), and PDF.js's own colors take their dark variant (`light-dark()` in `pdf_viewer.css`; its pages stay white, `color-scheme: only light`). The browser of 3a is a separate WKWebView: each site keeps its own colors. Checked in a sandbox selftest: the computed `color-scheme` is dark, and the page looks as before. The scroll bars themselves are not in the WebKit snapshot: with macOS's overlay scroll bars (setting Automatic), they show only during a real scroll gesture, and a `scrollTop` set by JS does not show them.
- Text selection (found on 2026-11-15, Review mode's diff): pywebview injects `body { -webkit-user-select: none; cursor: default }` (`text_select` of `create_window`, off by default), so no HTML text of the window can be selected with the mouse; the editors and the terminals select by themselves. HTML text that must be selectable needs `-webkit-user-select: text` on its element: this WebKit ignores the unprefixed `user-select` (`getComputedStyle(el).userSelect` is undefined), so a rule with only `user-select: none` does nothing. A copy handler can be tested without the real pasteboard: `new ClipboardEvent('copy', { clipboardData: new DataTransfer(), bubbles: true, cancelable: true })` dispatched inside the selection, then `getData('text/plain')` of that DataTransfer.
- Claude Code runs unmodified in xterm.js (permission prompts, plan mode, slash commands). Shift+Enter is mapped in xterm.js to ESC CR, the bytes that Claude Code reads as a new line.
- Terminal test (step 0, 2026-11-09; its spike, `spikes/terminal/`, was deleted on 2026-11-10 and is in git history). Passed: a real turn with a tool call and a permission prompt, Shift+Enter and Option+Enter, plan mode / Esc / Ctrl-C, multi-line paste and copy, resize, colors, `/help` and `/model`, Option+arrows. Not tested yet: scrollback and find, replay after a page reload, clicking a URL.
- Keys: Cmd shortcuts belong to Brain Fry, because a terminal never sends Cmd to the program inside it. Option is Meta in xterm.js (`macOptionIsMeta`). The native menu of pywebview takes some keys before the page sees them: Cmd+H, Cmd+Option+H, Cmd+Q, Cmd+Ctrl+F, Cmd+X/C/V/A (checked in pywebview's `cocoa.py` on 2026-11-10). Other Cmd keys reach the page.
- While a terminal (4a, 3b) has the focus, the target of a key is xterm.js's hidden textarea (`.xterm-helper-textarea`). The key handler leaves the keys of text fields to them (a rename, a resume note, the browser's address field), but not this textarea. Until 2026-11-10 it skipped every textarea, so no Brain Fry Cmd key worked while the focus was in 4a (Renaud: Cmd+P and Cmd+N "are not working"). The selftests had missed it: they sent the keys to `document`. Fixed and checked with a selftest that sends the keys to the textarea; Renaud has not tried it in the window yet.
- A session without events for 4 h is closed (SessionEnd --> `live` off), except when its task has the `inbox` flag (a pin alone does not keep it). `--resume` restores it, nothing is lost.
- Selecting a task without a live session runs `claude --resume <id>` at once, in the task's `cwd`.
- Each live session is one `claude` process. Claude Code is a native binary (TypeScript, compiled), 320 MB on disk here. Running instances used 80 to 220 MB each on 2026-11-09. Brain Fry has no Node dependency at runtime.

## 2a, 3a, 3b: files and terminals

- Layout of Dev mode: 1 | 2a | column 3 (the middle column: a grid of panes, see below) | 4a. The splitters between them can be dragged. Cmd+Option+1 to 4 show or hide column 1, 2a, column 3 and 4a (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-12: "comment toggle on/off le inbox panel, explorer panel, claude panel? propose des keyword shortcuts"; he picked the digits of the columns over Cmd+Shift+I / E / C). Not Cmd+digit (the tabs, see below), not Cmd+Shift+3/4/5 (the screenshots of macOS), not Cmd+B (Organize mode). The key is read by `e.code` (Option changes `e.key`), or by the digit for a key that a browser page forwards (`browser.py` already forwards 1 to 4 with the Option flag). A hidden column takes the splitter at its right with it (4a hidden: the splitter of column 3); 4a hidden, column 3 takes its place; all four may be hidden. The shown columns are remembered by the window with the widths (`side_on`, `explorer_on`, `middle_on`, `agent_on` in the layout). A column that comes back takes the keyboard when it has a place for it (2a: the tree, column 3: the tab on screen in the pane that I use, 4a: Claude Code; column 1: nothing). Hiding the column that has the keyboard gives it to 4a, or to column 3 when 4a is hidden; the focus is released first, since WebKit keeps it in a hidden element and a hidden terminal would still get the keys. Cmd+J C and Cmd+J E show 4a and 2a again; a tab of column 3 that takes the keyboard (`showTab` with focus: Cmd+1-9, Cmd+J 1-4, a file opened from 2a) and Cmd+N show column 3 again. A new task (Cmd+T) and a fork (Cmd+Shift+D) show 4a again, and its Claude Code takes the keyboard (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-13: "if I create a new task, it should reveal the Claude Code panel and give focus to it"; the fork too, since it is a new task; done when the task arrives in the state, not at the key, so 4a never shows the old task for a moment; one call to `revealColumn('agent')` in `onState`, `app.js`). Tested with a sandbox selftest: Cmd+T and the fork bring 4a back and select the new task, the new task's terminal takes the keyboard, a select leaves 4a hidden. Not tested: the keyboard after a fork (the window was covered, frames stopped). Selecting a task does not show a hidden 4a. Cmd+Option+5 shows or hides columns 1 and 4a together, the agent columns (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-14: "cmd+opt+5: close column 1 and 4 (the agentic columns)"). Decided by Claude: a toggle, as 1 to 4; when one of the two shows, both hide, and only when both are hidden do both show; it then gives the keyboard to Claude Code as Cmd+Option+4 does, and hiding them gives it to column 3; columns 2a and 3 do not change (column 3 hidden stays hidden); in Review mode, 5 does nothing. Same code (`toggleColumn(5)`); a browser page already forwards Cmd+Option+5. Tested with a sandbox selftest: both hide, both show, one shown hides both, the forwarded key without code, nothing in Review mode. Not tested: the keyboard after the key (the window was covered, frames stopped). Only in Dev mode; Review mode takes Cmd+Option+1 and 4 as its Cmd+1 and Cmd+4; Organize mode: nothing. Code: `columnKey`, `toggleColumn`, `revealColumn` in `layout.js`, the classes `no-side`, `no-explorer`, `no-middle`, `no-agent` on #dev (`app.css`). Tested with a sandbox selftest (window visible): each key alone and together, column 3 filling the width, the tree taking the keyboard, a forwarded key without code, Cmd+N T and Cmd+1 showing column 3, Cmd+J E showing 2a with its labels, the keyboard leaving a hidden pane terminal, Review mode, Cmd+1 still picking a tab. Not tested: Cmd+J C (needs a task), a real key from a real browser page. Before: 2a and the middle column were always shown (Renaud, 2026-11-11: no key to hide the explorer; offered a drag to the edge, as VS Code, or a View menu item, and chose neither), and before that Cmd+1 and Cmd+2 showed or hid them; Cmd+1 to Cmd+9 now pick a tab (see below). Their widths are set with the splitters (2a: 140 px at least). When the window is narrow, the middle column gives way first; 4a keeps at least 360 px. The middle column has no minimum width since 2026-11-12 (Renaud could not widen 4a on his laptop; it was 300 px for the splitter, 280 px in the CSS): dragged to 0, it is gone and its splitter stays. The drag of its splitter starts from its width on screen, not from the saved width: a width saved on a large screen is larger than what a laptop shows, and the drag did nothing until the mouse had crossed the difference. Tested with a sandbox selftest (window 1470 px, saved width 1400: 4a went 360 --> 560 --> 760 --> 988 px, back to 688). Sizes are remembered by the window (`window.json`, see App, "Window settings"), except the middle column's width: at start, 4a takes a third of the window (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-13: "at startup, column 4 should take by default 1/3 of the width"). 4a takes what the middle column leaves, so `startWidths` (`layout.js`) sets the middle column to the window's width minus the shown columns 1 and 2a, the splitters and a third; columns 1 and 2a keep their saved widths, a drag still works for the session, and with the middle column or 4a hidden nothing changes. Decided by Claude: at every start, not only at the first one ("at startup"), and from `innerWidth`, which is final when `layout.js` runs. Tested with a sandbox selftest (window 1600 px): 4a 533 px at start, also after a saved middle width of 1400 and with column 1 hidden. Until 2026-11-10 the middle column was 3a (files and pages) over 3b (terminals, always shown, Cmd+J).
- The grid of column 3 (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-10, `web/grid.js`): up to four panes, 1 top left (the main pane), 2 top right, 3 bottom left, 4 bottom right. Each pane has its own tabs, its own bar and its own tab on screen; a tab is a file, a thread, a browser page or a terminal. A pane shows only while it has tabs. The grid is two lines of two panes, in one of two shapes that each view (a task, the project view) remembers with its grid (`cols` in its grid; a new task and a fork copy it with the grid): rows first (1|2 over 3|4) or columns first (1 over 3, beside 2 over 4). A pane alone in its line takes the whole line, and a line alone takes column 3; so three panes make an L: rows first, 3 alone takes the bottom; columns first, 2 alone takes the right side. The panes and splitters are positioned absolutely inside column 3 (`layoutPanes`), so a change of shape moves no element: terminals (WebGL), editors and the native pages stay where they are in the DOM. The splitters keep fractions of column 3 (in the window's layout, 15% at least): rows first `gridRows` (the top row's share of the height), `gridTop` and `gridBottom` (the left pane's share of its row); columns first `gridCols`, `gridLeft` and `gridRight`. An empty pane 1 shows nothing: its hint "Open a file from the explorer, or Cmd+N then T, B or F." was removed (Renaud, 2026-11-11: "distracting", "disturbing").
- Create (Renaud's keys): Cmd+N, then T (terminal), B (browser, the address field focused) or F (an untitled text file), then a digit 1 to 4: created in that pane, which opens if it was empty. No digit within 1 s, or another key: pane 1 (Renaud: no hint on screen, just wait). After Cmd+N the letter may come within 2 s. Esc cancels, an unknown letter cancels too; while the chord waits every key is taken (also in a text field), so that none reaches a terminal or Claude Code. Cmd held through the letter or the digit works the same. Only in Dev mode. Cmd+T is now the new task (it was a browser tab); `browser.py` forwards Cmd+N from a focused page and gives the keyboard back to Brain Fry's page, so the letter after it arrives there. The "+ Browser" and "+" (terminal) buttons are gone: creating is Cmd+N.
- Jump (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-11): Cmd+J, then a digit 1 to 4: the keyboard goes to the tab on screen in that pane, which becomes the pane that I use (as a click). No digit within 1 s, another key, or Esc: nothing (the key is taken). No default pane, unlike Cmd+N (Renaud, 2026-11-11: he removed "no digit: pane 1"). An empty pane: nothing happens, no message. J for "jump"; Renaud keeps F for find, and Cmd+J was free since 3b went away. Other letters were weighed: K is the command palette of many web pages (GitHub, Slack, Notion) and would be taken from them, G is "find next" in CodeMirror and in pages, O, I, U, Y belong to editors and browsers. Tested with a sandbox selftest (from a terminal and from an editor, an empty pane, no digit, another key, Esc).
- Jump, extended (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-11): after Cmd+J, C sends the keyboard to the selected task's Claude Code (4a; no task: nothing), and E to the explorer, with a key on each row. The key after Cmd+J may now come within 2 s (it was 1 s), as after Cmd+N. A second Cmd+J while the chord waits starts the wait again (before, it cancelled the chord, and the digit after it went to the terminal). Each outcome writes a line in `bf.log` ("jump: pane 2", "jump: no key within 2 s", "jump: cancelled by the key x"...), with no prompt text: one key at most.
- Why Cmd+J was sometimes missed (Renaud, 2026-11-11: "sometimes it doesn't catch the Command-J"): not found. Read and ruled out: the menu bar has no Cmd+J (pywebview's menus: Cmd+H, Q, X, C, V, A, Ctrl+Cmd+F); the document's capture listener runs before xterm.js, CodeMirror and text fields; from a browser page, `browser.py` forwards Cmd+J and gives the keyboard back to Brain Fry's page; the running app had the code (started at 17:24, `grid.js` last changed at 14:58). Guesses, both fixed above: the digit came after the 1 s window, or Cmd+J was pressed twice. If it happens again, the `bf.log` lines tell which: no line at all means that Cmd+J never reached the page. One race remains possible from a browser page: the forwarded Cmd+J travels through the backend and the websocket, so a digit typed within a few ms could arrive before it.
- Cmd+J E (`explorer.js`): each row of 2a on screen (its middle inside the tree's visible area) shows a key in a small blue label right after its name: 1 to 9, then a to z, from the top, so 35 rows at most and one key per row (Renaud chose it from a mockup on 2026-11-11: four placements and four looks; he rejected 10, 11...). Rows below the 35th have no label. Typing a label acts as a click on that row: a file opens (in the pane that I used last, as a click) and the keyboard stays in the tree with the row selected, so Enter renames; a folder opens or closes. The keyboard then goes to the tree. A key with no row, Esc, or another key: nothing. The labels stay until a key (no time limit: they show that the chord waits), a click anywhere, or the window losing the keyboard. They are counted again when the tree is redrawn or scrolled: they are always what is on screen. No project, or no row on screen: nothing. One shot: after a folder opens, Cmd+J E again labels its files. Cmd held through E and the label works the same (Cmd+A picks row a). Built first with numbers just left of the name and two-digit numbers (a 1 waited 1 s for a second digit); replaced the same day. Tested with a sandbox selftest (window visible, 25 rows, labels 1 to p): a folder (2), a letter (f), a letter with no row (q), Enter, Cmd+J Cmd+E Cmd+A from 4a, the label 6 px after its name; and, in the first version, Esc, X, a click, 2a hidden, Cmd+J C, Cmd+J pressed twice, a digit after 1.5 s, no key for 2.3 s. Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- Cmd+1 to Cmd+9 (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-11: "jump to the first tab in the open panel that I'm in, etc with cmd-2"; Cmd+1 and Cmd+2 showed or hid 2a and the middle column before): the n-th tab from the left in the pane that I use (`w.pane`) goes on screen and takes the keyboard, as a click on it would. Wherever the keyboard is (a pane, 4a, 2a), as Cmd+W. No such tab (beyond the last one, an empty pane, no task or project): nothing, no message. Only in Dev mode; Review mode keeps its Cmd+1 (side panel) and Cmd+4 (agent). Digits only, 1 to 9: no "Cmd+9 = the last tab" of the browsers (not asked), no number shown on the tabs (not asked). Cmd+0 stays the page's zoom reset in a browser tab. A browser page forwards Cmd+1 to Cmd+9 (`FORWARD` in `browser.py`), and its pane becomes the one that I use first (`forwardKey`), so the digit counts in the page's pane. A terminal leaves them to Brain Fry (`OUR_KEYS`). `showTabAt` in `grid.js`. Tested with a sandbox selftest (window visible, keys sent to the focused element; pane 1: three files and a terminal, pane 2: one file): Cmd+2 from the terminal, Cmd+1 from an editor, Cmd+4 to the terminal, Cmd+5 (nothing), Cmd+1 and Cmd+2 in pane 2 (one tab), Cmd+1 and Cmd+3 from 4a (the keyboard goes to the tab, in the pane used last), Organize mode (nothing), 2a and the middle column stay shown. Not tested: the key forwarded from a real browser page. Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- Copy a tab from another task (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-11, settled over five mockups): Cmd+N, then O, opens a list (`web/copytab.js`, `copytab.css`). No digit: the copy goes into the pane that I use (`w.pane`), even when its tab on screen is a terminal (Renaud: "the panel I'm currently on"; the files of 2a skip a terminal). The header says "into pane N". Only in Dev mode with a task selected, else a toast.
  - What it lists: the other tasks of the selected task's project, the one that I opened last first, archived ones included. Each task is a heading with only its color and title (Renaud removed the time and "archived"), and one line per tab under it: an icon for the kind (text file, image, PDF, page, terminal), the name on the left, the full path, URL or folder on the right (a long one loses its start). Then, under "Other projects", the pages of the tasks of other projects (their files and terminals belong to another folder). Not listed: a file or a page that is already open in the selected task (Renaud: "I don't want to be offered these, which are already open"), a task with nothing left, threads and untitled files (Renaud removed them), Finder tabs (excluded by the agent that added them). A task built since the start lists the tabs of its panes; another one lists what its task file keeps (files, URLs, one "Terminal" per terminal tab, in "./").
  - Terminals are named after their last command (Renaud: "put the last command that was run there, if possible"), "Terminal" before the first one. The bash of a terminal tab runs `shell_init.bash` (`bash --init-file`, as VS Code's shell integration does). It reads what a login shell reads (/etc/profile, then the first of ~/.bash_profile, ~/.bash_login, ~/.profile). Then a DEBUG trap sends each command line that history recorded, and the last entry of PROMPT_COMMAND sends the folder, as OSC 7771, which xterm.js ignores. `ptys.scan_info` reads them (a sequence may be cut between two reads), and the `shells` of the state carry `cmd` and `cwd`. Commands are never logged. A line that history does not record (a leading space with HISTCONTROL=ignorespace, an empty line) is not sent. Other shells (zsh, fish) start as before (`-l`) and tell nothing: their terminals show "Terminal" in their first folder. Renaud's shell is bash 3.2; his startup files have no PROMPT_COMMAND or DEBUG trap of their own (checked).
  - Keys: Up/Down (the headings are rows too), Enter copies, Esc closes and gives the keyboard back, typing filters (on the name and the right side; a task whose title matches shows all its tabs). No digits (Renaud). A click on a line copies it. The list takes every key first (a capture listener on `window`); Cmd keys do nothing there, except the editing keys of the filter.
  - A copy: a file opens as any file does, so it shows the other task's text, unsaved edits included (One text per file, below; until 2026-11-11 it was read from disk), a page loads its URL (no history), a terminal is a new shell in the folder where the other one is now. `shell.copy` takes that folder from the backend's own PTY, and refuses a shell of another project or one that has ended; a terminal tab with no shell running gives a new shell in the task's folder. Enter or a click on a heading copies all the tabs listed under it, in their order; the last one shows and takes the keyboard. Several terminals asked at once are queued (`w.nextShells`, which replaced `nextShell`).
  - Preview (Renaud: "when I hover over one of the lines, it shows me the status in the panel image"): on the right, the tab under the mouse, else the selected one (the keyboard takes over until the mouse moves again), drawn as a pane with its tab bar. A text file: a read-only editor at the line where the other tab is scrolled, with its unsaved edits (the position is noted while the tab scrolls, since a hidden tab reads 0); a file not open since the start: from the top. An image: the image. A PDF: a copy of the canvas of the page that the other tab shows. A page: a snapshot of its native view (`browser.snapshot`, which works on a hidden view: checked). A terminal: its screen as text. A heading: the task's digest, or its notes. With nothing to show (a task not opened since the start has no shell, no loaded page, no PDF canvas): the name and the path in grey. The native pages are hidden under the list (`covered()`).
  - Tested: `tests/test_shell_info.py` (the reader of the sequences; bash with a fake HOME, its own PROMPT_COMMAND and HISTCONTROL; `shell.copy` over the websockets), bash with Renaud's real profile (the sequences arrive, no error), and sandbox selftests (window visible, keys sent to the focused element): the list, the pane in use (2), each kind of preview, the scrolled file (line 118 in both), the filter, Esc, a copied file, a copied terminal (a new shell in `proj/sub`), copy all from a heading, a click on a heading of another project. Not tested: real mouse hovers and clicks, snapshots of real sites. Renaud has not tried it; the app must be restarted.
- Untitled file (Cmd+N F): "Untitled-1", plain text, kept in memory only (not in the task file: it has no path; closing it with text asks first). Its first save (Cmd+S, Save) shows macOS's save panel in the project folder (`fs.saveas`, pywebview's `create_file_dialog`; the panel asks itself before it replaces a file, so the write is forced). A path outside the project is taken since 2026-11-14 (the tab is then named by its absolute path, see Files outside the project below; until then it was refused with a toast). Saved, it becomes a file tab: the language of its extension, the explorer refreshed; a tab of the replaced file is closed.
- `bf <file>` in a terminal tab (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-14: "which command could I use so that the file opens in Brain Fry's editor"; he chose `bf` itself over `bf open`, and the terminal tabs of Brain Fry only). `shell_init.bash` defines a `bf` function: `bf` alone, its commands (`install`, `uninstall`, `ublock`, `init`: `cli.COMMANDS`, and a test checks that both lists agree) and its options (`-...`) run the real `bf`; anything else is a file. A file named like a command opens with `bf ./init`. For each argument the function writes the absolute path (`$PWD/` before a relative one) as OSC 7771 `open` to `/dev/tty`, so that a redirected output does not swallow it. `ptys.scan_info` reads it, `Core._shell_open` checks it in a thread (`files.bf_target`) and sends `{type: "open", tid, path, line, new}` or `{type: "open", tid, error}` to the front end, in the order typed (`PtySession.opening`); `onShellOpen` (files.js) opens it. The path is not logged (it is part of a command).
  - Decided by Claude (proposed on 2026-11-14, accepted by Renaud with File > Open below): the file opens where a Cmd+click on a file link opens it (`openPane`: the pane that I used last among those that do not show a terminal, else pane 1), so it does not cover the terminal; the editor takes the keyboard, as after VS Code's `code file`. `bf a b` opens each, in order, the last on screen. `bf app.py:42` (or `:42:7`) opens at line 42 when no file has that exact name (a file named `odd:7` opens as itself). A file that does not exist yet, in a folder that does, opens empty and its first save writes it (VS Code's `code new.txt`): the tab has `version` null, which the poll leaves alone (null on disk too), `saveOpenFiles` leaves it out of the task file until that save, and the save is refused if a file appeared there meanwhile. A toast says why for a folder or a missing folder. A file outside the project opens by its absolute path (Files outside the project, below; on the first day it was refused with a toast). A terminal that is no longer on screen when the message arrives opens nothing (toast). Outside Brain Fry, `bf notes.txt` prints that it works only in Brain Fry's terminal tabs (exit 2). Other shells (zsh) have no `bf` function, as they have no OSC 7771.
  - Tested: `tests/test_files.py` (`bf_target`: line suffixes, a name with `:7`, new files, outside, a link out of the project, a folder, a missing folder), `tests/test_shell_info.py` (the reader; bash with a fake HOME: absolute paths, a redirected `bf`, `bf ./init`, the commands passed through; the command lists; the CLI message; the backend over the websockets, the order of five files), and a sandbox selftest on 2026-11-14 (window visible): `bf a.py:30` from a terminal in pane 3 opened in pane 1 at line 30 with the keyboard in the editor; `bf new.md` opened empty, typing made it dirty, Cmd+S wrote it and the task file then listed it; the three toasts in the order typed.
- File > Open... (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-14: "File > Open, and it should open the file in the current panel"): the first item of the File menu. The menu's thread calls `menuAction('open')`; the page asks the backend for macOS's open panel (`fs.openpanel`, pywebview's `create_file_dialog(OPEN)`, several files allowed), which starts in the project on screen, since the menu's thread does not know it. The files open in the pane that I use (`w.pane`), also when it shows a terminal, unlike a click in 2a. Decided by Claude: no key (Renaud asked for the menu only; a key of the menu bar would never reach the page anyway); a file that is open already shows where it is (one tab per file in a view); files outside the project open by their absolute path (on the first day they were named in a toast and not opened); from Organize or Review mode, Dev mode comes first; if another project came on screen while the panel was open (an inbox arrival), a toast and nothing opens. Tested: `tests/test_app_backend.py` (the panel's arguments, inside and outside, cancel, no window) and the same selftest with the panel stubbed (notes.txt in pane 3, the terminal's pane; b.py, open already, stayed in pane 1; the toast for a file outside; from Organize mode to Dev mode).
- Files outside the project (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-14: "I wanted, really, to be able to open a file outside the project. Why is it a blocker?"; he picked "outside files as normal editable tabs" from three options, the others being read-only tabs and the default app). Until then the file tabs of column 3 held files of the project only, a choice made when 3a was built (2a's root is the task's project), not a need: the shells already read any file, the Host and Origin check protects the server, and the Finder tabs already use absolute paths. Now a file tab names a file of its project by a path relative to the project, and any other file by its absolute path (`files.tab_path`, which follows symlinks; a relative path never starts with "/"). Every way in gives that form: `bf`, File > Open, file links of 4a and 3b (`link_target`), Save As of an untitled tab, a double-click in a Finder tab. The tab commands take both (`files.locate`: `fs.read`, `fs.write`, `fs.versions`, `fs.open`, `/file/` for images and PDFs, the selection as context, the play button); the explorer and its right-click commands keep `files.resolve`, inside the project only. The task file and `project.json` keep the absolute paths in `open_files` as they are; the loader's fix for a moved project leaves them alone, as it leaves `finder_tabs`.
  - Decided by Claude: the tab shows the file's name, its tooltip the absolute path (as VS Code); nothing marks it in 2a. One text per file holds across projects for such a file. The play button runs it from the project's root, with the project's `.venv` when it has one. The Diff button works when the file is in a git repository. Links in the Markdown preview (mdpreview.js) still stop at the project (a toast): not asked.
  - Tested: `tests/test_files.py` (`tab_path`, a symlink out of the project, read, write and versions by absolute path, a relative `..` still refused, the explorer's listing refused), `tests/test_app_backend.py` (the commands over the websocket, `files.set`, the open panel, Save As, the selection, the play button), `tests/test_shell_info.py` (`bf` of an outside file, in order with others), and a sandbox selftest on 2026-11-14: `bf ../outside/far.txt` opened a tab named far.txt (tooltip: the absolute path); an edit and Cmd+S wrote it, and a poll after it changed nothing; an outside PNG loaded in the viewer; with unsaved edits in it, the task of a second project opened the same file and showed the same text (one shared state); File > Open in that task of a file of the first project opened it by its absolute path in the pane in use; both task files listed the absolute paths.
- Drag a tab (mouse events, not HTML drag and drop: while the button is down AppKit sends every move to Brain Fry's page, also over a browser page, which would take a drop for itself; the pages show snapshots meanwhile, `covered()`): onto another pane's tab bar --> into that pane, before the tab under the mouse (also reorders a bar); onto a pane, within a third of an edge whose neighbour cell is empty --> into that neighbour, which opens, and only the pane under the mouse splits (Renaud, 2026-11-13: with two columns, a tab dropped at the bottom of the left one made a row across the whole width; he wanted the right column to keep its height, as in VS Code). The drop sets the shape: a left or right edge --> rows first, a top or bottom edge --> columns first. So [1 | 2] and a drop at the bottom of 1 --> 1 over 3, beside 2 at full height; [1 over 3 | 2] and a drop at the right of 3 --> rows first, the four panes in a 2x2. Cmd+N then a digit keeps the shape of the view. Checked on 2026-11-13 with a sandbox selftest of real mouse drags (the L of each shape, the 2x2 reached from each, the splitters of the columns-first shape). Otherwise: elsewhere on a pane --> into that pane. A blue mark shows where it goes: the half of the pane for a new one, the pane otherwise. A pane emptied by the move closes.
- Close all tabs of a pane (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-11: "a little cross at the top right of each panel ... asks for confirmation through an alert panel"; VS Code's "Close All" of an editor group): a small × button (SVG, the look of `#tree-collapse`) at the right end of the pane's tab bar, inside `.pane-tabs`, `position: sticky; right: 0; margin-left: auto`, so it stays visible when the tabs overflow and scroll. Only while the pane has tabs. A click asks once with JS `confirm()`, which pywebview shows as a native NSAlert (above the browser's native views): "Close the N tabs of this pane?" (one tab: "Close the tab of this pane?"), plus what is lost: the names of the files with unsaved changes that no tab of another task holds (since 2026-11-11, One text per file, below), and the number of terminals, whose shells end. No second question per dirty tab (`closeFile(tid, path, ask = false)`). Every kind of tab closes as its own × would. It always asks, also for one tab (as asked). No key (not asked). `closePane` in `grid.js`, the button in `renderFileTabs` (`files.js`). Tested with a sandbox selftest (`window.confirm` replaced by a stub: the real NSAlert would block the run): Cancel keeps the tabs, OK closes a file with edits and a terminal (shell gone, pane 2 hidden, pane 1 becomes the one that I use), the one-tab text, 17 overflowing tabs (the button stays at the right end and receives the click, `elementFromPoint`), and the WebKit snapshot. The window was covered during that run (`visibilityState` hidden), which does not affect these checks. Not tested: a real click and the real NSAlert. Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- The pane that I use (`w.pane`): the last one clicked or focused, or the pane of a browser page whose forwarded key arrived (a click in a native page never reaches the page, so the forwarded key carries its tab). Cmd+L, Cmd+W, Cmd+F act there. Cmd+W closes the tab on screen there, of any kind, as its × does, wherever the keyboard is (Renaud, 2026-11-11), and it never closes the window: pywebview's WKWebView subclass (`WebKitHost.keyDown_`) closes the window on a Cmd+W that the page leaves unhandled (WebKit resends a key that the page did not `preventDefault`), so the page always takes the key, also with nothing to close or outside Dev mode. The keyboard follows to the tab that takes the place when it was in the closed tab or nowhere, not when it was in 4a or 2a. Its tab on screen keeps the accent mark when several panes show; the others' turn grey. Files from 2a, threads from 1b, file links and URLs from a terminal open in the pane that I used last unless its tab on screen is a terminal (then the one before it, else pane 1): a click in a terminal must not make the next file cover it. A file already open shows where it is. A page that a page opens (window.open, Cmd+click) goes to its opener's pane (`opener` in `browser.py`'s `opened` message).
- Selection as context follows the text tab on screen in the pane that I used last among those that show one (VS Code's active editor), so it stays while I type in a terminal. 2a marks the file on screen in the pane that I used last.
- Remembered per task: each tab's pane, the order of the tabs, the tab on screen per pane, the pane that I used, the shape. A browser tab is named by its position among the saved ones (`web#0`), a terminal by its position among the task's (`shell#0`), since their keys are new at each start; threads and untitled files are not kept. A new task and a fork copy the grid of their source, as they copy its tabs. Until 2026-11-14 the grid was in localStorage (`grid:<task id>`), which is lost at each start of the app (see App, "Window settings"), so after a restart every tab came back in pane 1. Tested on 2026-11-10 with a sandbox selftest (keys sent to the focused element, window visible): Cmd+N F, Cmd+N T 3, Cmd+N B 4, Cmd+N T without digit, Esc, an unknown letter, a drag to the right edge of a single pane (two columns), a drag onto a tab bar, closing the last tab of a pane, a new task with the same grid, Cmd+T, the sizes of lone panes and rows. Not tested: real mouse drags, a real save panel, a forwarded key from a real page. Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- Where the grid is kept (2026-11-14, decided with Renaud: the grid belongs to the task file, not to WebKit's store). A task's grid is the frontmatter field `grid` (`actions.set_grid`); the grid of a project's tabs is the key `grid` of `<project>/.brain_fry/project.json` (`actions.set_project_grid`). Both are `{pane, act, at, cols, order}` (`gridRecord`, grid.js); `actions._grid` drops anything else. The state brings it (`grid` in `task_json` and `project_tabs_json`); the front end reads it at the restore only (`loadGrid` --> `ownerData(tid).grid`). `saveGrid` sends `grid.set`, which writes only on a change, logs nothing at INFO (`QUIET`) and pushes no state; the front end also skips a send when the record is the one it sent last (`sentGrid`), since a click on a tab calls `saveGrid`. A new task and a fork: the backend copies the source's `grid` with its tab lists (`actions.new_task`, `fork_task`); the commands of one connection run in order, so the copy holds every `grid.set` sent before `new`. `copyGrid` (front end) then uses the source's grid on screen when the source was built since the start, as before; otherwise the copied record. No migration: the old localStorage data never survived a start.
- The order of the tabs across kinds (2026-11-14). The record has `order`: the ids of the owner's tabs in their order. A restore adds the tabs kind by kind (files, pages, Finder tabs), and the terminals come with a state push, before or after. `applyOrder` (grid.js, called by `applyWant`, so at the end of `restoreFiles` and at each `renderFiles`) sorts the owner's tabs by that order each time a remembered tab comes in. It stops when all have come, or at the first `saveGrid` (a change on screen: from then on, the order on screen is the record). Tabs that the record does not name keep their place, at the end. A `saveGrid` before the terminals arrive (a click in the first second) loses their place, as it loses their pane (`at`): known, not fixed.
- Tested on 2026-11-14: `tests/test_window_store.py` (the field, `grid.set` for a task and for a project, no push, the copy to a new task and a fork, junk dropped) and a sandbox selftest in two runs on one sandbox, window visible in both: run 1 moved `b.md` to pane 2 (a drag to the right edge of pane 1), made a Finder tab and a terminal in pane 1, dragged the terminal before `a.py` and the Finder tab before `c.txt`; run 2, a new start, showed pane 1 as terminal, `a.py`, Finder, `c.txt` (before: `a.py`, `c.txt`, Finder, terminal), pane 2 as `b.md`, the Finder tab on screen in pane 1. Not tested in the window: a new task or a fork after a restart, project tabs.
- 2a, explorer: the tree of the selected task's project (with no task selected, the project picked in the dropdown: see Project dropdown), loaded folder by folder. It hides `.git`, `.venv`, `node_modules`, `__pycache__`, `.brain_fry` and a few other caches. The open folders are reloaded when an event arrives for the task (the agent may have added files), when I land on another layout (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-13: "automatically refresh the file explorer when i land on a new layout"; each view keeps its tree, which was as it was when it was last on screen), and with Refresh (the right-click menu, or File > Refresh Explorer in the menu bar). "Another layout": the view of 2a changes (`wsId`: another task, the project view); the idle screen keeps the layout that I left, so it does not reload, nor does Organize mode back to Dev mode on the same task (`S.treeWs` in `renderExplorer`, updated only in Dev mode, so a task picked in Organize mode reloads when Dev mode shows it). During a reload, a folder that fails to load was deleted: it is forgotten without a toast (before, the event reload toasted "No such file or directory" for an open folder that the agent had deleted). Tested on 2026-11-13 with a sandbox selftest: while task B was on screen, files added to the project were missing from A's tree; landing on A showed them, and an open folder deleted meanwhile disappeared with no toast.
  - After a command in a terminal tab (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-17: "if there's a slight chance that I modified a file or folder, refresh the explorer. It's fine if there are false positives"; he first named `rm`, `mv`, `touch`, then picked the negative list that Claude proposed, so that scripts that write files, such as `python make_plots.py` or `make`, count too). The bash of the tab sends each command line before it runs (`cmd`, see Cmd+N O above). `ptys.changes_files` decides whether the line may have created, deleted, renamed or moved a file: yes, unless each of its commands is read-only (`READ_ONLY`: `ls`, `cd`, `cat`, `less`, `head`, `tail`, `grep`, `rg`, `pwd`, `clear`, `man`, `which`, `echo` and a few more; `git` status, log, diff, show, blame, branch, fetch, remote) and the line writes no file with `>` (`2>&1`, `>&2` and `> /dev/null` write none). The commands of a line are split at `;`, `&`, `|`, `(`, `)`, `` ` ``, `{`, `}`, and found after `sudo`, `xargs`, `env`, `VAR=x` and the like. A quote that holds `;` or `|`, an alias or `git -C dir status` gives a false positive. At the next prompt (the command has ended), the shell's `fsgen` grows (in the state, with `cmd` and `cwd`). The front end reloads the tree on screen when any shell's `fsgen` changed (`shellsFsSig` in `files.js`), whatever the terminal's task or folder, so `cd ../other && rm x` in a tab of another project reloads it too. Other views reload when I land on them, as before.
  - A line that history does not record sent nothing before: the same line again, which Renaud's `HISTIGNORE="&"` drops (Up, Enter: the most common way to run a script again), or a line with a leading space under `ignorespace`. Bash now sends `ran` with its first simple command (`$BASH_COMMAND`); the app counts the line as changing files when that command or the last `cmd` may change them. To tell such a line from an empty one, `__bf_prompt` (a no-op that keeps `$?`) runs first in PROMPT_COMMAND: when the DEBUG trap fires for it, no command ran. Not seen: commands typed inside a program that runs in the tab (a nested shell, ssh, a Python REPL). The command text is not logged.
  - Cost of a reload (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-17, "how does refreshing the explorer work if a folder has several thousand files?"): only the open folders are listed, each in full, with no cap and no timeout (`files.list_dir`, measured on the local disk: 2,000 entries in 6 ms and 150 KB of JSON, 5,000 in 17 ms and 376 KB, 20,000 in 66 ms and 1.5 MB). The window's commands run one after the other, so a slow listing delays the next command by its time. Since 2026-11-17 (asked by Renaud with the negative list), `loadDir` draws the tree only when a folder's list changed (`sameKids`): before, each reload drew the whole tree once per open folder, and the tree has no virtual list. The tree still redraws at each state push, as before.
  - Tested: `tests/test_shell_info.py` (`changes_files` on 28 lines, the session's `fsgen` at the prompt, a real bash with `HISTIGNORE='&'` and a PROMPT_COMMAND that shows `$?`, and `touch` in a terminal tab over the websocket: `fsgen` 0 after `cd`, then 1). A sandbox selftest on 2026-11-17 (window visible, Renaud's real `~/.bash_profile` in the terminal): `ls` listed nothing; `touch new.txt` listed the root once and showed the file; the same line again listed once and did not redraw (one redraw, from the state push); `rm new.txt` removed the row; an empty line did nothing.
- 2a has no title bar (Renaud, 2026-11-10): the project is in the header's dropdown. Folder names end with `/`, so that folders stand out. A button at the top right, over the first row and not on a line of its own, collapses every open folder (VS Code's "Collapse Folders in Explorer").
- Explorer search (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-14: "i need a way to search for files. best is in the explorer, at the top. cmd-f i guess. if i type smthg, it should find occurences in the explorer tree and only show these files"; then "I want Command-F to open from anywhere" and "clicking enter searches the files. but does not close the search field"; settled over two mockups, his answers to the second: "1 A 2 dots 3 none 4a"). Code: `web/treesearch.js`, `web/treesearch.css`, `treesearch.py`, the command `fs.search` (`app/core.py`), small hooks in `files.js` (`renderExplorer`, `refreshTree`, `clickTreeNode`, the Cmd+F listener), `grid.js` (`chordKey`) and `explorer.js` (the right-click menu).
  - Keys. Cmd+F opens it from anywhere in Dev mode: 4a, a terminal tab (xterm.js does not keep the key), the explorer, the inbox, a tab bar, nothing focused. Not while the keyboard is in a tab that has a find of its own: a CodeMirror editor (text and thread tabs: CodeMirror's find panel), a PDF tab (its find field), a browser page (its find: the key that the native page forwards, or the address or find field of its bar). Cmd+J then F opens it from anywhere, also from a file, and writes "jump: explorer search" in `bf.log` (never the query). Both show 2a again when Cmd+Option+2 hid it, and do nothing without a project, in Organize or Review mode, or with a picker open. Cmd+F while it is open: the field takes the keyboard, its text selected. The Cmd+F listener of `files.js` stops the key (`stopImmediatePropagation`), so that browser.js does not also open the find of a page that shows in the pane that I use.
  - The field. Hidden until one of those keys; then one row at the top of 2a: a field with no placeholder and a small button with three dots. The field holds the last query that ran in this view, selected. Enter searches (typing does not); the field stays, with the keyboard. Enter with an empty field: the plain tree, the field stays. Esc (in the field, the extended area or the tree) closes it: the tree comes back as it was (the search never touches its open folders), and the keyboard goes back to where it was when the search was opened (gone or hidden since: the tree; nowhere: nowhere). The dots button opens or closes the extended area: Aa (match case), ab (whole word), .* (regular expression), names and text (what is searched; both on at first), then the fields "include" and "exclude". A toggle runs the search again while results show; Enter in include or exclude searches too. The button: dim when the area is closed, grey pressed when open, accent blue when closed while an option differs from its default.
  - include / exclude: comma-separated patterns, tested on the path in the project, case ignored: `*.py` a name, `tests/` a folder anywhere, `src/brain_fry` a piece of the path, `**` any depth (`src/**/x.py` also matches `src/x.py`), `?` one character, a leading `/` the project folder. The walk skips an excluded folder entirely.
  - What is searched: the whole project, which the backend walks (the tree loads folder by folder). The names of files (not of folders) and the text of files. The folders of `files.HIDDEN` stay hidden; a symlinked folder is not followed. The text is skipped (the name still matches) for a binary file (a NUL in the first 8192 bytes), a file over 2 MB, an image or a PDF (`files.VIEW_TYPES`), and a file that is not UTF-8 (the editor refuses it too); a link to a file outside the project matches by its name only. Nothing else is excluded (.gitignore is not read, vendored bundles match). Matching: a plain substring with case ignored, or Python's `re` with the toggles (whole word: `\b(?:...)\b`); an empty match does not count. An invalid regular expression: a toast with the message of `re`, and the results stay. The count of a file is the number of its lines that hold a match. The text of each file is searched as a whole (one `search` per matching line, never line by line).
  - Results (layout A): only the files that match, under their folders, all open, in the order of the tree (the backend walks in that order: folders first, then by lowercase name, as `files.list_dir`), with the look and indent of the tree. A file whose text matches shows the count at its right, dim. A match in a name has the faint grey pill of inline code. No result: an empty tree, no text. The file on screen keeps its `.active` mark. "Collapse folders" hides while results show; while the field is open over the plain tree, it sits at the top right of the rows, below the search area (`#tree-wrap` in `index.html` holds the tree and the button).
  - In the results: a click on a file opens it as from the tree (the keyboard stays in 2a), and when its text matches, the first match is selected and scrolled to the middle (`line`, `col`, `len` of the reply; col and len in UTF-16 units, as JS counts). The results stay. Down in the field: the keyboard to the first file; Up / Down over the files; Up on the first: back to the field; Enter opens the selected file (in the tree, Enter renames; F2 still renames here). A click on a folder closes or opens it in the results only.
  - Decided by Claude: the reply is `{files: [{path, name, count, line, col, len}], more}`, where `name` lists the matches in the name (UTF-16 spans) rather than a flag, so that Python's matcher also places the pill (JS reads some regular expressions differently). The query travels as `text`, which the command's log line hides; `fs.search` is in `QUIET`, and `treesearch.command` logs only "search in <project>: N files (M by their text), K searched, T ms", and turns every failure into an error that `server.py` logs without the raw message, which holds the query (an unknown task or project: PermissionError). `fs.search` runs beside the other commands of the connection (`server.CONCURRENT`); a newer search of the same view stops an older one at its next file, and the front end drops a reply that comes after a newer search started. At most 2,000 files; beyond, one toast. Each view keeps its own search (`work(tid).treeSearch`: open or closed, the typed text, the query that ran, the results, the folders closed in them); a new task or a fork starts without. A search that shows runs again quietly (the query that ran, no toast, closed folders stay closed) 300 ms after `refreshTree` (an event of the task, Refresh, a rename) and when I land on its view. Esc drops the results: the next Cmd+F shows the last query, selected, and Enter runs it again. The keyboard goes back to the element that had it at the key that opened the search, or at a later Cmd+F from outside 2a. A toggle runs the field's text as it is now. A click that would turn off both names and text is refused with a toast. The options (the five toggles, include, exclude, the extended area open or closed) are kept per project in `window.json` under `treeSearch:<project path>`, and forgotten when all are at their defaults; include and exclude are kept at Enter, at a change and before each search. A text field keeps Cmd+F (a rename field, a field of a Finder tab), except the search's own fields; an image tab gives it to the search (it has no find). The right-click menu of the results hides New File..., New Folder... and Paste, whose new entry would show in the tree and not in the results (on the empty space only Open in Finder and Refresh are left); Rename, Delete, Cut, Copy, the paths, Open in default app, Open With, Open in Finder and Open a terminal here work on the rows, and the results run again after them. A forwarded Cmd+F is told apart by three marks: not trusted, sent to `document.body`, a page on screen in the pane that I use (`forwardKey`, browser.js). The results are drawn again only when their HTML changes (`renderExplorer` runs at each state push). The buttons of the area do not take the keyboard.
  - Timing (`treesearch.search` on Renaud's largest project, `SQL_course_26-27`, read-only: 5,720 files, 26 M characters of text): 150 to 330 ms per search with the files in the disk cache, 560 ms for the first pass (walk, read, decode); a names-only search 40 ms.
  - Known limits. The first match of an opened result is a real selection of the editor, so Selection as context sends it with the next prompt, and the chip of 4a shows "1 line selected" (CodeMirror's own find does the same). A cursor on the match would avoid that, but the match would no longer be marked. A regular expression that backtracks without end (`(a+)+$` on a long line) keeps its search thread busy until the app quits: Python's `re` has no time limit, and a newer search stops an older one only between two files.
  - Tested: `tests/test_treesearch.py` (names, text, match case, whole word, regexp, an invalid regexp, empty matches, include, exclude and the pruned folder, hidden folders, binary, over 2 MB, not UTF-8, images and PDFs, symlinks to folders and files, the cap, the first match with é and 😀 before it, CRLF, a newer search stopping an older one, `fs.search` over the websocket for a task and a project's tabs, an unknown project or task refused, the query absent from Brain Fry's log at DEBUG, a slow search not blocking `fs.list`), and two sandbox selftests (window visible, keys sent to the focused element): Cmd+F from 4a's terminal, a terminal tab, the explorer, nothing focused (2a hidden first: it came back); Cmd+F in an editor (CodeMirror's panel) and in a PDF (its find field, also from that field); Cmd+F from 4a with a PDF or a page on screen (the search); a forwarded Cmd+F with a page on screen (the page's find); Cmd+J F from an editor; a query and Enter (rows, counts, the pill's computed colors); Down, Up, Up back to the field, Enter (the file opened, "finder" selected on line 2 after "é 😀"); a click on a file and on a folder; the three states of the dots button; Aa; names off, then text off refused; exclude; the right-click menus; Cmd+J E labels on the results; a new file shown after a refresh; an invalid regexp; Esc (the tree with its open folder, the keyboard back in the terminal, the editor, the terminal tab, nowhere); the collapse button below the area; two tasks with their own search and typed text; F2 then Esc in the rename (the search stays); Organize mode (nothing). Scripts: `check.js`, `check2.js`, `build_sb.py`, `run.sh` in that session's scratchpad (`treesearch_agent/`). Not tested: real key presses and clicks, the key forwarded by a real page (simulated as `forwardKey` sends it), the toast of the cap in the window, picking a row of the results by its Cmd+J E label.
  - Rejected: layout B (files with their lines under them) and C (a flat list with lines), the sliders and chevron buttons, searching while typing, Cmd+F taking the key inside a file (answer 4b of the mockup: "find in the file" would have needed another key). From the first discussion: a search of names only (Renaud: "I want you to search inside a file"), fuzzy matching, a field that always shows (an extra row at the top of 2a), Enter that opens the selected match and closes the search (Claude's first reading of "Enter is fine"; he meant that Enter runs the search and the field stays), and ripgrep (`/opt/homebrew/bin/rg` is installed, but Python's `re` is fast enough and adds no dependency).
- The menu bar has a File menu (Open..., see 2a, 3a, 3b; Add New Project..., see Project dropdown; Refresh Explorer), right after the app menu. pywebview appends custom menus after Edit and View, and builds the menu bar again when the window becomes key, so `main.py` wraps pywebview's builder to move File (internal to pywebview 6.2; if it changes, File stays at the end). No key equivalents: a key of the menu bar never reaches the page. A menu action runs in a thread and calls `menuAction(name, arg)` in the page. A Task menu comes after View (see Modes, "Task menu").
- Clicks in 2a work as in VS Code (Renaud uses VS Code all the time): a click opens the file and leaves the keyboard in the tree, so that Enter renames; a double-click (the second click, see Modes) moves the keyboard to the file. Before 2026-11-10 a click moved the keyboard to the file.
- Right-click menu of 2a (`web/explorer.js`), asked by Renaud on 2026-11-10 from his VS Code habits: New File... and New Folder... first (2026-11-12, see below), then Open in default app (a file only), Open With (a submenu, see below), Open in Finder (see below), Open in BF Finder (2026-11-16, see below), Open a terminal here (2026-11-11, see below), Cut, Copy, Paste, Zip (2026-11-16, see below), Copy path, Copy relative path, Rename (Enter), Delete (Cmd+Backspace), Refresh. On the empty space under the tree: New File..., New Folder..., Open in Finder (the project folder), Open in BF Finder (the project folder), Paste (into the project folder) and Refresh. Keys, while the tree has the keyboard: Enter or F2 renames the selected entry, Cmd+Backspace deletes it, Ctrl+C copies its relative path (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-10; not VS Code's key, which is Cmd+Option+Shift+C; Cmd+C stays free; the menu shows ⌃C next to Copy relative path; tested with a sandbox selftest, not with a real key yet).
- Open in Finder (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-14: "add a new menu when I right-click, which says Open in Finder"; the message named no menu, so 2a was chosen, and the Finder tabs, whose menu copies 2a's): a folder opens in Finder (as before); a file shows selected in a Finder window on its folder (`open -R`, VS Code's Reveal in Finder), and Open in default app stays above it; the empty space opens the project folder (a Finder tab: the column's folder). `fs.open` and `finder.open` take `reveal` (`files.reveal_in_finder`). Tested: `test_images_and_pdfs_are_served_for_the_viewers` (fs.open with reveal, the project folder, a path outside refused), `test_backend_commands_and_icon_route` (finder.open), and a sandbox selftest of the six menus (file, folder, empty space, in 2a and in a Finder tab) with `send` stubbed: the labels and the commands sent. Not run for real: `open -R` (it opens a Finder window).
- Open in BF Finder (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-16: "add right-click menu to explorer: open in BF Finder"): a Finder tab of column 3 (see Finder tab), placed as Open in Finder places a Finder window: a folder becomes its first column with nothing selected; a file shows selected in its folder (the folder as first column, the file's preview after it); the empty space opens the project folder. Decided by Claude: the label is Renaud's words; the item comes right after Open in Finder, in both menus of 2a and in the results of the explorer search; the tab goes where a file from 2a goes (`openPane`: the pane that I used last, unless it shows a terminal or a Finder tab; with nothing else on screen, pane 1, as Cmd+N E with no digit) and takes the keyboard, as Cmd+N E; with no task selected it is a project tab (`ownerFor`, as Open a terminal here); not added to the menu of a Finder tab (not asked). Code: `finderHere` in `explorer.js`, `newFinder` takes the selected names (`chain`). Tested with a sandbox selftest (window visible): a folder, a file in a folder (row current, preview), a file of the project folder, the empty space; each tab on screen with the keyboard, the four saved in `finder_tabs`.
- Open With (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-14: "one more menu: open with: and then it opens with the available apps"; he picked a submenu as in Finder over a native macOS menu and a picker, then look 2 of a mockup: text only, "default" in gray on the right). On files and folders, in 2a (its search results too) and in the Finder tabs; not on the empty space. The submenu lists what macOS offers (`finder.apps_for`: `NSWorkspace.URLsForApplicationsToOpenURL`, read at each opening, under 5 ms): the default app first, a line, the others in Finder's order of names, a line, Other... (macOS's open panel in /Applications, apps only, `apps.pick`; the app is not remembered). One row per app: an app installed twice (two versions) shows once, under the path that macOS ranks first. The commands: `fs.apps` / `finder.apps` (quiet), then `fs.open` / `finder.open` with `app` (`files.open_with`: `open -a`, an `.app` bundle only). The submenu (`openSub` in `explorer.js`, for any item with `sub`) sits inside `#ctx`, so a click outside, Esc and the browser's cover take both menus as one. It opens on hover after 150 ms or at once on a click, beside its item with its first row level with it, on the left when the right has no room, moved up to fit the window (a scroll bar beyond its height). The mouse may cross other items on its way: the submenu closes only after 300 ms on another item (a delay, not Finder's triangle). Tested: `test_open_with_lists_the_apps_of_macos_default_first` (the real list for a text file and a folder), `test_open_with_takes_only_an_app_bundle`, the commands in `test_images_and_pdfs_are_served_for_the_viewers` and `test_backend_commands_and_icon_route` (`apps.pick` refused without a window), and a sandbox selftest (window covered, so synthetic events): the hover delay, crossing Open in Finder to the submenu, 300 ms on Cut closing it, a click, an app sent with `fs.open`, Other... (panel stubbed), a folder (Finder default), Esc, the right edge (on the left) and the bottom (moved up), a Finder tab (`finder.open` with the app), the look in a screenshot. Not run for real: `open -a` and the open panel.
- Open a terminal here (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-11: "It would open a terminal in panel 3 by default"; VS Code's "Open in Integrated Terminal"): on a folder, a new terminal tab whose shell starts in that folder; on a file, in the file's folder. Always in pane 3 (bottom left), which opens if it was empty, and the keyboard goes to the new terminal, as Cmd+N T 3. Not on the empty space under the tree (not asked; Cmd+N T starts in the task's `cwd`). No task selected (idle screen, project view): a project tab, as Cmd+N T (see Project tabs). The backend: `shell.new` takes `path` (relative to the project, `Core._shell_folder`): the folder as the tree shows it (`files.entry`: a symlink is not followed to its target, its folder must be inside the project). Open: in a symlinked folder, `pwd` probably shows the target, because the shell gets no `PWD` for the link path (not checked, not fixed: rare; one line in `_start_pty` would set it). The folder is not remembered: after a restart, and in a new task or a fork, the shell starts in the task's `cwd`, as the other terminals. Tested: `test_terminal_here` (a folder, a file, the project, `../` and a missing path refused), and a sandbox selftest (window visible): the menu on a file, a folder and the empty space; three terminals in pane 3, each in the right folder, with the keyboard. Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- New File... / New Folder... (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-12: "right click on folder or on an empty region will allow to create new file or new folder. if on an empty region, then creates the folder/file at root of project"): the first two items of the menu: on a folder they create in it, on a file in the file's folder (added the same day on his "add the 2 offers", as VS Code), on the empty space under the tree in the project folder. As in VS Code: the folder opens and an empty name field appears in a new row where the entry will show (a folder first in the folder, a file after its folders), with the Rename field's look. Enter creates it: a file opens in the pane used last and gets the keyboard, a folder is selected and the keyboard stays in the tree. A click elsewhere creates it and changes nothing else (no tab, no selection: the click may have opened another file). Esc or an empty name: nothing. A name with `/` also makes the folders on the way and opens them ("a/b.py", as VS Code). A taken name (also with another case on macOS), `.`, `..`, or a file on the way: a toast with the error, and the field closes, as for Rename. Never replaces anything; a link on the way that leads out of the project is refused. The backend: `fs.new` (`files.create`), logged. No key (not asked). Tested: `test_new_file_and_folder_never_replace_and_make_the_folders_on_the_way`, `test_commands_of_the_explorer_menu`, and two sandbox selftests (window visible): the menus on a folder, a file and the empty space; on a file, the field in the file's folder; the field's place and indent in a closed folder, at the top, before the first file of the project folder, in a folder with folders only; Enter on a file (tab in pane 1, keyboard in the editor) and on `zz/inner` (both folders open, `zz/inner` selected); Esc; a click elsewhere; a taken name (toast). Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- Rename: a field in the row, the name without its extension selected. Enter or a click elsewhere keeps the new name, Esc cancels. 2a is not redrawn while the field is open. A name with `/` is refused (that would be a move), and so is a name that is taken; a change of case only is allowed (one entry on macOS).
- Delete moves the entry to the Trash of macOS (`NSFileManager.trashItemAtURL`), after a confirmation, so Finder can put it back. Tests replace `files._move_to_trash`: a real call leaves the file in my Trash, and auto mode refuses to delete from `~/.Trash` afterwards.
- Cut / Copy keep the entry in Brain Fry (not on the clipboard of macOS: Finder cannot paste it), and a cut entry is dimmed. Paste goes into the folder that was right-clicked, or into the folder of the file. A copy never replaces anything: it takes the name that Finder gives ("a copy.py", "a copy 2.py"). A move onto a taken name is refused; a cut is pasted once. Paste works across projects (the source is the task where I chose Cut or Copy).
- Drag a row into a folder (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-14: "drag and drop files and folders in explorer"; a drop from Finder already copied into 2a, so the request was read as moves inside the tree, as in VS Code). Built in `explorer.js` (`TD`, `treeDragShow`, `moveEntry`), tested in a real window on a sandbox (33 checks, 2026-11-14). Decided by Claude:
  - A press on a row, then 6 px of mouse travel, starts the drag (mouse events, as a tab's drag in `grid.js`, not HTML drag and drop). Not with a modifier held (Ctrl+click is the right-click menu), not while a name field is open in the tree.
- Zip (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-16: "in explorer, right click on file or folder: "Zip" to create zip file"): on a file or a folder (not on the empty space under the tree), it writes `<name>.zip` next to the entry, then `<name> 2.zip`, `<name> 3.zip` when the name is taken, as Finder's Compress names it ("a.py.zip" for a file). Never replaces anything. A folder's entries start with its name, so unzipping gives the folder back. The zip is then selected in the tree, as after Paste. Decided by Claude:
  - Python's `zipfile` (deflate), not `ditto` (Finder's Compress): no `__MACOSX` folder and no `.DS_Store` in the zip, which matter when the zip goes to a Windows user. The resource forks and extended attributes of macOS are lost (none in a code project).
  - The zip holds what the explorer shows: the names of `files.HIDDEN` are left out at every depth (`.git`, `.venv`, `node_modules`, `__pycache__`, the caches, `.DS_Store`, `.brain_fry`, `.idea`, `.tox`), unlike Finder, which zips everything. Dotfiles that the tree shows (`.env`) go in. A link is stored as a link (unzip, `ditto` and Archive Utility make it again), never its target, as Paste copies a folder; sockets and pipes are left out. Dates before 1980 (the oldest that a zip holds) are stored as 1980.
  - The name is taken at once (exclusive create), so a second Zip of the same entry while the first runs takes the next name; a zip that fails (unreadable file, full disk) is removed and its error shows in a toast.
  - `fs.zip` is in `server.CONCURRENT`: a big folder does not hold the other commands of the window (the tree, the tabs). A zip that takes over 1 s shows a toast "Zipping docs...", then "Zipped: docs.zip". The backend logs `zipped docs to docs.zip in proj (1234 bytes)`.
  - In the results of the explorer search too (`treeSearchMenu` drops only the items that make a row in the tree there). Not in the menu of a Finder tab, and no key (not asked).
  - Code: `zipPath` in `explorer.js`, `files.zip_entry`. Tested: `test_zip_takes_a_free_name_and_holds_what_the_explorer_shows` (a folder with an empty folder, hidden names, a dotfile, links to a file and to a folder outside, a pipe, a 1970 date; unzipped with `ditto`; a file in a folder, the next free names, paths outside or missing refused, a failed zip removed), `fs.zip` in `test_commands_of_the_explorer_menu`, and a sandbox selftest (window visible, 12 checks): the item right after Paste, a folder, a file in a folder, the next name, no item on the empty space, the two toasts of a slow zip (request delayed), the toast of an error.
  - The folder under the mouse is that of a drop from Finder (`treeInto`, shared with `drop.js`): a folder's row, that folder; a file's row, its folder; the empty space, the project folder. Same mark (the folder's row; the project folder, or a row off screen: the whole tree) and a label next to the mouse, "Move a.py into sub/" (over no target, only the name). Its own folder, the entry itself, or a folder inside it: no mark, and a drop there does nothing; so does a drop outside the tree.
  - The move is Cut and Paste's (`fs.paste` with `cut`, `files.paste`): nothing is replaced, a taken name gives a toast ("a.txt already exists in sub"), a folder cannot go into itself. Its tabs and the open folders follow (`movedPaths`), the target folder opens, and the moved entry is selected, as after Paste. Logged by the backend as a Paste: `moved a.txt -> sub/a.txt from proj into proj`.
  - No confirmation (VS Code asks by default): nothing is replaced, and dragging it back undoes the move. Not Option for a copy (VS Code has it): not asked for, and Copy / Paste does it.
  - A closed folder under the mouse opens after 0.5 s, so that a folder inside it can be reached. Within 24 px of the tree's top or bottom edge, the tree scrolls (8 px every 40 ms). Esc, the window losing the keyboard, or a mouseup that never reached the page (the next move comes with no button down) cancel the drag. The click that follows the mouseup is swallowed, so a drop does not also open the file under the mouse.
  - In the results of the explorer search: the same, without the opening of a closed folder (the results open their folders themselves).
  - Not tested: a real drag with the mouse (the check sends synthetic mouse events), and the swallowed click (a synthetic mouseup makes no click).
- Copy path / Copy relative path: the path as the tree shows it (symlinks not followed), relative to the project folder, not to the task's `cwd`. The backend writes it with `pbcopy`, under a UTF-8 locale, or accents come out wrong.
- A symlink is renamed, moved and trashed as a link, never its target (`files.entry` resolves only the folder of the entry).
- Tabs follow: a renamed or moved file keeps its tab, in every task of the project (tasks of one project share its files), with its unsaved edits and the language of its new extension. A deleted file's tab closes, except with unsaved edits: it then stays as "Deleted on disk", and Save writes the file again.
- Unsaved edits: the tab's name in italics (Renaud, 2026-11-12: "instead of showing an orange dot, just show the title in italics"); until then a yellow dot after the name. The × and the star of a project tab stay upright. `.tab.dirty` in `app.css`, set by `renderFileTabs` (`files.js`). The first edit also redraws the bars of the panes (`renderBars`), so Save becomes active at once; until 2026-11-12 it stayed grey until something else redrew the bar (Cmd+S worked). Checked with a sandbox selftest: before an edit, upright and Save grey; after it, italic and Save active. Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- One text per file (Renaud picked it on 2026-11-11 from three options, after a loss: on the idle screen he edited a file without saving, a reply arrived, and the arriving task showed the file as on disk; the edits were still in the tab of the task that he left, out of sight). The text tabs of one file, in all the tasks of a project (and the tabs of a project picked in the dropdown), show one text, as VS Code's editors of one file share one model. A file outside the project is shared by the tasks of every project (since 2026-11-14: `peerTab` compares absolute paths, `fullPathOf`). Each tab keeps its own pane, cursor, scroll, Diff, Wrap state and undo history; the text and the state of the file (`version`, `head`, `dirty`, `diskChanged`, `gone`) are shared. Code (`files.js`): `tab.file` holds that state, and the tab's properties of the same names read and write it (`shareFile`), so `saveTab`, the poll and the explorer needed no change. An edit in one tab (typing, undo, replace) is applied at once to the others (`docChanged`, the same ChangeSet; a whole replace if the texts ever differ). A tab that opens takes the text of a tab of that file in another task, unsaved edits included, not the disk's (`peerTab` in `openFile`). Reload replaces the text in all of them, each cursor kept where it can be (`replaceText`). Save As onto a file that another task has open: those tabs take the saved text and share it. Untitled tabs, images and PDFs are not shared.
  - Decided by Claude: closing a tab whose unsaved edits another tab holds asks nothing (VS Code does the same), and the × of a pane lists only the edits that would be lost (`losesEdits`). Undo in a tab may undo an edit made in another task's tab, since it is one text with one sequence of changes (as in VS Code). The quit question names the files with unsaved edits since 2026-11-14 (below).
- Unsaved edits at quit (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-14: "absolutely"; before, quitting lost the unsaved edits of every tab of every task without a word). The one question at quit (App, "Quitting") names the working tasks and the files with unsaved edits: "2 files have unsaved edits, which quitting loses: a.py, Untitled-1." (the first 5 names, then "and N more"; a file's path in its project, as the question of its ×, or the name of an untitled tab). No question when there is neither. The question runs in Python on the main thread, where waiting for the page would block, so the page tells the backend the list each time it changes: `files.dirty` (`Core.dirty_files`; logged at DEBUG only, in `QUIET`; no state push). `files.js`: `dirtyFiles()` takes every tab of `S.work` whose `dirty` is set, one name per text (tabs that share a text count once); untitled tabs from Cmd+N F count once typed in. `syncDirty()` sends the list when it differs from the last one sent; it runs after a change of `dirty` (the setter of `shareFile`, coalesced with a timer of 0), after a dirty tab is destroyed, and at each tick of the 2 s poll, which also catches a tab removed elsewhere and sends the first list after a (re)load, since `dirtySent` starts undefined (a reloaded page has no unsaved edits). Decided by Claude: no autosave and no new UI (as asked); the list is not cleared when the front end disconnects (it would need `server.py`; a new page sends its own list within 2 s); a file open in two projects under the same path shows its path twice. Tested: `tests/test_quit.py` (the text, `files.dirty` over the websocket, DEBUG only), and the sandbox runs of App, "Quitting": two dirty tabs (an edited `a.txt`, a typed Untitled-1) gave `["a.txt", "Untitled-1"]` in the backend within 1 s; after saving `a.txt` only Untitled-1 was left, after closing it the list was empty and the close button asked nothing. Not seen by Renaud; the app must be restarted.
  - Tested on 2026-11-11 with two sandbox selftests (window visible for the first; the second ended covered, which does not affect checks that wait for no frame; `shared_text_test.py` and `shared_saveas_test.py` in that session's scratchpad, not kept): an edit on the idle screen showed in the arriving task's tab, built at that select; typing in one showed in the other; closing one asked nothing, closing the last asked; a tab opened again took the edits; Save in one cleaned both (same version as the disk); a write by the agent reloaded both; a file opened on the idle screen and edited, with no other tab, stayed with the task left and showed in the next task's new tab; one that another task held went at the select, its edits in that task; Save As of an untitled tab onto a file open in another task. Not tested: real typing and real clicks. Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- 3a, file tabs: CodeMirror 6, editable. Editing is required before Brain Fry is usable (Renaud, 2026-11-09). Cmd+S saves. "Diff vs HEAD" shows the changes against git HEAD inside the editor (unified view, `@codemirror/merge`); the file stays editable.
- Wrap (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-10): a toggle button just left of "Diff vs HEAD", in text tabs only (thread tabs always wrap; viewers have no lines). It belongs to the file, as VS Code's Option+Z: every open tab of the file follows, in every task of the project, and the window remembers it (the key `wrap:<project path>:<path>` of `window.json`, see App, "Window settings"; kept only while on; a rename carries it to the new path). Off by default, for every extension. Works with Diff vs HEAD on (the deleted lines wrap too). No key. Tested with a sandbox selftest; Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted. The "on" look of the toggles of the file bar (Wrap, Diff vs HEAD, 100%) is a gray pressed button, not the blue fill (Renaud, 2026-11-13: blue was too prominent; option 1 of 5 in a mockup).
