- Run a Python file (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-11: "a play button at the top. Clicking on it opens a terminal in panel 3 at the root of the folder, calls Python, and then the path to the file"; VS Code's "Run Python File"). A green ▶ at the right end of the bar of a `.py` file tab (VS Code puts it at the top right of the editor), not in an untitled tab. A click saves the file first when it has edits, as VS Code does (a save refused because the file changed on disk: nothing runs, the bar shows the choice). Then a new terminal tab in pane 3 (which opens if it was empty), whose shell starts at the project's root and gets `python <path relative to the project>` typed, then Enter, so the line stays in the shell's history and the shell stays after the script. With `.venv/bin/python` when the project has `.venv/bin/python` (VS Code picks the workspace's `.venv` too; the `python` of a conda PATH lacks a uv project's packages). The tab shows in pane 3 but the keyboard stays in the file, as in VS Code (`python.terminal.focusAfterLaunch` is off by default): Cmd+J 3 goes to the terminal, for Ctrl-C or an `input()`. A new terminal at each click (VS Code reuses one); the tabs add up in pane 3. No key. No task selected: a project tab, as Cmd+N T (see Project tabs). The backend: `shell.new` takes `python` (`Core._python_line`: a `.py` file of the project, quoted with `shlex`), and `_type_when_ready` types the line once the shell waits for a command: at its first prompt when its bash runs shell_init.bash (`PtySession.prompts` counts the `cwd` sequences), else once its output has been quiet for 0.3 s, at most 5 s after the start. Typed at once, the terminal would echo the line once before the prompt and once after it. Tested: `test_run_python_file` (a fake shell: the root, a path with a space, `.venv`, a `.txt`, `../`, a missing file and a folder refused), `test_run_python_file_in_bash` (the line shows once, after the prompt), and a sandbox selftest (window visible, Renaud's real bash profile): an edited file saved, then run in pane 3 from the root, the keyboard still in the editor, no button on a `.txt`. Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- 3a is pure black (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-10; One Dark's own background is #282c34): the text and thread editors with their gutters, the image viewer around the image, and the active file tab. A rule in `app.css` whose ids beat the scoped classes of One Dark; One Dark keeps its colors for the text, the active line, the panels and the tooltips. The file bar (#21252b), the PDF backdrop (#3b3f46) and 3b (#1e1e1e) keep their grey. Checked with a sandbox selftest (computed backgrounds `rgb(0, 0, 0)`, screenshot). Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted. Open: whether the file bar and the PDF backdrop should be black too.
- Inline code in markdown (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-13: "it does not display prominently enough"): a faint grey pill (`rgba(255, 255, 255, .10)`, radius 3 px) behind each inline code, backticks included, with the backticks dark grey (#4b5263); the text keeps its color. Why it was plain: One Dark gives the markdown tag of inline code (`monospace`) no style and colors only the backticks, in the green of every markdown mark, a thin glyph where the green barely shows. Renaud picked option 2 at strength 0.10 from a second mockup, on black (the first one used One Dark's #282c34 by mistake, and he found its orange too flashy). Rejected in the two mockups: orange text, an orange pill, a thin outline, a muted tint (blue, sage, sand, lilac), brighter text. Code: a `ViewPlugin` in `frontend/codemirror.js` adds the classes `cm-inline-code` and `cm-inline-code-mark` (the backticks) to the `InlineCode` nodes on screen, for `.md` and `.markdown` files and thread tabs (`languageFor`); the look is in `app.css`. A decoration, not a highlight style: the grammar gives the same tag to the text of a fenced block without a language, and the style of a tag would not cover the backticks (child nodes). The bundle was rebuilt with the esbuild step of `vendor.sh` alone, after a check that the cached build tree gave the committed bundle byte for byte. Checked with a sandbox selftest (4 inline codes marked, computed pill and backtick colors, the fenced block not marked, a typed inline code marked at once, screenshot). Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- Headings in markdown are blue (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-15: "headlines should be blue"; One Dark draws them coral #e06c75, bold, with the `#` marks in the green of every markdown mark): One Dark's blue #61afef, bold, on the whole heading line, `#` included (as VS Code), and on the `===` / `---` line of a Setext heading. Code: a `HighlightStyle` on `heading1` to `heading6` in `frontend/codemirror.js`, added by `languageFor` to markdown files and thread tabs, under `Prec.high` so that its CSS is mounted after One Dark's and wins on the `#`. Not the tag `heading`: the header row of a GitHub table has it too (it stays coral, but the editor does not parse tables anyway, below). The preview's headings are unchanged. Side effect, left alone: the last line of a YAML frontmatter, with its closing `---`, is a Setext heading for the grammar, so it is blue too. Checked by rendering the real bundle in WebKit with the extensions of a file tab and measuring the computed colors.
- HTML and bold in markdown (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-15, after a page that showed every format: "remove the yellow from the HTML ... only the brackets yellow, the rest gray", "whatever is in bold should have a yellow background"; he picked from a mockup "html 1, bold 1b, but 7%"). HTML: `<`, `>`, `</` and `/>` in One Dark's yellow #e5c07b; tag names, attribute names, `=` and attribute values in its gray #7d8799 (the gray of comments). Before, One Dark drew the tag names yellow, the brackets plain, attribute names coral, values green and `=` cyan. The text between the tags and the HTML comments are unchanged. Bold: a faint yellow behind the text of each bold (`rgba(229, 192, 123, .07)`, radius 3 px), the `**` or `__` outside it and still green; also in headings, lists and quotes; a bold inside a bold is not marked twice. Rejected in the mockup: a darker gray (#5c6370, #4b5263); the yellow at 10, 18, 28 % or solid with dark text; the background over the `**` too (as the inline code pill). Code, in `frontend/codemirror.js`, for markdown only (`md()`): the HTML colors are in the same `HighlightStyle` as the blue headings (`mdStyle`, under `Prec.high`); the bold is a mark `cm-bold` that the inline code plugin adds (one walk of the tree for both), drawn by `app.css`. A mark, not a highlight style: a style per token would round the corners of every span inside the bold. Checked by rendering the rebuilt bundle in WebKit with the extensions of a file tab and measuring the computed colors and backgrounds.
- What the markdown editor recognizes (measured on 2026-11-15 for a page that showed Renaud every format, `languageFor`): `markdown()` with its default base, plain CommonMark. GitHub's extensions (tables, task lists, strikethrough, bare URLs) stay plain text in the editor, while the preview has them; fenced code is not colored by its language (no `codeLanguages`), while the preview colors it. Footnotes and math: plain text (`[^1]` reads as a link). Colors of One Dark: the marks (`#`, `*`, `_`, `>`, `-`, list numbers, fences, link brackets) green #98c379; link text and images underlined, in green for the brackets and grey #7d8799 for the text and URL; URLs of reference definitions and autolinks cyan #56b6c2; info strings of fences and reference labels blue #61afef; HTML tags sand #e5c07b (changed the same day, above); comments grey #7d8799; escapes cyan; entities coral #e06c75; italic and bold keep the text color #abb2bf (bold got a background the same day, above).
- The agent writes the same files. Brain Fry checks the open files every 2 s (version = mtime and size). A file without my edits follows the disk. A file with my edits shows "Changed on disk", with two buttons: reload (drop my edits) or overwrite with mine. A save refuses to overwrite a file that changed since I opened it, and shows the same choice.
- A save is atomic and keeps the file's mode (a script stays executable) and its line separators (CRLF stays CRLF). Text files over 2 MB are not opened. Paths must stay inside the project.
- Images and PDFs open in viewers, as tabs of 3a (built 2026-11-10, asked by Renaud). The kind comes from the extension (`files.view_kind`). The images are what WebKit decodes: PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, AVIF, BMP, ICO, TIFF, HEIC. SVG is text and stays in the editor. The bytes come over HTTP from `/file/<task id>/<path>?v=<version>`, not as text over `/ctl`: `<img>` and PDF.js want a URL, the browser streams the file, and a 20 MB PDF does not block the websocket of state and commands. The route serves only those extensions, inside the project, up to 200 MB, with `nosniff` and `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox`, so that it can never serve a page that runs in the app's origin.
- A viewer has no edits, so it always follows the disk: the 2 s poll sees the new version and reloads it (a new `?v=` URL, never a cached copy). A file that is half written fails to load: the old one stays on screen, and the next version is tried at the next poll. A version that failed is not fetched again. A file that comes back as it was clears "Deleted on disk".
- Image: fits the panel and is never enlarged; a click on it or the 100% button shows it at its size. A checkerboard shows transparency. The bar shows the size in pixels and in bytes. A new version is loaded aside, then swapped in, without a blank between the two.
- PDF: PDF.js's page viewer (`PDFViewer`, `PDFLinkService`, `PDFFindController`): continuous scroll, text selection, links (web links open in the default browser), fit to the width at first. Its tools sit in the bar: page box, zoom (- / + / a list: Width, Page, 50% to 200%; Cmd+scroll and pinch), find (Cmd+F, Enter next, Shift+Enter previous, Esc back to the PDF, a count "1 / 2"). A reload keeps the page, the point on it and the zoom (Renaud: "I want the PDF to refresh when the file is refreshed").
- The bar of 3a is rebuilt only when its HTML changes, and the PDF's tools are one element that moves into it: `render()` runs at every state push, and a rebuild would take the focus out of the find field.
- PDF.js's viewer does not fit itself to a new size (its app does it on a window resize), so a ResizeObserver on the container fits it again (Width, Page). A PDF tab that is hidden (another tab, Organize mode) may come back with its scroll position reset: the tab keeps the last location that PDF.js reported while it was on screen (`updateviewarea`: page, and the point at the top left in PDF units) and goes back to it when it shows. Reports are ignored while the tab is hidden and until that restore has run, since PDF.js may report page 1 first. A reload uses the same location, so it also works while the tab is hidden. A PDF opened while its tab is hidden (restored with the task) gets a scale for a width of 0 (negative); the fit on showing corrects it.
- Other binary files and text files over 2 MB: a toast with an "Open in default app" button (`fs.open`, macOS `open`). Images and PDFs have the same button in the bar.
- Markdown preview (built 2026-11-14, asked by Renaud: "right click on explorer to preview md files", "preview gets refreshed every time the file is updated (even if not saved)"; `web/mdpreview.js`, `mdpreview.css`). As VS Code's Open Preview:
  - "Open Preview" in the right-click menu of 2a, for a `.md` or `.markdown` file only. A tab "Preview README.md" (tooltip "Preview of <path>"; the bar shows the path and "Deleted on disk." when it is gone). It opens beside the file when the file is on screen in a pane (1 and 2, 3 and 4: VS Code's Open Preview to the Side, so the editor and its preview show together while I type), else where a file opens (`openPane`). It takes the keyboard (arrows and Page Up/Down scroll it). No key and no button in the editor's bar (not asked).
  - Live: while a text tab of the file is open anywhere in the project (`peerTab`, the shared text of "one text per file"), the preview shows that text, unsaved edits included: every change of the editor's text (typing, undo, a reload from disk) renders it again, at most once per 150 ms, or per twice the last render time for a long file (this file, 306 KB, renders in about 60 ms). With no editor open, it follows the disk: the previews on screen are checked every 2 s (`fs.versions`, as the file tabs). When the last editor of the file closes, the preview reads the disk again at once (dropped edits, or edits saved since it read the file). A hidden preview renders when it shows (a ResizeObserver).
  - Only the top-level blocks that changed are replaced in the page (`mdMorph`, `isEqualNode` from both ends): the other blocks keep their images (no reload, no flash), the scroll position and the selection. No scroll sync with the editor (VS Code has one; not asked).
  - Kept like any tab: its path is `preview:<file>`, in the task's `open_files` (or project.json) and its grid, so it is restored with the task (`openFile` hands such a path to `openPreview`). A double-click makes it a project tab. A rename or move in 2a carries it to the new path; Delete closes it. Cmd+N O (copy a tab) does not list previews.
  - The renderer is `frontend/markdown_html.js`, in the CodeMirror bundle (`CM.markdownHTML`), on the parser that the editor already uses (`@lezer/markdown`, with GitHub's tables, task lists, strikethrough and bare links): `npm install` of marked and DOMPurify was refused by auto mode (Renaud's rule: no Bash command that reaches the network), and one grammar for the editor and its preview agree. It handles CommonMark's blocks and inlines, tight and loose lists, reference links, GitHub's heading slugs (`-1`, `-2` for duplicates). Fenced blocks in a language of the editor (`py`, `python`, `js`, `ts`, `json`, `md`, `html`, `css`, `yaml`, `sql`, `sh`, `bash`, `toml` ...) are colored with One Dark's classes (`highlightCode`), as in the editor. Not covered: footnotes, math, emoji shortcodes, Mermaid.
  - Security: a markdown file may hold any HTML, and the page can drive the shells. The HTML is parsed by `DOMParser` (inert: no script runs, no image loads), then only an allowlist of tags and attributes stays (`MD_TAGS`, `MD_ATTRS`; script, style, iframe, svg, form... go with their content; `on*` and `style` go); classes only CodeMirror's (`ͼ...`), `language-...` and `task`. Links: http, https and mailto (they open in the default browser, `target=_blank` as the links of a PDF), and paths of the project; a `javascript:` link loses its `href`, also when spelled with tabs or newlines (WebKit's URL parser drops them). Images: http, https, `data:image/png|gif|jpeg|webp|avif`, and the project's images through `/file/` (no SVG: the route serves no page). No `id` in the page: a heading has `data-anchor` (its slug), and an `id` or `name` of the file's HTML becomes `data-anchor`; an element `id="tree"` would have replaced the explorer for `$('#tree')`.
  - Links in the preview: `#anchor` scrolls to the heading; a link to a markdown file of the project opens its preview in the same pane (scrolled to its anchor), as VS Code; any other file of the project opens in its tab (`openFile`); outside the project: a toast.
  - Look: VS Code's preview on the black of column 3 (system font 14 px, headings 1 and 2 underlined, tables with borders), the editors' Menlo for code with their faint pill for inline code (Renaud's pick of 2026-11-13), code blocks on #16171a. The task boxes are drawn in CSS: this WebKit's checkbox is round and looked like a bullet.
  - Tested: a sandbox selftest (2026-11-14, window visible): the menu item, the pane beside the editor, headings, the alignment of table columns, task boxes, colored code, a project image through `/file/`, the HTML refused (`onclick`, `onerror`, `<script>`, `javascript:`, `id="tree"`: nothing ran, one `#tree` in the page), an anchor click, an edit not saved shown at once with the image element kept, the editor closed (back to the disk), a line appended on disk (shown by the poll), a link to another markdown file at its anchor; a second run: the previews restored in their panes after a restart, and a rename in 2a carried to the preview. The renderer was also run with Node on the repo's markdown files.
- In a code editor, the editor keeps Cmd+D (next occurrence) and Cmd+Up/Down (start / end of the file); the other Brain Fry keys still work.
- Find and Select All Occurrences (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-11: "cmd-F to find in a document. cmd-G to select all the occurrences of the highlighted text, and edit it in parallel"). Cmd+F opens CodeMirror's find panel (at the bottom of the editor, its own look; the query starts from the selected text) in the text or thread tab whose editor has the keyboard (CodeMirror's own key); a PDF tab opens its find field when the keyboard is in the PDF or in its bar. Since 2026-11-14, Cmd+F from anywhere else opens the explorer search (Explorer search, above; Renaud: "I want Command-F to open from anywhere"), also from a terminal, and Cmd+J F opens it from inside a file. From 2026-11-11 to 2026-11-14, Cmd+F opened this panel (or the PDF's field) in the tab on screen in the pane that I used last, wherever the keyboard was (in the explorer, on a tab bar, nowhere), but not from a text field nor a terminal (VS Code's Cmd+F in a terminal finds in the terminal: not built). Cmd+G: VS Code's Select All Occurrences (its key is Cmd+Shift+L, which CodeMirror also has), then typing edits every selection. From the find field: every match of the query (with its match case / regexp / by word options), and the keyboard goes back to the text. In the editor: every occurrence of the selected text (case-sensitive). Nothing selected, or no match: nothing happens. Renaud removed the word at the cursor (VS Code's behaviour with nothing selected) on 2026-11-11. At most 1000 occurrences, as CodeMirror's commands; above, nothing. Cmd+G was CodeMirror's find next: Enter in the find field still does it, Shift+Enter goes back (Cmd+Shift+G is Review mode). In a thread tab (read-only) Cmd+G does nothing: Renaud removed it there on 2026-11-11; the key stays taken, so that it never means find next in one tab and select all in another. The command is `selectAllOccurrences` in `frontend/codemirror.js` (the bundle exports it, with `openSearchPanel` and `Prec`); `@codemirror/search` is now a direct dependency (same version, 6.7.2). Tested with a sandbox selftest (window visible): Cmd+F from nothing, the explorer, a terminal (nothing), a PDF; Cmd+G from the field, with a selection, with nothing selected, in a thread tab, with no match; Esc. Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- Terminal tabs (`web/shells.js`): one shell (`$SHELL -l`; bash: `bash --init-file shell_init.bash`, see Cmd+N O above) per tab, in a PTY of the app, started in the task's `cwd`, in any pane of the grid. None at start: Cmd+N T. `exit` closes the tab; × ends its shell, and the tab goes when the state no longer lists it. Hidden tabs keep running. Named after the shell and their position ("zsh 2"); a task's terminals are numbered after its project's, which come before them (Project tabs). The tooltip of the tab shows the folder where the shell is now (bash tells it, see Cmd+N O above; another shell: where it started). Before 2026-11-11 it said "Terminal in undefined": the state carries no `cwd` for a task.
- Cmd+K in a terminal tab (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-12: "cmd k in a terminal should flush it"): clears it, scrollback included, as VS Code (xterm.js's `clear()`) and Terminal.app do. The line of the cursor (the prompt and what is typed) stays as the first line. The front end also sends `clear` on the PTY's websocket, and the backend drops its replay up to the last newline (`PtySession.clear_replay`): without that, a page reload would bring the cleared output back. Decided by Claude: terminal tabs only, not 4a (clearing Claude Code's screen under its TUI leaves its input box half drawn until it redraws); no menu item. Tested: `test_clear_replay_keeps_the_line_of_the_cursor`, and a sandbox selftest (fake `HOME`, scripts in that session's scratchpad, not kept): after `seq 1 80` and `echo abc` typed but not run, Cmd+K on xterm.js's textarea left one line, `$ echo abc`; a new websocket got only that line as replay; Enter then ran it. Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- The size of a terminal (4a and the terminal tabs): xterm.js's FitAddon picks the rows and columns from the computed height and width of the div that holds the terminal (`.term`), and subtracts only the padding of the terminal's own element (`.xterm`). With the global `* { box-sizing: border-box }`, that computed height included the 6 px top padding of `.term`, so the last row ran up to 6 px past the bottom of 4a, at some window heights only (Renaud, 2026-11-11: "the last line is a little cut"). Fixed with `box-sizing: content-box` on `.term`. Checked with a sandbox selftest that fits one terminal at 20 heights of `#terms` and compares the bottom of `.xterm-screen` with the bottom of `.term`: before, 6 of 20 heights cut, up to 6 px; after, none. These are layout measurements, so they hold in a covered window (`visibilityState` hidden), unlike frames.
- A task's open files (`open_files`) and terminal tabs (`terminal_tabs`, their names) are saved in its task file. When the task is selected for the first time after a start, they come back: the files reopen, and fresh shells start (the old ones and their scrollback ended with the app).
- A new task (Cmd+T, Task > New Task, "New task in <project>") starts with the tabs of the previous task (Renaud, 2026-11-10: "the exact same setup"). The previous task is the selected one if it is in the new task's project; else the task of that project that I opened last, archived ones included (a task that I just archived is often the one that I continue). The front end picks it (`newTask`, `from` in the `new` command); the backend copies its `open_files`, `browser_tabs`, `terminal_tabs`, `finder_tabs` and `grid` into the new task file (`actions.new_task`; the grid since 2026-11-14) and starts one fresh shell per terminal tab, in the project root. The front end then copies the view: the grid on screen, when the source was built since the start (each tab's pane and the tab on screen in each, see the grid above), and the open folders of 2a (`copyView`). A browser tab is matched by its position among the saved tabs, since each tab has a new key. Not copied: thread tabs (the new task has no threads), a page's history, and what the old shells hold (their scrollback, their current folder). Unsaved edits show in the new task's tabs, which share the text of the old ones (One text per file, above; until 2026-11-11 the new tab read the file from disk). A task of another project is never a source: its paths belong to that project, and the backend refuses it. The backend copies from the task file, which is current; the front end's copy of a task's lists may be older, because `files.set` and `browser.tabs.set` push no state.
- A fork starts with its parent's tabs in the same way (Renaud, 2026-11-10), its shells in the parent's `cwd`.
- Project tabs (decided by Renaud on 2026-11-11, built the same day). A tab belongs to a task (as above) or to its project. Why: with no task selected (the idle screen, the project view of the dropdown), a terminal or a page had no owner, so Cmd+N T and B showed the toast "Start a task first" (Renaud: "I should just be able to open a terminal"). That toast was Claude's choice, not a technical limit: the backend's `shell.new` takes any task. Renaud weighed four models: tabs of the task only (a new tab goes to the task on screen), of the project only (VS Code; he would lose the files that come back with their task, which he likes), task and project, a home task without a session per project (it would undo the idle screen's layout). He chose task and project.
  - A tab opened with no task selected (idle screen, project view) is a project tab, of every kind: file, terminal, page, Finder. Files too (Renaud: "it becomes a project tab"); until then a file opened on the idle screen was temporary (`idle`, dropped at the next select), and the tabs of the project view lived in memory only.
  - A double-click on a tab turns it into a project tab, or back into a tab of the task on screen. No key (Renaud: "I don't want a key to pin and unpin"; "double-clicking on them would be good enough"). Not on a thread tab (a thread belongs to its task). In the project view, a double-click on a project tab does nothing (no task to give it to); on the idle screen it goes to the task on screen.
  - The mark: a small grey star (★, 9 px) before the name, at 60% opacity. Renaud picked it from a mockup (a star, a bar on the left, an underline under the name, a line under the tab; five colors; a strength slider).
  - Decided by Claude (listed in the mockup's note, not contradicted): a project tab shows in every task of its project, on the idle screen of a task of that project, and in the project view. It is one shell, one page (same process, same scrollback). It has one pane for all tasks: moving or closing it changes it everywhere. In a pane, project tabs come first (left). Which tab is on screen in a pane stays per task. Saved in `<project>/.brain_fry/project.json`; terminals come back as fresh shells after a restart. A new task or a fork does not copy project tabs (they show anyway); Cmd+N O does not list them. Tabs of another project never show.
  - Not called "pinned": a pinned task stays in the inbox, and VS Code's pinned tabs are something else.
  - Code. A tab's `tid` is its owner: a task, or `project:<path>`, the work state that the project view already used (files.js). `w.tabs` holds only the tabs that `w` owns; a view (a task, the idle screen's task, the project view) shows `viewTabs(w)`: its project's tabs, then its own, so project tabs come first in each pane. `act`, `pane` and `mru` belong to the view, `tab.pane` to the tab (one pane for all tasks). Helpers in `app.js`: `viewIds`, `inView` (the checks `tab.tid === wsId()` became `inView(tab.tid)`), `ownerFor` (the selected task, else the project's tabs), `ownerData` and `shellsOf` (what the state says of an owner). In `files.js`: `viewTabs`, `viewWork`, `viewsOf` (closing or moving a project tab fixes the tab on screen in every view), `reown`, `dropDuplicates`, `toggleProjectTab`. A grid names a project tab with `p:` (`gridKey`, grid.js), and the pane of each project tab is in the project's grid.
  - One tab per file in a view: a file that becomes a project tab (opened with no task selected, or double-clicked) closes its tabs in the tasks of the project (they share its text: nothing is lost), and a task's restore skips a file that is a project tab. Decided by Claude: so a task file loses such a file at its first restore; if the project tab closes later, the task does not get it back.
  - Backend: `actions.project_tabs` and `set_project_tabs` (project.json, written atomically, under a lock; a missing or broken file means no tabs). `Core.ProjectTabs` stands in for a task where shells need one (`id`, `project`, `cwd` = the project folder, `terminal_tabs`): `shell.new`, `shell.copy`, `fs.link` and `idle` take `project:<path>` (`Core._owner`). `files.set`, `browser.tabs.set` and `finder.tabs.set` write project.json for it (`Core._set_tabs`). `select` and `idle` start the terminals of the task and of its project, once per run (`_restore_view_shells`); `idle` with `project:<path>` is the project view's. `shell.move {key, to}` gives a shell to the other owner of the same project and saves both lists; the front end moves its tab when the state says so (`renderShells`, `reown`): same xterm, same pane. The state lists each project's tabs under `projects[].tabs`, with a task's names (`views.project_tabs_json`). A page that a project tab opens is a project tab (its opener's owner).
  - Tested: pytest (`test_project_tabs_are_kept_in_project_json`, `test_project_terminals_and_moving_a_terminal`, `test_select_and_idle_start_the_terminals_of_the_project_tabs`) and two sandbox selftests (window visible; `project_tabs_test.py` and `project_tabs_test2.py` in that session's scratchpad, not kept): at start the idle screen of a task showed the project's file (once, though the task file listed it too) and terminal from project.json, with stars; Cmd+N T 2 and a file opened there became project tabs; another project showed none; another task of the project showed them all; a double-click sent a project file back to the task and a task file to the project (the other task lost its own tab of it); a double-click moved a project terminal to the task (same shell, same tab) and the numbering followed; closing a project tab in one task closed it in the others; the project view of a project without tasks made a terminal and a file project tabs, and a double-click there did nothing; a page and a Finder tab opened on the idle screen were project tabs, shown in another task and hidden in another project; a Finder tab went back to a task (both files saved). Not tested: a real mouse double-click (the selftest sends two clicks), a page moved by a double-click, the restore of a project's pages and Finder tabs after a restart. Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- Veil (`web/veil.js`, asked by Renaud on 2026-11-10: the tabs of a new task appeared one by one and "flashed", which is disruptive; "as little disruption as possible"). While a task's tabs are built (its first select after a start, a new task, a fork), 2a and column 3 have opacity 0. Once everything is in place, they fade in together (300 ms, ease-out); the hide is instant. "In place": restoreFiles is done (one `fs.read` per file, the browser tabs), the root and the open folders of 2a are loaded, and each terminal of the task file (`terminalTabs` in the state) has its tab, at two frames in a row (the terminals fit their panes at the next frame). Not waited for: the shell prompts, the page loads, the decoding of images and PDFs. Shown after 1.5 s in any case, with a line in `bf.log`. Not veiled: switching to a task that is already built (nothing is built, the swap is instant), 1 and 4a. A native browser page ignores CSS: `browser.place` carries `alpha` (0 while veiled, so the page is laid out and loads unseen) and `fade` (ms), and `app/browser.py` animates the view's `alphaValue` with `NSAnimationContext`. Checked with a sandbox selftest that samples every frame (2026-11-10): first select after a start with a 4-pane grid, about 900 ms of building, then one fade with nothing changing underneath; a new task, about 430 ms. The native fade itself was not seen (WebKit's snapshots do not hold the native views), only the alpha values sent. Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- JS libraries: `frontend/vendor.sh` builds `src/brain_fry/web/vendor/` once with Node (npm, esbuild): xterm.js as it is, CodeMirror as one bundle (730 KB) with the languages Python, JS/TS, JSON, Markdown, HTML, CSS, YAML, SQL, shell and TOML, and the markdown renderer of the preview tabs (`frontend/markdown_html.js`, see "Markdown preview"), and PDF.js 6.4.299 (4.6 MB, 283 files: the library, its worker, the page viewer component minified by esbuild, character maps and standard fonts for PDFs that do not embed theirs, the wasm decoders of JPEG 2000 and JBIG2, a color profile; not the full viewer app nor the JS engine of PDF forms). PDF.js is loaded at the first PDF, not at the start.
- Rebuilding only the CodeMirror bundle, offline (2026-11-14, when `npm` was refused): copy `frontend/codemirror.js` and `frontend/markdown_html.js` into `~/.cache/brain_fry/vendor-build/`, then run vendor.sh's esbuild line there with `./node_modules/.bin/esbuild` (no `npm ci`). Built from the unchanged sources, it gave a file identical to the committed one.
- Adding an npm package to `frontend/package.json`: first `npm install --save-dev <package>` in `~/.cache/brain_fry/vendor-build`, copy its `package-lock.json` back to `frontend/`, then run `vendor.sh`. On 2026-11-11, `npm ci` and a bare `npm install` accepted the new package (already in the tree through `codemirror`) without writing it into the lock's root entry. npm also sorts its copy of `package.json`: keep the repo's order.
- PDF.js and the system WebKit (macOS 26, checked on 2026-11-10): PDF.js 6 reads the text of a page with `for await` over a `ReadableStream` (also in its worker, to decompress some images), and this WebKit cannot iterate a stream (`ReadableStream.prototype[Symbol.asyncIterator]` is undefined). Pages render without it, but find matched nothing. The legacy build, which is vendored for its other polyfills, has the same code. `web/pdf_polyfill.mjs` adds the iteration (spec behaviour) before PDF.js loads, and `web/pdf_worker.mjs`, the worker given to PDF.js, imports it before PDF.js's worker. Also in PDF.js 6: a document has no `destroy()` any more; its loading task frees it. One `PDFWorker` serves every PDF. The result is committed; Brain Fry needs neither Node nor the network at runtime. npm's `node_modules` stays in `~/.cache/brain_fry/vendor-build`, outside SWITCHdrive.
- Commands that need an answer (files, a new shell) carry a request number on `/ctl`; the backend replies with the same number.

- No annotation editor in the PDF viewer (`annotationEditorMode: AnnotationEditorType.DISABLE`, since 2026-11-17). With PDF.js's default (`NONE`), each `PDFViewer` creates an `AnnotationEditorUIManager` once its document loads, and that manager listens on the whole page: `dragover` and `drop` on `document`, `keydown` and `pointerdown` on `window`, `selectionchange`. Its `dragOver` and `drop` loop over the editor types (`#tt` in the minified code), which stay null until a page of that viewer renders its editor layer. So each PDF viewer that had not rendered a page (a tab restored with its task and never shown) threw a TypeError for every `dragover` in the window: Renaud's `bf.log` had 301 lines `null is not an object (evaluating 'this.#tt')` in two bursts, each while he dragged a card on the board. Reproduced in a sandbox selftest: a hidden viewer with the default logs that line once per `dragover` and once per `drop`; with `DISABLE`, nothing. 3a has no UI to annotate PDFs, so nothing is lost; text selection, links and find do not use the manager.
- A closed PDF tab frees its viewer (`freeTab` in `files.js`, since 2026-11-17, Renaud: "fix it"): `viewer.setDocument(null)` and `link.setDocument(null)`, then the loading task. Until then only the task was destroyed: the viewer kept its pages, its text layers (PDF.js keeps them in a static map) and its listeners on the page (the copy listener, and the editor's manager before `DISABLE`) until the app ended, so a closed tab could still log the `#tt` TypeError above. Save As over an open file frees the old tab the same way. Checked in a sandbox selftest (window visible): after Cmd+W the viewer has no document and no page left, the same file opens again in a new tab, no JS error.
## Finder tab

Asked by Renaud on 2026-11-11 (TODO.md: "very often, within a project, I'm using multiple instances of Finder windows to find different files. Ideally, it has the exact same look and feel as the macOS Finder in Column View"). Built the same day: `finder.py`, `web/finder.js`, `web/finder.css`.

- A new kind of tab of the panes of column 3, next to files, pages and terminals. Cmd+N, then E, then 1-4 (as T, B, F; no digit: pane 1). It starts at the project folder (Renaud: "it probably opens at the root of the project") with nothing selected. Several Finder tabs, in one pane or in several, each at its own place: the pane's tabs are the "with tabs" of the request. Allowed in a project with no task (as Cmd+N F), kept in memory there.
- Look, chosen by Renaud from a local mockup at the real shape (8 states, 3 looks, 3 bottom bars, a slider for the column width): Finder's dark mode (not his light macOS, not Brain Fry's black), the path bar and the status bar (his Finder shows both: `ShowPathbar`, `ShowStatusBar`), a folder icon before the tab's name, columns of 170 px, hidden files dimmed (his Finder shows them: `AppleShowAllFiles`). No toolbar, no sidebar.
- Exactness comes from Finder's own calls (`finder.py`): the order of names (`localizedStandardCompare:` in one `NSArray` sort: dotfiles first, case-insensitive, `file9` before `file10`, folders mixed with files since his Finder has no "Keep folders on top"), the icons (NSWorkspace: one per extension, one for plain folders, one per path for apps, packages and the special folders: home, Desktop, Downloads, `/`, ...), the kind ("Python script", NSURL's localized type description), sizes and free space (NSByteCountFormatter, file style; the free space counts purgeable space: `NSURLVolumeAvailableCapacityForImportantUsageKey`), dates in the format of the system (NSDateFormatter, long date and short time), "Last opened" (Spotlight's `kMDItemLastUsedDate` through `mdls`, not the access time), hidden entries (a leading dot, or the UF_HIDDEN flag: `/usr`, `~/Library`), packages (`NSURLIsPackageKey`, asked only for folders whose name has an extension: an app is a file).
- The whole disk (Renaud: "you should be able to browse the whole disk"): paths are absolute, normalized, symlinks not followed (`finder.absolute`). A folder that macOS's privacy settings hide is an empty column, as in Finder.
- Columns: a click selects. A selected folder shows its content in the next column, a selected file a preview column (Quick Look's thumbnail through `qlmanage -t`: an image, a PDF's first page, a script's text; else the big icon; then the name, kind - size, Created, Modified, Last opened). The folders of the selection are grey, the last selection blue while the tab has the keyboard (`:focus-within`). The columns that do not fit scroll sideways; the end stays in view, also when the preview grows. The grip at the bottom of a divider resizes its column; Option+drag resizes all (in memory, per tab).
- Open (double-click, Cmd+Down): a folder becomes the first column. A file opens in a tab of column 3 (since 2026-11-14 also a file outside the project, by its absolute path: 2a, 3a, 3b, Files outside the project), never in the Finder's own pane: `openPane` now skips a pane whose tab on screen is a Finder tab, as it skips a terminal, and when no other pane is left, the neighbour opens (1 and 2, 3 and 4). An image or a PDF opens in its viewer; a binary file or one over 2 MB opens in its default app at once (no toast: `openFile`'s `external`). An app opens in itself (macOS `open`), as Finder does. Until 2026-11-14 a file outside the project opened in its default app too.
- Keys, as Finder, while the tab has the keyboard: Up/Down move in the column of the last selection (nothing selected: Down the first, Up the last), Right goes into the selected folder (its first entry), Left back to the folder, typing a name selects the first entry that starts with it (letters within 1 s add up; else the next name in Finder's order), Enter renames (the name without its extension selected, as 2a), Cmd+Backspace moves to the Trash (with the confirmation of 2a), Cmd+Up adds the enclosing folder as the first column (the selection stays; at `/`: nothing), Ctrl+C copies the relative path (as 2a). `app.js` leaves Cmd+Up/Down to a Finder tab (they are previous / next inbox item elsewhere), as it leaves them to an editor. Not built: Space (Quick Look), Cmd+[ / Cmd+] (back / forward), several selected entries (Cmd+click, Shift+click), drag and drop of files between its folders (a folder dropped from the real Finder opens a new Finder tab: see Drop from Finder), tags, Finder's double-click on a grip (fit the names).
- Path bar: the folders from the disk ("Macintosh HD") down to the selection. A double-click on one (Finder's gesture) makes it the first column; the selection stays when it is inside. Too long for the pane: the first folders shrink first (to their icon, then away), the selection keeps its name.
- Status bar: "5 items, 393.5 GB available", or "1 of 5 selected, ..." for the column of the last selection.
- Right-click: the menu of 2a, on absolute paths (`finder.*` commands, which call `files.rename`, `files.paste`, `files.entry` with the entry's folder in the role of the project): Open in default app (a file only), Open With and Open in Finder (as in 2a), Open a terminal here (pane 3, `shell.new` with `folder`: any folder on the disk), Cut, Copy, Paste, Copy path, Copy relative path (disabled outside the project), Rename, Delete, Refresh. On the empty space of a column: Open in Finder (its folder), Paste into its folder, Refresh. New File and New Folder first, everywhere (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-12, after 2a's): into the folder, the file's folder or the column's folder, as Paste. They work as Finder's New Folder, not as 2a's field (the Finder tab copies Finder, and in Finder's order a new row's place is unknown before its name): the entry is made at once under a free name, "untitled folder", "untitled folder 2", ..., or "untitled file" (Finder has no New File), selected in its column, and the Rename field opens on it, the whole name selected. Enter or a click elsewhere keeps the typed name, Esc keeps the untitled name, as in Finder. No ellipsis in the labels, as Finder's New Folder (nothing is asked before the entry exists). A new file does not open in a tab (Finder does not). `finder.new` (`finder.create`, then `files.create`); 2a reloads (`fdProjectChanged`). Tested: `test_new_file_and_folder_take_a_free_untitled_name`, `test_backend_commands_and_icon_route`, and a sandbox selftest (window visible): New Folder on a folder (field on "untitled folder" in the next column, with the keyboard, renamed by Enter), New File on the empty space then Esc ("untitled file" stays), New File on a file then a click elsewhere ("untitled file 2", 2a shows both). Cut / Copy / Paste share `S.fileClip` with 2a, both ways: a Finder clip carries the absolute paths (`abs`) and also the project's paths when they are inside it, so 2a dims a cut and pastes it with its own command; outside any project, 2a pastes with `finder.paste`. A rename, a move or a Trash made in a Finder tab inside the project updates the file tabs and 2a (`movedPaths`, `gonePaths`).
- Live: every 2 s the folders of the Finder tabs on screen are read again (only the selected task, in Dev mode, the window visible); a tab is drawn again only when one of its folders changed, and a selection whose entry is gone ends there. A change made in a Finder tab reloads every Finder tab at once. Selecting a file reads its preview again.
- Saved in the task file (`finder_tabs`: for each tab its first column `root` and the selected `path`, absolute; `actions.set_finder_tabs`), restored at the task's first select (`restoreFinderTabs`, before the veil lifts), copied to a new task and a fork, as browser tabs. The grid names a Finder tab `finder#<i>`, its position among the task's. Cmd+N O (copytab.js) does not list Finder tabs (not asked).
- The backend handled a connection's commands one after the other. Quick Look never returns for some files (`qlmanage -t` on a `.bin` of random bytes ran 68 s until killed), and while it hung, every command behind it waited: in the selftest, the next folder did not load. Two fixes: `server.CONCURRENT` (`finder.list`, `finder.info`, `finder.thumb`) run beside the other commands, whose order is unchanged; `qlmanage` gets 3 s, then the preview keeps the big icon (and the failure is remembered per version of the file). Thumbnails are cached in the backend (64, per path, mtime and size) and in the page (50).
- `/icon/<key>.png?s=32|64|256`: the PNG of an icon key that a listing gave out (a key `p-...` names a path that the backend remembers; `e-<ext>` and `folder` need none), drawn by AppKit in the asyncio worker threads (drawing into a bitmap context works off the main thread) and cached. WebKit may cache it a day: a column draws the same icon on many rows at every redraw.
- The top-level names of `finder.js` start with `fd` or contain `Finder`: the scripts share one global scope, and a first version's `select` and `move` replaced app.js's (select a task, the inbox's Cmd+Up/Down).
- Tested: `tests/test_finder.py` (Finder's order, hidden flags, packages, absolute paths only, info and icons, rename, Trash, paste and copy path on absolute paths, the tabs in the task file, a new task and a fork, the backend commands with the icon route and a terminal outside the project, a slow thumbnail that does not hold the other commands). A sandbox selftest (window visible, 2026-11-11): Cmd+N E 2 from 4a, a click on a folder, Right, Down, Cmd+Down on a file (opens in pane 1, the Finder stays in 2), Cmd+Up, a double-click on the path bar, Enter and a rename (the open file tab follows), Left and typing "t", the right-click menu, Copy and Paste into a folder, Cmd+Backspace (Trash stubbed), a file written by the backend that appears within 2 s, the grip, a double-click on a binary file and a text file outside the project (default app, `send` stubbed), an image's thumbnail, the saved `finder_tabs` and the grid record. Not tested: real mouse drags of a grip, Option+drag, a real Trash, the restore after a restart (the code is the browser tabs' path). Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- Still open: the look of Finder's light mode is one CSS block away if he wants it with his light macOS; the preview of a folder or an app's size can be slow on a huge package (`_tree_size` stops after 20,000 folders).

## Drop from Finder

Asked by Renaud on 2026-11-14 ("is it possible to drag/drop a file or folder from the OS X explorer into BF?"). Built the same day: `web/drop.js`, `app/drops.py`, the command `drop.take`, `newFinder(tid, n, folder)` in `finder.js`.

- What a drop does depends on where it lands. Renaud chose from a table of targets: all of them, except "a file dropped on a pane opens as a file tab" (when Renaud chose, 3a showed files of the project only; since 2026-11-14 it opens any file, see 2a, 3a, 3b, Files outside the project), and not the other direction (a drag from 2a to Finder).
  - Claude Code (4a), or a terminal tab (the body of a pane whose tab on screen is a terminal): the paths are pasted at the cursor, with a space between two paths and after the last one, and the terminal takes the keyboard. Folders too, without a `/` at the end.
  - A pane of column 3 (its tab bar, its bar, its body unless a terminal shows there; an empty column 3 is pane 1): each dropped folder opens there as a Finder tab whose first column is that folder, the last one on screen with the keyboard, as Cmd+N E. With no task selected they are project tabs. Files in the same drop are left out. A drop without any folder does nothing and shows a toast that says where a file can go.
  - The explorer (2a): a copy into the folder under the mouse. On a folder's row: that folder. On a file's row: the file's folder. On the empty space, or on a row of the project folder: the project folder. Always a copy (as VS Code), never a move, and with the rules of Paste (`finder.paste`, `cut` false): a name that is taken gets a free one ("notes copy.txt"), nothing is replaced, a folder cannot go into itself. The folder opens and the last copy is selected in the tree. While the results of the explorer search show, a toast names the copy, since the tree is hidden. Not while a name field is open in the tree, and not on the search fields.
  - The header (every mode), or the board of Organize mode: each dropped folder becomes a project, as File > Add New Project... (`addProject`), in the order of the drop, so the window ends on the last one. A drop without any folder: a toast.
  - Anywhere else (the inbox, 1b, the splitters between columns, 4a without a live session, a dialog over the page, Review mode's panels): the drop is refused. The cursor shows it, and nothing happens.
  - A browser page of 3a is a native view over Brain Fry's page: it takes the drop itself (an upload on a site). Nothing was built for it, and `drop.js` never sees that drop.
- The mark and the label. While the drag is over a target, the target has the blue mark of a dragged tab (`.drop-mark`), and a small label next to the mouse says what a drop does there: "Paste the path into Claude Code", "Paste the path into the terminal", "Open the folder as a Finder tab", "Copy into sub/", "Add the folder as a project". Decided by Claude: one gesture does four things, and nothing else tells which one before the mouse is let go. The label is one element (`OSD.label`, the look of `.tab-ghost`): removing it is two lines of `osDropShow`.
- The mark depends on the place only. While a drag is over the page, WebKit tells that it holds files (`dataTransfer.types` is `['Files']`) but neither how many nor of which kind: `items` and `files` are empty until the drop. So a pane shows "Open the folder as a Finder tab" also for a drag of files, and the toast comes after the drop.
- How the paths arrive. A web page gets the names of dropped files, never their paths (`getData('text/uri-list')` is empty, a folder is a file of size 0). pywebview's view of the page (`WebKitHost.performDragOperation_`, `platforms/cocoa.py`) reads the file URLs from the drag's pasteboard, for a drag whose source is another app, and keeps (name, path) pairs in `webview.dom._dnd_state["paths"]`, but only while `_dnd_state["num_listeners"]` is above 0. `drops.enable()` (in `main.run`) sets it to 1. At a drop, `drop.js` sends the names that it got (`drop.take`), and `drops.take` returns `{path, dir}` for each name (the path recorded last under that name, names compared in NFC, a folder's trailing `/` removed), then empties the record. Internal to pywebview 6.2, as `_recreate_menus` and the title bar: if it changes, `take` finds nothing and the page shows "Could not read the path of what was dropped", and `drop_check.py` fails.
  - Rejected: pywebview's public way, `window.dom.document.events.drop += DOMEventHandler(...)`. Its JS handler serializes the whole event for Python, and a node of the event (`currentTarget` is the document) is serialized with everything under it: the whole page at each drop. The handler is also lost when the page reloads, and the paths would arrive in Python, away from the code that knows the target.
  - Rejected: reading the system's drag pasteboard (`NSPasteboardNameDrag`) at the drop. No pywebview internals, but the simulated drop of the window check uses a pasteboard of its own, so the real path would have stayed untested.
- The paths in a terminal: a backslash before every character that is not a letter, a digit, one of `_-.,:+=@%/` or above ASCII, as Terminal.app types a dropped file (`osShellPath`). A path with a control character (a line break in a name) is left out with a toast: in a shell without bracketed paste (bash 3.2), what follows the line break would run. The text goes through xterm.js's `paste()`, so it is one bracketed paste where the program asked for it (Claude Code does), and it goes through the terminal's own input, so a hidden terminal drops it as it drops keys.
- What Claude Code does with it (read in the binary of 2.1.325, its paste handler): it splits a pasted line at each space that `/` (or a quote, then `/`) follows, and when a line holds several parts, all must look like paths. Each part is trimmed, its quotes or backslashes removed, and a part that ends in `.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.gif` or `.webp` is read from disk and attached as an image; any other path stays in the prompt as text. Quoted paths ('...' or "...") would work too; the backslashes were chosen because the same text is right for a shell. Tried on 2026-11-14 with the real Claude Code 2.1.325 in a PTY (hooks off, no prompt sent, so no turn; script in the session's scratchpad, not kept): one bracketed paste of two escaped paths of PNG files, one with a space and parentheses in its name, showed `[Image #1] [Image #2]`; the escaped path of a `.txt` file stayed in the input box as typed, with its backslash.
- Drags that do not come from Finder. A drag that starts in Brain Fry's page (a card of the board, an image of a tab, selected text) is left alone: `dragstart` sets `OSD.inner`, and `drop.js` then ignores it. Its listeners are on `window`, in the capture phase, and stop the events of a drag of files, so the board's own listeners and CodeMirror never see one. A mouse event clears `OSD.inner` and a mark left on screen, since no mouse event comes during a drag: a lost `dragend` or `dragleave` (the element was redrawn) cannot block the next drop. A file dragged out of a browser page of 3a onto Brain Fry's page has a source inside the app, so pywebview records no path: the toast "Could not read the path...".
- Before 2026-11-14 (measured with the same simulated drop, on the code without `drop.js`): a drop was refused everywhere (WebKit answers "no operation" where the page cancels nothing, and it does not navigate to the file, which Claude had expected), except on an editor of 3a, where CodeMirror inserted the text of the dropped file into the open file. That is gone: a file dropped on an editor does nothing.
- Logs: `drop: 4 dropped, 4 with a path (1 folders)` (the backend), then what was done: `drop: 4 paths pasted into Claude Code`, `drop: 1 folder as Finder tab in pane 2`, the `copied a -> b` lines of `finder.paste`, the `init` line of a new project. Never the names or the paths of what goes into a terminal (in Claude Code they are a part of a prompt): `drop.take` is in `QUIET`, and `_describe` writes its `names` as a count.
- Tested: `tests/test_drops.py` (the match by name, the path recorded last, NFC and NFD names, the emptied record, no pywebview, `drop.take` over the websocket, no name in the log at DEBUG), and `tests/window/drop/drop_check.py WORKDIR`, 55 checks in a real window on a sandbox (2026-11-14, window visible). It simulates a drop at the level of AppKit, as WebKit's own tests do: an object that stands for the drag (`NSDraggingInfo`, no source), with a pasteboard of its own that holds the file URLs, is given to the page's view (`draggingEntered:`, `draggingUpdated:`, `performDragOperation:`). The mouse and the system's drag pasteboard are not touched, WebKit sends the page real `dragenter`, `dragover` and `drop` events, and pywebview records the paths as for a real drop. Checked: each target with its mark and label; four paths into a fake `claude` as one bracketed paste (a name with a quote, parentheses, `&`, `$` and an accent, a decomposed name, a folder); two paths typed at the prompt of bash; the keyboard in the terminal; a folder on a tab bar and on an editor (a Finder tab in that pane); a file on an editor (a toast, the text unchanged, no tab); the four places of the explorer, the free name, the source still there; the header and the board (a project, and a toast for a file); 4a without a task and the inbox (refused, no mark); a file without a path (a toast); a card of the board still moves by its own drag. `DROP_CHECK_SHOTS=DIR` saves a picture of the window during each drag.
- Not tested: a real drag from Finder with the mouse (what Finder puts on the pasteboard, the periodic `dragover` while the mouse is still, the cursor's badge, a drag with Cmd or Option held: a drag that allows no copy is refused, since the page always answers "copy"), Review mode's 4a, a drop while the page redraws under the mouse, a drag from a browser page of 3a.
- Not built: a file dropped on a pane as a file tab (Renaud: no), a drag out of Brain Fry to Finder (Renaud: no), a drop into a folder of a Finder tab (a folder dropped on a Finder tab opens another Finder tab), a move in place of a copy (Option or Cmd held), a progress display for a large copy (the connection's other commands wait until `finder.paste` ends, as for Paste).

## Selection as context

Asked by Renaud on 2026-11-10: what VS Code does without a key. The text that I select in an editor goes with my next prompt to Claude Code, which shows "N lines selected".

- VS Code does it over its IDE connection: the extension runs a websocket server, and sends `selection_changed` (`filePath`, `selection`, `text`) to the CLI at each change. Seen in the binary of 2.1.322. Rejected for now: the protocol is internal, and its discovery and authentication were not checked. It would also bring `at_mentioned` (the key that types `@path#L51-52`) and `openDiff`.
- Brain Fry does it through its hook. The front end sends the selection of the active file tab (`selection.set` on `/ctl`, 120 ms after the last change). The backend keeps it in `~/.brain_fry/selection.json`: session id, the app's pid, path, first and last line, text, unsaved flag. At UserPromptSubmit, the hook prints it when the prompt's session id is the file's and the pid is alive. Claude Code adds that stdout to the model's context as "UserPromptSubmit hook additional context". Tested on 2026-11-10 with `claude -p` and the hook in `--settings`: the model named the file, the lines and the text.
- The text follows the CLI's own words for an IDE selection: "The user selected the lines 51 to 52 from <path>: <text> This may or may not be related to the current task." One line says "line 51". Unsaved edits add a note, because Claude reads the file from disk, where the lines may differ.
- Sent only when the selected task's session runs in the app (`pty`), the tab is a text file (not a thread, an image or a PDF), and 3a is on screen: a selection that I cannot see is not sent. Only the main range of a multiple selection. A selection that ends at column 0 of a line stops at the line before, as in VS Code.
- The path is relative to the session's `cwd` (where Claude runs), or absolute for a file outside it. Paths must stay inside the project.
- At most 10,000 characters; the hook then adds "cut here: read the file for the rest".
- The selection stays until it changes, as in VS Code: every prompt to the task carries it while it is selected. CodeMirror keeps its selection when I click into 4a.
- Indicator: a chip at the top right of 4a, "⧉ 2 lines selected · x.py", with × to unselect. It lies over the terminal, because a strip above it would resize the PTY at each selection, and Claude Code would re-render.
- The file is deleted when the app starts and quits, and when the last front end disconnects. A reloaded page sends its selection, or a clear, at once. The pid check covers a crash. Sessions in VS Code or a terminal never get it: their session id differs.
- The hook in `~/.brain_fry/` must be updated (`bf install`) for this to work: the banner asks for it.
- Not built: a key that types `@path#L51-52` into 4a, as VS Code's Cmd+Option+K (the IDE message `at_mentioned`, lines 0-based; the CLI writes the path relative to its cwd and a final space). Renaud uses the selection, not the key. If wanted later, it needs no IDE connection: the CLI's @-mention parser reads `#L51` and `#L51-52` (regex `^([^#]+)(?:#L(\d+)(?:-(\d+))?)?`, seen in 2.1.322), so the front end can write the text to the PTY, as a bracketed paste so that `@` does not open the file autocomplete (not tested). For a session that runs elsewhere: the clipboard.

## Browser (3a)

Asked by Renaud on 2026-11-10: a decent browser in panel 3 that keeps its cookies and has uBlock. Built 2026-11-10. The research behind the choice of engine is under Rejected.

- Engine: the system WebKit, as a second native view. A browser tab is a tab of 3a (`kind: 'web'`, `web/browser.js`) whose element is a placeholder; the page is a WKWebView (`app/browser.py`) added to the window over the placeholder's rectangle. The page sends the rectangle at every frame and every 250 ms (`browser.place`; WebKit pauses frames while the window is covered). The view hides when its tab is not on screen (another tab or task, Organize mode).
- Why not an `<iframe>`: most sites forbid framing (`X-Frame-Options`, CSP `frame-ancestors`), and WebKit blocks the cookies of an iframe from another origin as third-party, so logins fail.
- A native view always paints above Brain Fry's page. While an HTML dialog covers 3a (the project picker, the splash), the view is hidden and a snapshot of it (`browser.snapshot`, JPEG) fills the placeholder. A toast that would fall on the page moves over 4a. (For PDFs, a native view was rejected for this reason; a browser has no other way.)
- Cookies: one persistent website data store (`WKWebsiteDataStore(forIdentifier:)`, macOS 14+) for every tab of every task, so a new tab has them. Its id is a uuid5 of the Brain Fry home, so tests (`BRAIN_FRY_HOME`) have their own profile. WebKit keeps it in `~/Library/WebKit/python3/WebsiteDataStore/<id>/`, a folder named after the process: if Brain Fry becomes a `.app`, the profile must be moved. pywebview's private mode wipes the default store at each start, not this one (tested).
- Session cookies (no expiry) die with the process in WebKit, as in any browser without session restore (tested). Brain Fry saves them every 30 s and at quit to `~/.brain_fry/browser/session_cookies.json` (mode 600, its folder 700: it holds logins) and sets them back at start. Tested in the window: the first request after a restart carried both kinds of cookie.
- My Firefox cookies (`firefox.py`, asked by Renaud on 2026-11-10): copied once, at the first start of the browser (when `browser/firefox_import.json` does not exist yet), and again by "Overwrite my cookies" in the burger menu. Per site, Firefox's cookies replace the browser's: the cookies of every site that Firefox has cookies for are deleted first (WebKit's data records, one per registrable domain: `google.com`), so that two logins never mix. The sites that only Brain Fry knows keep theirs. Page loads wait during the copy (20 s at most). Open pages keep what they loaded: a reload uses the new cookies.
- What is copied: the persistent cookies of the default container of the profile that Firefox opens (the `Install` section of `profiles.ini`, else the profile whose cookies changed last). Firefox locks `cookies.sqlite` while it runs, so Brain Fry reads a copy of it and of its WAL (the latest writes, about 850 KB here), in a private temp folder deleted at once. Skipped: Firefox's session cookies (it keeps them in memory and in its compressed session store, not in `cookies.sqlite`), partitioned cookies (`^partitionKey=`), container cookies (`^userContextId=`), expired ones. Firefox 13x stores `expiry` in milliseconds (seconds before) and `sameSite` 256 for "not set". CFNetwork caps a cookie's lifetime at 400 days. Renaud's profile on 2026-11-10: 2,714 rows, 2,530 in the default container, 748 hosts.
- The burger menu (☰, next to the logo, `web/menu.js`) holds actions that I need rarely. Two: "Overwrite my cookies", with the time and counts of the last copy. No confirmation: a click that I did not mean replaces the cookies of a site with Firefox's, where I am logged in anyway.
- "Show shortcuts" (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-11: "whatever is easy"): a modal (`#keys` in `index.html`) that lists every key of the app, in three columns, one group per context (everywhere, Dev mode, files and PDFs, browser tab, explorer, Organize mode, Review mode, pickers, new card dialog). The list is static HTML written by hand, read from the key handlers of the front end on 2026-11-11: a new or changed key must be added there too. Esc or a click outside the box closes it; while it shows, a keydown listener on `window` (capture phase, so before every listener of `document`) swallows every key, so that nothing reaches a terminal or a mode. It hides the pages of 3a (`covered()` in `browser.js`), and shares `.modal` with the pickers and the commit dialog of Review mode. `.modal` had no z-index, so `#tree-collapse` (2) and `#sel-chip` (20) painted over every modal and could be clicked through its backdrop (seen in a snapshot, then checked with `elementFromPoint` for Cmd+P). Since 2026-11-11 (Renaud: "fix it"): `.modal` 40, `#toast` 45 (a toast must stay above a dialog, for example a failed create in the new card dialog, as before by page order), the menus 50 and 60 unchanged. Tested with a sandbox selftest: with Cmd+P and with "Show shortcuts" open, the modal is on top of both elements, and the toast on top of the modal; the commit dialog not tested (same class). No key opens it (not asked). Tested with a sandbox selftest (window visible): opens from the menu, Cmd+B swallowed, Esc and a click outside close it, a click inside does not, Esc in Organize mode closes only the modal; a WebKit snapshot checked the look. Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- uBlock Origin Lite, the Safari build of the official release (`ublock.py`). `bf ublock` downloads it, refuses it unless its SHA-256 matches the one that GitHub publishes for the asset (`digest` in the releases API), and unpacks it into `~/.brain_fry/extensions/ublock-lite/`. `bf install` runs it too; `bf ublock --zip` installs a local file. It is loaded through WebKit's extension API (`WKWebExtension`, macOS 15.4+), with all its permissions granted. Full uBlock Origin cannot run: it needs `webRequestBlocking`, which WebKit does not have (and Chrome removed in 2025); only Firefox keeps it.
- The extension needs a fixed identity (`uniqueIdentifier`, base URL `webkit-extension://ublock-lite/`), else WebKit gives it a new one at each start and its settings are lost (tested). Its own pages (popup, dashboard) work only in a view made from `context.webViewConfiguration()`.
- uBlock's rules apply about 2 s after its context loads at a start (measured on 2026-11-10: a load at +1 s showed the ads, one at +2 s did not). Page loads wait until 2.5 s after the load, so that a restored tab shown at once is filtered (tested). The first start after an update compiles the rules again (about 5 s in the spike): those seconds leak.
- Filtering mode: uBOL's default ("optimal": network rules, per-site cosmetic filters, scriptlets). Its generic cosmetic filters (ad slots hidden on any site) run only in its "complete" mode, by design. Both tested offline in a spike (deleted on 2026-11-10). WebKit applies uBOL's CSS only in a page on screen: uBOL inserts it in a `requestAnimationFrame`.
- uBlock's popup: the uBlock button of the toolbar shows uBOL's popup page in an NSPopover. WebKit loads the page but did not call the delegate `presentActionPopup` (macOS 26.7), so Brain Fry shows `action.popupWebView()` itself.
- Toolbar (the bar of 3a): back, forward, reload / stop, address (a URL, a host, `localhost:5173`, or words: a Google search), find (Cmd+F; Enter, Shift+Enter, Esc), uBlock. A tab is named after its page's title; its tooltip is the URL. Cmd+N B (then a pane's digit, see the grid) opens a tab with the address field focused; until 2026-11-10 it was "+ Browser" or Cmd+T.
- Keys: a key monitor (`NSEvent` local monitor) sees each key before WebKit. While a page has the focus, Brain Fry's Cmd keys (Cmd+Up/Down, D, E, Shift+D, N, J, T, P, B, G, 1-9) and the browser's keys that live in Brain Fry's page (Cmd+L, W, F) go to the page, with the key of the page that had the focus (`browser` message `key`, dispatched as a keydown). Cmd+R, Cmd+[ / ] and Cmd+= / - / 0 (zoom) act on the page directly. The rest (copy, paste, undo) stays with the page. Cmd+W, forwarded, closes the tab on screen in the pane that I use, like any Cmd+W ("2a, 3a, 3b").
- Focus: a tab that I open or click gets the keyboard at its next placement (`browser.place` with `focus`). A view that hides gives the keyboard back to Brain Fry's page.
- `window.open`, `target=_blank` and Cmd+click on a link open a tab of the same task (Cmd+click: in the background). A popup's view is made with the configuration that WebKit passes, so `window.opener` works (OAuth logins). `window.close()` closes the tab. Links with another scheme (`mailto:`, `zoommtg:`) go to their app.
- Closing a tab ends its page. Found by Renaud on 2026-11-10: a closed YouTube tab kept its sound. Removing the view from the window is not enough while something holds it, and the tab held itself: page --> its WebKit extension tab --> page, a cycle through an Objective-C object that PyObjC cannot collect. The close now pauses the media, closes fullscreen and picture in picture, cuts the cycle, and closes the page (`_close`, WebKit SPI). Tested in `host_check.py`: with a view still held, the old steps left a looping sound playing, the new close stops it. A hidden tab (another tab or task) plays on, as a background tab in any browser.
- Downloads (`Content-Disposition: attachment`, a type that WebKit cannot show, `<a download>`) go to `~/Downloads` under a free name (`a (1).pdf`), with a toast "Show in Finder". JS alert, confirm, prompt and file pickers are native panels. Right click --> Inspect Element, and Safari's Web Inspector, work in the pages.
- Per task: the URLs of a task's browser tabs are saved in its task file (`browser_tabs`). When the task is selected for the first time after a start, the tabs come back, and each loads its page when it is first shown. A task without file tabs shows its first browser tab.
- Links in the terminals: Cmd+click on a URL in 4a or 3b opens it in a browser tab of the task, as in VS Code's terminal (a plain click selects text). Also for OSC 8 links. A URL that wraps onto the next line is cut at the line end. Until 2026-11-15, a URL after an emoji was shifted by one column per emoji (the link took the index in the text as the column), so a Cmd+click on its last characters missed; it now takes the columns from the cells (`termLineCells`), as the plain paths do.
- File links in 4a (Renaud, 2026-11-10: "url and md is good enough"; built the same day). He missed one thing from the VS Code panel: a click on a link opens the document. The `claude` PTYs get `FORCE_HYPERLINK=1` (`_start_pty`, not the shells of 3b, not the digest), so that Claude Code writes its links as OSC 8. Cmd+click, as for URLs: an http(s) target opens a browser tab, as before; a `file:` target or one without a scheme is a file: the backend (`fs.link`, `files.link_target`) resolves it against the task's cwd (the project if the cwd is gone; an absolute path as it is; `file://` and `%20` decoded), and `#L<n>`, `#L<n>-<m>` or `#L<n>-L<m>` opens it in 3a at line n, centred (`openLink`, `goToLine` in files.js). A file that does not exist, or a folder: a toast. A file outside the project opens by its absolute path since 2026-11-14 (2a, 3a, 3b, Files outside the project; until then a toast). Another scheme (`mailto:`, `vscode:`): ignored, so that a link in a reply cannot start an app. The handler needs `allowNonHttpProtocols: true`: without it, xterm.js's OscLinkProvider drops every target that `new URL()` does not parse as http(s), `hello.py` included. The same handler serves 3b, where a `file://` link that a program prints (`ls --hyperlink`) opens too; a relative one is resolved against the task's cwd, not the shell's.
- Plain paths in the terminals (Renaud, 2026-11-15: "when claude writes smth like 'I wrote the spec for WP1 at docs/work-packages/WP1-server-on-steacher1.md' ... i would like a way to open this link in the editor"). Claude Code links only Markdown links (below), and Claude writes most paths as plain text, so Cmd+click did nothing on them. Now, as in VS Code's terminal, a word of a line that has a `/` or ends in an extension (`.md`, `.py:42`, `.py:42:7`, `.py:42-50`; a leading `@` and a trailing `.,:;!` dropped; no `://`) is a candidate, and the backend (`fs.paths`, `files.text_paths`) says which ones are files: relative to the task's cwd (in 3b: the terminal tab's folder after any `cd`, `PtySession.folder`), then to the project; `~` expanded; an absolute path as it is. Only those are links (underlined on hover), so `e.g`, `term.options` or `missing.py` never are. Cmd+click opens the file in 3a as a file link does (`openLinkAt`, files.js), at its line. The answers are kept in the page (`pathSeen`, per task, shell and word): a line whose words are all known answers at once, which the Cmd+press of the fullscreen mode needs (its lookup, `linkAt`, is synchronous; the mouse move before the press asks first), and a word known for more than 5 s is asked again after that answer. A path that the terminal wraps onto the next row is one link (`termWrappedLine`); a path that the program breaks with a new line is none. Columns come from the cells (`termLineCells`), so an emoji before a path does not shift its link. Not linked: names without `/` or extension (`Makefile`), paths with spaces, `#L` fragments in plain text, a bare name that is neither in the cwd nor at the project root (`app.js` for `src/brain_fry/web/app.js`).
- Tested on 2026-11-15: `test_text_paths_finds_the_plain_paths_of_a_terminal_line`, `fs.paths` in `test_file_commands`, and a sandbox selftest (window covered) whose fake `claude` prints Renaud's sentence, an emoji line and `@notes/todo.md:`: the providers report exactly the three files (the WP1 path wrapped over two rows as one link), each range covers its text, a plain click opens nothing, Cmd+click on `src/a.py:42` opens it at line 42, a mouse move then a Cmd+click through xterm.js's own path opens `notes/todo.md`, and with mouse tracking on, a move then a Cmd+press opens the WP1 file; no toast.
- Tested on 2026-11-10: unit tests (`test_link_target_*`, `fs.link` and `HYPERLINK=1` in the backend tests) and a sandbox selftest whose fake `claude` prints six OSC 8 links (window visible): xterm.js's providers report all six targets, a plain click does nothing, Cmd+click on `a.py#L150` with the cwd in `src/` opens `src/a.py` at line 150 (lines 104 to 196 on screen), `file://.../README.md` opens it, `mailto:` does nothing, `missing.py` and `/etc/hosts` give a toast, a second click on an open tab jumps again. Not seen in the selftest: whether the editor takes the keyboard after a real click (JS cannot give it the focus there). Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted (its sessions end with it and are resumed with the new environment).
- Side effect: `FORCE_HYPERLINK` reaches the commands that Claude runs with its Bash tool (checked with `printenv`, `claude -p`, 2026-11-10). A command that follows it (Node tools that use `supports-hyperlinks` or `terminal-link`) writes OSC 8 escape sequences into the output that Claude reads. Harmless as far as known. If it becomes a problem: the `hyperlinks` config value that Claude Code reads first (see below) might switch links on without the variable; not explored.
- What Claude Code links, tested on 2026-11-10 (2.1.322, one Haiku turn in a PTY set up like Brain Fry's: the same variables dropped, `TERM=xterm-256color`, the queries that xterm.js answers answered; script in the session's scratchpad, not kept). With `FORCE_HYPERLINK=1`: Markdown links (`[example docs](https://...)` --> the text "example docs", `[hello.py:2](hello.py#L2)` --> "hello.py:2", the target as written: relative, no `file://`) and bare URLs. Not linked: plain `hello.py:2`, the same in backticks, an absolute path with `:2`, the Read tool's header. Without it: no OSC 8 at all, and a Markdown link shows as `text (target)` (as `text` alone when both are equal). An OSC 8 link is not cut by the wrap, since the target travels in the escape sequence. So file links appear only when Claude writes a Markdown link; the CLI tells it to write `path:line`, which stays plain. Renaud accepts that.
- How Claude Code decides (the binary, 2.1.322): the `hyperlinks` config value, then `FORCE_HYPERLINK` (any value but `0`), then the package `supports-hyperlinks`, then `TERM_PROGRAM` in a list (ghostty, Hyper, kitty, alacritty, iTerm.app, iTerm2, WarpTerminal), `TERMINAL_EMULATOR` JetBrains, `WT_SESSION`, tmux 3.4+, `LC_TERMINAL`, a `TERM` that contains kitty. Brain Fry drops `TERM_PROGRAM` (`CHILD_ENV_DROP`), so nothing matches today. At start, Claude Code also sends XTVERSION (`ESC[>0q`) and treats a reply that starts with `xterm.js` as VS Code's terminal, but that reply does not count for links, and the vendored xterm.js does not answer XTVERSION.
- Links in Claude Code's fullscreen mode (Renaud's `"tui": "fullscreen"` in `~/.claude/settings.json`), found on 2026-11-13: Cmd+click opened nothing (a Markdown link in a reply of the adele task). Seen in a PTY (2.1.323): fullscreen still writes OSC 8 links (`ESC]8;id=...;target`), switches to the alternate screen and turns on mouse tracking (`?1000h ?1002h ?1003h ?1006h`). xterm.js then sends the press to Claude Code, which redraws the row, and xterm.js builds a new link object for it; its Linkifier opens a link only when the link at mouseup is the same object as at mousedown, so nothing opens. Claude Code itself opens a clicked link (with `open`, the system browser) only when the SGR report has the Alt or Ctrl bit, or under ghostty or Warp, never under `TERM_PROGRAM=vscode`; xterm.js never puts Cmd into a mouse report. Fix (`browserTermLinks`, browser.js): while `term.modes.mouseTrackingMode` is not `none`, a Cmd+mousedown is caught in the capture phase on `term.element`, the link under the pointer is looked up through the link providers (internal `_linkProviderService`, as in the tests), it opens through Brain Fry's handler, and the press and its release are stopped, so they never reach the program. A plain click, or a Cmd+click that is not on a link, goes to the program as before. Without mouse tracking, nothing changes (xterm.js's own path). Also helps 3b (vim, htop). Side effect left alone: an Option+click on a link in fullscreen makes Claude Code open it in the system browser, not in 3a.
- Tested on 2026-11-13: a sandbox selftest whose fake `claude` turns on the same mouse modes and redraws the link row on every input byte (it logs what it gets). Before the fix, Cmd+click opened nothing; after, Cmd+click on the web link opens a browser tab, on `a.py#L1` opens the file, and the fake receives no press; Cmd+click on plain text and a plain click on a link reach it. The control without mouse tracking opens each link once.
- The user agent ends with Safari's `Version/<macOS> Safari/605.1.15`, so sites treat the pages as Safari.
- Not logged: the URLs (browsing history). Changes of `browser_tabs` are logged as counts (`browser_tabs 1 -> 2`); until 2026-11-10 they were logged with their URLs. bf.log has the tabs opened and closed, uBlock's load, the session cookies restored and saved, and each copy of the Firefox cookies (counts and profile).
- PyObjC: the Objective-C classes of `browser.py` implement WebKit's protocols without `protocols=[...]`. With it, PyObjC takes the signatures from the runtime protocol, which does not describe blocks, and calling a `decisionHandler` crashed the app ("cannot call block without a signature"). The protocols are added afterwards through the runtime. PyObjC 13's metadata lacks `presentActionPopup`: registered by hand.
- Tests: `tests/test_browser.py` (installer, task field, backend commands, helpers). `tests/window/browser/host_check.py` (in `spikes/` until 2026-11-11) runs `BrowserHost` in a real window against a local site (`site.py`): placement, keys, popups, `window.close()`, a download, uBlock's popup, session cookies, the sound of a closed tab, the copy of Firefox cookies from a fake profile (32/32 on 2026-11-10). `bf --selftest` draws the browser views into its screenshot.
