- Not tested yet: Google sign-in (Google blocks embedded browsers and forbids faking the user agent; saved cookies may still keep me logged in), real sites with uBlock (the tests use only the local site: no network), trackpad gestures (swipe back and forward, pinch), fullscreen video, dragging the splitters by hand.
- Not built: password autofill (macOS Passwords does not fill in another app's web view), passkeys (they need an Apple entitlement that a Python app cannot get), other extensions, bookmarks, a history view, private tabs.

## 1b: thread navigation

- 1b is closed by default (Renaud, 2026-11-15): only its header shows. A click on the header opens or closes the list, as a section of VS Code's side bar, with a chevron in the left margin. The state is a window setting (`threadsOpen` in `window.json`), the same for every task.
- Clicking a thread in 1b opens it as a read-only tab in 3a: digest, question, prompt and reply as Markdown, read from the task file (`thread.get`). Thread tabs are not saved with the task's open files.
- The tab's "Find in conversation" button scrolls 4a to the thread: xterm.js's search add-on looks backward in the scrollback for the first 30 characters of the prompt (short, because the terminal may wrap a long line). Limits: the scrollback size (50,000 lines), and Claude Code re-renders the history on resume.
- Tested with a fake session that prints a conversation. Not tested yet with real Claude Code, which draws the prompt in its own way.

## Modes

- No text when a panel or a list is empty (Renaud, 2026-11-11: "I don't want any text that appears when a panel is empty"): no "Loading...", "Starting...", "Select a file.", "None.", "No change.", "Empty folder.", "No task selected.", "No branch yet.", "No project yet" in the pickers, nor a key-hint row. Kept: toasts (they answer an action), errors and "not in a git repository" in Review mode, the notes of a diff that cannot be shown (binary, too big, a mode change), and the two overlays of 4a that carry a button ("No live session" + Resume, "No open task in ..." + New task).
- One key toggles Organize and Dev mode. A third mode, Review (a git client, Cmd+Shift+G), was built on 2026-11-10: see Review mode.
- Organize mode: the board takes the whole window. 4a is hidden; its terminal keeps running.
- Enter on a card switches to Dev mode with that task selected.
- Dev mode has no list of all tasks. A task that is not in the inbox is reached through the board, or, in the working column, with "All working" on (below).
- The side panel (1) holds only Inbox and Threads (Renaud, 2026-11-10). No Archive section: archived tasks are reached through the board (H). No row of keys at its bottom ("just disturbing, not helping"). An empty inbox shows its heading and nothing below it. A selected task without a thread or notes shows nothing under Threads. Threads is hidden while no task is selected, which an empty inbox implies (the selected task stays at the top of 1a even when it is not in the inbox).
- The selected task stays visible at the top of 1a when it is not in the inbox (for example after Cmd+Up back to a task that I answered), until I select another one. After an answer or an archive, Brain Fry moves on to the next item (see Auto-advance).
- An inbox row shows its title on two lines at most, then an ellipsis (Renaud, 2026-11-13: "it would help me read a little more"; it was one line). Same CSS as the title of a card (`-webkit-line-clamp: 2`), plus `overflow-wrap: anywhere` for a long path with no space. Cost: a row with a long title is one line taller.
- Cmd+J I (Renaud, 2026-11-13: "select inbox item N"): each row of 1a on screen gets a key in a small blue label, 1 to 9 then a to z from the top, as Cmd+J E in the explorer (same keys `HINT_KEYS`, same look). Pinned rows and the selected task kept at the top of 1a get one too. Typing a label selects that task, and the keyboard goes to its Claude Code (4a). Dev mode only (Cmd+J does nothing in Organize and Review mode). Column 1 hidden: Cmd+J I shows it again. 4a hidden: the pick shows it again, since the keyboard must not go to a hidden terminal (as Cmd+J C). The labels stay until a key, a click, or the window losing the keyboard; Esc, another key, or a key with no row: nothing. Cmd held through I and the label works the same.
  - The label sits before the title, inline: the first line of the title moves right by its width while the labels show. Renaud chose it from a local mockup of four placements (over the status dot, which Claude had built first; before the title; at the end of the project line; at the right edge of the first line).
  - Decided by Claude: a label stays on its task while the rows move (a new reply or a block reorders the inbox every 0.5 s): Claude raised the risk that a label read as "3" selects another task once a row moves, Renaud accepted the fix. A task that comes in while the labels show gets none; a labelled task that leaves the inbox loses its label, and its key does nothing. The keyboard goes to 4a at once (`term.focus()` right after `select`), as Cmd+J C: `showTerm` focuses in an animation frame, which a covered window never runs.
  - Discussed: Renaud first proposed Cmd+I. Claude objected: I was ruled out for Cmd+J on 2026-11-11 (CodeMirror's Cmd+I selects the enclosing syntax node, web pages use it for italic, and `browser.py` leaves it to the page), and Cmd+J already means "the keyboard to a place". Claude also said that picking by label weakens the queue (the inbox order is the priority); Renaud: "when the queue has more than one item, I can select what I want to work on next". The gain over Cmd+Down: Cmd+Down selects every task on the way, so each is marked read, and one without a process is resumed.
  - Code: `startInboxHints`, `drawInboxHints` (called by `renderInbox`, whose rows are rebuilt at every push), `inboxHintKey` in `app.js`; the chord in `grid.js` (`chordKey`, and `LABELS`: the click and blur listeners of both kinds of labels, moved there from `explorer.js`); `.inbox-hint` in `app.css`. Tested with a sandbox selftest (4 tasks, one blocked, one pinned): two-line titles (a 3-line title clamped to 2), the labels 1 to 4, kept on their tasks through a render, 3 selects the third row with the keyboard in 4a, z (no row), Esc, a click, Cmd+J Cmd+I Cmd+1, column 1 hidden then shown with its labels, 4a hidden then shown with the keyboard in it (the same task and another), Organize and Review mode (no labels). The window was covered during the last runs. Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- Keys in Dev mode: Cmd+Up/Down (previous/next in the inbox), Cmd+Option+1 to 4 (show or hide a column, see Layout of Dev mode), Cmd+Option+5 (columns 1 and 4a together), Cmd+E (archive), Cmd+Shift+D (fork), Cmd+T (new task: pick a project), Cmd+N then T, B or F (create in a pane, see the grid), Cmd+N then O (copy a tab of another task), Cmd+J then 1-4, C, E or I (the keyboard to a pane, to Claude Code, to a labelled row of the explorer, to the Claude Code of a labelled inbox row), Cmd+P (switch project, see Project dropdown; also in Organize mode), Cmd+B (Organize mode). The terminal never receives these keys.
- Keys in Organize mode are single letters, because no terminal is on screen: arrows move the cursor between cards, Shift+Left/Right moves the card to the next prio column, Shift+Up/Down moves it one place up or down in its column (see Status, inbox, board, "Order in a column"), Enter opens it in Dev mode, 1 to 9 open a card of the working column, E archive, F fork, N new task (a card, not started: see below), H shows or hides later / more later / never / archived, / puts the cursor in the search (below), Esc or Cmd+B back to Dev. A double-click on a card opens it in Dev mode; dragging a card to another column changes its prio.
- Right-click menu of a card (Renaud, 2026-11-14: "right-click on a task and either archive it or delete it"): two items, Archive (Unarchive on an archived card, with its key E shown) and Delete. It is the menu of the explorer (`#ctx`, `openCtx` in `explorer.js`); the cursor moves to the card first, as VS Code's explorer selects the row. While it is open, Esc closes it and no other key acts on the board (the board's keys run before the menu's Esc listener, so Esc would also have left Organize mode).
  - Delete moves the task file, open or archived, to the Trash of macOS (`files._move_to_trash`, as the explorer's Delete), after a `confirm()` that names the task and says "Its Claude Code session ends." when the app runs one. Finder's Put Back brings the file back (named `<session id>.md`), and the task comes back at the next read. Claude Code's transcript stays (Claude Code deletes it after 30 days).
  - The command `delete` (`core.py`) first sends SIGHUP to the task's `claude` and to its terminal tabs of column 3, then `actions.delete` trashes the file under `taskfile.LOCK` (an event applied meanwhile would write it again), and clears the backend's selection when it was that task. The exits find no task to mark (`actions.NotFound`, ignored there).
  - A Stop or a Notification already on its way would import the task again from its transcript (a session that started outside Brain Fry). So `events.FORGOTTEN` holds the deleted sessions for the rest of the run, and their events are ignored when no file exists; a file put back from the Trash takes its events again. A SessionEnd alone never created a task.
  - Front end (`deleteTask` in `app.js`): the card leaves at once (no flash before the state push), the cursor moves to the next card of its column, or to the one above. A deleted task that was selected or on the idle screen: the idle screen shows the task opened last, as at start. Its terminal of 4a is disposed.
  - Decided by Claude: Trash plus a confirm (the explorer's pattern), not an Undo toast; no key for Delete (none was asked). The inbox got its own Delete on 2026-11-15 (below). Tests: `test_delete_moves_the_task_file_to_the_trash`, `test_a_deleted_task_comes_back_only_from_the_trash`, `test_delete_ends_the_session_and_the_terminal_tabs`, and a sandbox selftest (2026-11-14, window covered: `select` waits for an animation frame, so check.js sent the select command itself): the menu's items on an open and an archived card, Esc keeps Organize mode, Archive and Unarchive, a declined confirm keeps the task, Delete of an archived card and of the selected task with a live session (SIGHUP in bf.log, the cursor on the next card, the idle screen on the task opened last).
- Right-click menu of an inbox row (Renaud, 2026-11-15: "in column 1, ability to right click to 1 close 2 archive 3 restart CC session <-- possible?", then "1 i meant: delete"): Archive (Unarchive on the archived task kept at the top of 1a), Restart Claude Code, Delete. The menu of the explorer (`#ctx`, `openCtx`); code in `app.js` (the `#inbox` contextmenu listener, `archiveTask`, `restartTask`, `deleteTask`).
  - The right-clicked row is not selected (unlike the board's cursor and VS Code's explorer): selecting marks the task read and starts its session. ⌘E shows next to Archive on the selected task's row only, since the key acts on the selected task.
  - Archive does what Cmd+E does (`archiveTask`, now shared with `act`): the selected task moves on to the next inbox item at once; another row just leaves the inbox. Delete is the board's (`deleteTask`: Trash, confirm); in Dev mode, deleting the selected task selects the next inbox item, as an archive does (before, it showed the idle screen until the next state push selected the first item).
  - Restart Claude Code: the command `restart` (`core.py`) ends the task's `claude` and starts `claude --resume <id>` (`_resume_argv`, as a select) in the same PTY key, with the next generation: Claude Code reads CLAUDE.md, the settings, hooks, MCP servers and skills again, or picks up a new version. The new process starts in the exit callback itself (`Core._start_again`, beside `_retry_without_prefill`), so no state push shows the task without a session and 4a never flashes "No live session"; 4a's terminal follows the new generation as after Resume. The end is `PtySession.kill` in a thread: SIGHUP, then SIGTERM and SIGKILL 3 s apart, so a hung session restarts too and the commands of the connection do not wait. The SessionEnd hook of the old process still comes: `live` off (harmless while the app runs the PTY), and a turn that was running goes to the inbox (`actions.end_session`), which is true. A delete on the way cancels the restart (`Core.restarting`).
  - Decided by Claude: Restart is disabled on a row whose session the app does not run (a select resumes it anyway); a confirm only for a working or blocked task ("Its turn is cut off."), none for a waiting one, which loses nothing. Same order as the board's menu, Restart between Archive and Delete, so Delete stays last. No key (none asked).
  - Tests: `test_restart_starts_claude_again_on_the_same_conversation` (refused without a session, `--resume`, no state without a session in between), `test_restart_ends_a_session_that_ignores_sighup` (SIGTERM after 3 s), and a sandbox selftest (2026-11-15, window covered for part of the run; `window.request` stubbed for Delete, so no file went to the Trash): the menu of a row without a session (Restart disabled, no ⌘E) and of the selected row, the row not selected nor marked read, Esc, the confirm of a working task declined (no request), a restart (new generation in the state and in 4a's terminal, "FAKE CLAUDE --resume <id>" on screen, no overlay drawn meanwhile), Archive of another row (the selection stays), Delete of the selected task (the next inbox item selected). Not tried with a real right-click or the real `claude`.
- Rename in the right-click menu of an inbox row (Renaud, 2026-11-15: "right click on a task in the inbox "Rename" to edit the summary shown on the task"). Claude read "the summary" as the row's title (the bold lines, Sonnet's title), not its digest line. The title of the row becomes a name field with the whole title selected, the explorer's rename (`.inbox-rename`, the look of `.tree-rename`): Enter or a click elsewhere keeps the text, Esc changes nothing. The command `rename` (`actions.rename`) sets the title and the short title (the card's heading); the digest keeps them (Digest, "Title"). Code: `renameTask` in `app.js`; `S.inboxRename` stops `renderInbox` from drawing the rows again while the field is open (a state push would take the field away), and a click in the field selects nothing.
  - Decided by Claude: the order of the menu is Archive, Restart Claude Code, Rename, Delete (VS Code puts Rename just before Delete). An empty text or the same title: nothing is sent. The new title shows at once, before the state push. After Enter or Esc the keyboard goes back to the selected task's Claude Code (4a); after a click elsewhere it stays where the click put it. No key (none asked), not on the board's cards (not asked). The title is logged with the command, as the title of a new card (`_describe`): a title is not a prompt.
  - Tested with a sandbox selftest (2026-11-15, window covered): the menu, the field (focused, all selected), the field kept through two state pushes, Enter (the row at once, then title and short title in the file), Esc, an empty text, a blur on a row that is not selected.
- "All working" in the heading of 1a (Renaud, 2026-11-15: "a toggle button in the inbox, to show all tasks in working stage"). On, 1a shows every task of the working column that is not archived, not only the inbox: the inbox first in its order, then the others, red first, then in the order of the board (`views.working_list`, sent as `working` in the state beside `inbox`). The rows that are not in the inbox look like the inbox rows (only the selected task kept at the top is dimmed, `current`). The switch is an icon (three lines, its tooltip says "All working") at the right end of the heading, gray pressed when on (the look of Wrap), remembered by the window (`inboxAll` in `window.json`). Renaud chose the icon and the plain rows from a local mockup of four looks (a text button "All working", built first; the icon; two words "Inbox (1) Working (3)" in the heading; a dim word) and three row styles (dimmed, built first; plain; a thin rule before them). After a click the keyboard goes back to the selected task's Claude Code, as after the auto-advance switch.
  - What follows the rows on screen: Cmd+Up/Down (`visibleInbox`), the labels of Cmd+J I, the right-click menu. What still takes only a task that needs me: auto-advance, an arrival, the count in the heading. An archive or a delete takes the row out of both lists before the push (`dropRow`).
  - Decided by Claude: the order of the rows that are not in the inbox; no key (none asked). The heading still says Inbox.
  - Tested: `test_working_list_is_the_inbox_then_the_rest_of_the_working_column` and the selftest above: the rows off and on (a pinned task, a working task, a task in planning with a reply and an archived task left out), Cmd+Down in the order on screen, three labels of Cmd+J I, off again, the snapshot of the heading.
- Search on the board (approved by Renaud on 2026-11-13: "8 but kiss"; built 2026-11-14). Why: 122 archived tasks after 4 days, and H plus reading gray cards was the only way back to an old task.
  - One text field at the left end of the board bar, before the H checkbox (the Move button stays at the right end): 260 px wide, as tall as the buttons, the look of the title field of the plan dialog (dark field, thin gray border, a lighter border while it has the keyboard), placeholder "Search all tasks (/)". Claude chose the placement without a mockup; Renaud has not seen it yet.
  - Typing filters the cards of every column: a card stays when every word of the text occurs, ignoring case, in what the state holds for the task (title, short title, project name, digest, notes, and the digest and first prompt line of each thread; `searchText` in `app.js`). No backend search, no index. An empty result shows the columns and nothing in them.
  - While the field has text, the hidden columns and the archived tasks show too, whatever H says (the checkbox does not change), and the front end asks the backend for the archived tasks (`archive.show`, see App).
  - `/` puts the cursor in the field, only when the board has the keyboard: a dialog (the picker, the plan dialog, Show shortcuts) or another field takes the key first. Esc in the field clears it and gives the keyboard back to the board. Enter gives the keyboard to the board with the cursor on the first card that matches (the first card of the first column that has one), where Enter opens it as usual. The digits 1 to 9 follow the filtered working column, as they follow H.
  - Decided by Claude: the text stays when I leave the board (Enter on a card, Cmd+B) and is there when I come back; it lives in memory only (gone at a restart). Leaving Organize mode takes the keyboard out of the field: WebKit keeps the focus in a hidden field, and a field keeps its keys, so Cmd+B would not come back. Cmd keys typed in the field stay in the field, as in the other fields (Esc first).
  - Tested with a sandbox selftest (2026-11-14, window visible during the whole run): two projects, 4 open and 3 archived tasks written in the top folders. The start moved the 3 archived files; Dev mode held the 4 open tasks only; select, Cmd+E, Cmd+Up back to the archived task, Unarchive (the file moved back); on the board H showed the 3 archived cards and the state had them, H off removed them; `/` gave the field the keyboard; a word found an archived card and a card of a hidden column with H off; two words, in another case, kept one card; no match left empty columns; Esc cleared the field and the state was lean again; `/` with the picker open did nothing; a search found an archived card by its notes, Enter went to it, Enter opened it in Dev mode with its session, Unarchive moved the file back; the text was still there back on the board. Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted (which also runs the migration).
- The planning column is two columns wide, with two cards per row (Renaud, 2026-11-11: "planning is a larger column, 2-tickets wide"), in reading order (left to right, then down, in the order of the column). CSS: `flex-grow: 2` and twice the width limits of a column, a grid of two in `.cards`. The arrows follow the screen (`perRow`, `boardMove` in `app.js`): in planning, Left/Right first go to the other card of the row, then to the next column; Up/Down move a whole row, and Down from a full last row stays, from a row above onto a half-full last row goes to its card. Across columns the cursor keeps its row: entering planning from the left lands on the row's left card, from the right on its right card. Checked with a sandbox selftest (widths 420 and 852 px, cards 413 px, a sequence of 14 arrows).
- 1 to 9 (Renaud, 2026-11-10): the digit n opens the n-th card of the working column in Dev mode, as Enter would (the cursor moves to it too). The first nine working cards show their digit, drawn as a key, left of the status color. The number is the position on screen right now (in the order of the column, archived cards after the open ones while they are shown), not a number fixed to the task: Renaud reads the board, "this one, at position 2", and types 2. A digit beyond the last card does nothing. Only the working column has numbers. Toggle: Cmd+B, unchanged. Code: `openWorking` in `app.js`. Rejected: a fixed number per task (a slot kept while the task stays in working), and Cmd+1 to Cmd+9 in both modes.
- A double-click is handled as the second click (`detail` 2), not with a `dblclick` listener. Each click re-renders the board, so the card of the second click is already detached when WebKit sends it the `dblclick`, and the event never reaches the document (bug seen by Renaud on 2026-11-10, reproduced with `bf --selftest`). The same applies to any list that a click re-renders.
- Moving a card into a hidden column shows the hidden columns, so that the card stays in view. Whether they are shown is remembered by the window (`showHidden` in `window.json`, see App, "Window settings"), not in a task file.
- Task menu (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-15: "you can retire all the buttons ... The goal is to clean that up"): the header has no button left of its own, only the logo, the burger menu, the project dropdown and Dev / Organize / Review. Pin, Archive, Fork, "+ New task" and the switch Auto-advance moved to:
  - Task in the menu bar, after View (`app/taskmenu.py`): New Task (as Cmd+T: the picker), then Archive, Fork, Pin, then Auto-advance with a check mark. Each item calls `taskMenu(name)` in the page. The task is the one that Cmd+E and Cmd+Shift+D act on: the selected task, or the card under the cursor in Organize mode (`targetId`). Titles follow it (Unarchive on an archived task, Unpin on a pinned one); Archive, Fork and Pin are grayed with no task, Pin on an archived task. pywebview's menus have fixed titles, no check mark and no disabled item: the page sends what the menu shows (`menu.task`, at each change, `syncTaskMenu`), and `taskmenu.apply` sets it on the NSMenuItems, found by their tags; `taskmenu.prepare` runs inside pywebview's builder (the patch that moves File). If pywebview changes, the items stay plain. No key equivalents, as in File; the right-click menus show the keys.
  - The right-click menu of an inbox row: Archive, Fork, Pin (Unpin), a line, then Restart Claude Code, Rename, Delete. ⌘E and ⇧⌘D show on the selected task's row only. The right-click menu of a card: Archive, Fork, Pin (Unpin), a line, Delete, with E and F (the keys of Organize mode, which act on the card under the cursor, where the menu moves it).
  - "AA" in the heading of 1a, left of the All working icon, same look (gray, pressed when on): auto-advance on and off, as Task > Auto-advance. Renaud picked it (look 4b) from a mockup of five looks: nothing in the heading (the menu only), "AA", an arrow to the next item (recommended by Claude: "AA" looks like "Aa", match case, of the explorer search), a circle arrow, and one menu button holding both switches.
  - Decided by Claude: Fork and Pin in the card menu too (the same actions in every menu); the Task menu after View (where pywebview puts it; between Edit and View would need another patch); Fork from a row that is not selected forks that task, and the fork is selected as usual. Tested: `tests/test_task_menu.py` and a sandbox selftest (the header, AA and its stored value, both right-click menus, Unpin from a row, each Task item through `taskMenu`, the menu bar printout for an archived task: `Task (New Task, Unarchive, Fork, Pin [off], ✓ Auto-advance)`). A real click on the menu bar is not tested.
- No dot, title, project, status or prio dropdown in the header (Renaud, 2026-11-10: they repeated the row of 1a). The prio changes only in Organize mode.
- In Organize mode, Archive, Fork and Pin apply to the card under the cursor. A forked task switches to Dev mode, since its session waits for a prompt.
- A new task created in Organize mode is only a card: no session starts (Renaud, 2026-11-10: "I'm just organizing"). In Dev mode a new task still starts its session at once. The mode decides, whatever the way in (N, Cmd+T, Task > New Task). Flow: the project picker (its header says "on the board, not started"), then a dialog with a title and notes (Renaud: "I need more than the title"). Enter in the title moves to the notes, Cmd+Enter creates (from either field), Esc or a click outside closes. Command `plan` (`actions.plan_task`).
  - The card lands in the column of the card under the cursor (working on an empty board), and the cursor moves to it. The dialog's header names the project and the column.
  - The title is mine: `title` and `short_title`, as after a rename, so the digest keeps it. Status waiting (green, as a new task of Dev mode), `live` off, not in the inbox, not `opened`, not selected.
  - No tabs are copied: the previous task at planning time says nothing about the task that I will start days later. Opening the card in Dev mode starts `claude --session-id <id>` (`_resume_argv`: no thread, no parent), with empty tabs, and my title and notes in Claude Code's input box (below).
  - The title is required (Cmd+Enter without one puts the cursor in it); the notes are optional and free text.
  - A dialog closed without creating keeps its text, in memory, and shows it again at the next N (a long note is not lost to a reflex Esc). Creating clears it; a failed create keeps it.
  - The notes are kept in the task file, in a `# Notes` section before `# Digest` (blockquote lines, like every free text), and never change after. The card shows them in place of the digest until the first digest arrives (the tooltip holds all of it). In Dev mode, 1b shows them while the task has no thread, that is, while I write the first prompt. bf.log gets their length only, as for a prompt.
  - Not built: editing the notes after creation, and giving the notes to the digest.
  - A planned card starts with its notes (approved by Renaud, built on 2026-11-14). When the session of a task with no thread and no parent starts, Claude Code's input box holds my title, a blank line and my notes, not sent: I read them, edit them and press Enter myself. The rule is stateless: each start fills the box again (`select` and `resume`, both through `_resume_argv`) until the first prompt makes a thread. My edits in a box that I did not send are lost when the session ends. A task of Dev mode has no title and no notes before its first turn, so its box stays empty; one that I renamed before its first prompt gets its title, since the title is mine too. A fork gets nothing: it starts from its parent's conversation. A NUL character is removed (a command line cannot hold it). Code: `Core._prefill` and `Core._resume_argv` in `app/core.py`. Tests: `tests/test_prefill.py` (the rule, the arguments that a fake `claude` gets, the log, the retry below). Not tried in the window yet: select a planned card after a restart of the app.
    - How: Claude Code has a hidden option, `--prefill <text>`, "Pre-fill the prompt input with text without submitting it" (`.hideHelp()`, so not in `claude --help`). It serves Claude Code's deep links. Found on 2026-11-14 by scanning the binary of 2.1.325 for `"--prefill"`. It works with `--session-id`. Brain Fry passes `--prefill=<text>`: with the `=`, a text that starts with `-` stays the value of the option.
    - What Claude Code does with it (probed with the real `claude` 2.1.325 in a PTY): the box shows the text as plain text, new lines, accents and quotes included. A title or a line that starts with `/`, `!` or `#`, and an `@` inside, change nothing: no bash mode, no command menu. A line under the box says "Pre-filled prompt · review before pressing Enter"; for a long text, "Pre-filled prompt (2,452 chars) · scroll to review it all before pressing Enter". A 40-line note stays plain lines, and its top goes to the scrollback. The text survives the trust dialog of a new folder: the Enter that answers the dialog does not reach the box. No readiness rule is needed, since Claude Code fills its box itself at start. One quirk: when the text ends with a word that starts with `@`, the cursor sits after it, so the list of file suggestions opens under the box, as if I had typed that word.
    - Rejected: a bracketed paste once Claude Code is ready (tested): the box shows "[Pasted text #1 +6 lines]" for the 5-line note already, which I can neither read nor edit; the 40-line note gave "+17 lines". Typed characters (not tried, read in the source): Claude Code keeps the keys that arrive before its prompt is up in a buffer of its own, where ESC CR is dropped (the new lines are lost) and a chunk made of CR only sends a one-line text; once the prompt is up, an `@` opens a menu that may take the CR of ESC CR. The prompt as an argument of `claude` is sent at once.
    - Risk: a hidden option may go in a later version. Claude Code then stops at once with "error: unknown option '--prefill=...'" (checked with an unknown option). Brain Fry looks for that text in the output of a session that had a prefill when it ends, and then starts the session again without it, once: an empty box and a warning in bf.log, rather than a card that never starts (`Core._retry_without_prefill`; `PtySession` keeps its argv, folder and size for that).
    - Privacy: bf.log gets `--prefill=<N chars>` in place of the text (`ptys.loggable`). The text is on the command line of the `claude` process, so `ps` shows it while the session runs.
    - To probe it again: the recipe of Hooks ("To see what Claude Code writes to a terminal") with a fresh `--session-id`, `--settings '{"disableAllHooks": true}'` and `BRAIN_FRY_INTERNAL=1`, and pyte's `HistoryScreen` (`uv run --with pyte`, nothing added to pyproject.toml) to read the box and the scrollback. After SIGTERM, keep reading the PTY: the exit of `claude` waits until its output is read (an undrained probe waited more than 6 s). None of the probes left a transcript.
- Two guards keep keys from reaching a hidden terminal: entering Organize mode takes the focus out of the terminal, and the terminals drop their input while the mode is not Dev.
- The terminal is fitted only while 4a is on screen. In Organize mode 4a has no size, and fitting then would shrink the PTY and make Claude Code re-render.

## Project dropdown

Asked by Renaud on 2026-11-10. The header shows the logo, the burger menu (see Browser), then a dropdown of the registered projects (in place of the name "Brain Fry"), then Dev / Organize / Review, and nothing else since 2026-11-15 (see Modes, "Task menu").

- Picking a project switches to Dev mode and selects the task of that project that I opened last, with its file tabs, terminal tabs and session, as a click on the task would.
- "Opened" = selected in Dev mode. Each select writes `opened` (a timestamp) in the task's frontmatter. It does not change `updated`, so the board order stays, and it is not logged, because the select command is.
- A new or forked task gets `opened` at birth: the backend selects it, and the front end follows without sending a select. Before 2026-11-10 it stayed empty until I clicked the task, so the dropdown and the source of the next new task's tabs went to the older task.
- Archived tasks are never landed on (they are done). Tasks that were never opened (older than this feature) count by `updated`.
- A project without any task that is not archived: nothing is selected, and 4a says "No open task in <project>" with a button that starts a new task there. No session is started without that click.
- 2a and column 3 then show that project (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-11: "if no task is selected, but a project is selected, just open the file explorer of the selected project"). The front end keeps a work state of files.js under the id `project:<path>` (`wsId()` in `app.js`: the selected task, else this). The backend accepts that id in the `fs.*` commands and in `/file/` (the id URL-encoded), for a project registered in `projects.json` only (`Core._project_of`). The explorer works as for a task (folders, the right-click menu, its keys), and a file opens as a tab: text, image, PDF, and Cmd+N F (an untitled file, saved in the project). Every tab opened there is a project tab, kept in `<project>/.brain_fry/project.json` and shown by every task of the project (see Project tabs, built 2026-11-11; until then Cmd+N T and B showed a toast, and the tabs stayed in memory only, lost when the app ended). The open folders of 2a stay in memory only. No refresh on events (no task of the project sends any): Refresh does it. "New task in <project>" starts with the tabs of the previous task, as before; the project tabs show in it anyway. Tested with pytest (the `project:` id in `fs.*` and `/file/`, an unregistered path refused) and two sandbox selftests (window visible): the tree, a folder, a text file, an image, Cmd+N T and F, the calm photo and back, then "New task in demo". Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- The dropdown shows the project of the selected task (the task in 4a), or with nothing selected the project of the idle screen's task (see "The idle screen"), also in Organize mode, where the header's other parts follow the card under the cursor. It follows every selection, for example Cmd+Down onto a task of another project. Picking the project that is already shown does nothing (a native select sends no change for it).
- The inbox and the board are not filtered by the project.
- When two projects have the same folder name, the dropdown adds the parent folder.
- Cmd+P opens the same list in the picker of Cmd+T (Enter, arrows, digits 1 to 9), in Dev and Organize mode, with the highlight on the first project that is not the current one: Cmd+P, Enter goes back to the previous project. Each row marks the current project and says how long ago I last opened a task there. Picking a project does what the dropdown does (Renaud, 2026-11-10).
- Remove a project from the lists (Renaud, 2026-11-10: "keep it stupid simple"): each row of Cmd+P has a × at its right end, shown on hover and on the highlighted row, as the × of VS Code's recent folders. A click writes `"active": false` on the project in `projects.json` (command `project.deactivate`), and the project leaves the dropdown, Cmd+P and Cmd+T. `bf init` in the folder puts it back (it removes the field). The project stays registered: the hook still writes its events, the app still applies them, and its tasks stay in the inbox and on the board. The dropdown keeps a removed project while it is the current one (the selected task's project), so that the header shows it. Not in Cmd+T (a new task), no key, no confirmation (undoing is one `bf init`). Why `projects.json` and not a file in `<project>/.brain_fry/`: it is already the list of projects, read for every state push and watched for changes. Tested with pytest and two sandbox selftests (remove the highlighted row, remove the current project, Cmd+N without ×). Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- Add a project from the window (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-12: "a menu in File to Add new Project, same as uv run bf init"): File > Add New Project... opens macOS's folder dialog (pywebview's `create_file_dialog(FileDialog.FOLDER)`, which has a New Folder button; it opens in the folder that the panel used last). The menu's thread passes the chosen folder to the page (`menuAction('project', path)`), which sends `project.init` (`projects.init`, the code of `bf init`) and then switches to the project as Cmd+P does (`openProject`): a new project has no task, so 4a shows "No open task in <project>" and 2a its tree. A folder that is already a project: a toast "<name> is already a project", then the switch. A project removed with the × of Cmd+P comes back, as with `bf init`. Not a folder (the dialog never sends one) or the home folder: a toast with the error, nothing changes. Not in the dropdown or in Cmd+P (not asked), no key (a key of the menu bar never reaches the page). Tested: `test_add_project_from_the_file_menu` and a sandbox selftest (window visible) that calls `menuAction` directly: the menu bar, a new project (dropdown, 2a, "No open task"), an existing one (toast, switch), a file (toast). The dialog itself is not tested (modal, needs a click). Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- Order of the dropdown, Cmd+P and Cmd+T, one order since 2026-11-10 (Renaud): the most recently used project first, by the last `opened` of its tasks, archived ones included. Projects whose tasks were never opened follow, by their last `updated`. Projects without tasks keep the order of `projects.json` (`views.project_order`). The order comes from the task files alone. Since selecting a task writes `opened`, the current project is usually first, as in an app switcher.
  - Archived tasks (2026-11-14, with the archive folder, see Task file): the periodic reads no longer parse them, so each project's archive gives the `opened` and `updated` of its latest archived task (`taskfile.archive_last_use`, `actions.archive_times`): a regex on the head of each file (no YAML), redone only when the archive folder's time changed (every save renames a file in it). A project whose tasks are all archived keeps its place. Claude's brief said to use the newest time of the archive's files; Claude changed it, because a file is also written when its session ends (the quit of the app writes every live session's task, the close after 4 h too), hours after Renaud last used it: after each restart, the projects with archived tasks whose sessions still ran would have come first, and Cmd+P, Enter would no longer go back to the previous project. Tested: `test_the_archive_time_is_the_last_opened_not_the_file_time`.
- Before 2026-11-10 there were two orders, built by two agents on the same day: Cmd+N by my prompts in the last 2 days, Cmd+P and the dropdown by my work time per day over 7 days, both estimated from Claude Code's transcripts. Removed to simplify (about 170 lines; in git history).

## Status, inbox, board

- Status is automatic (hooks). The prio column changes only by my keystroke.
- Red and green both need me. Red sorts first, because the agent is stalled mid-work.
- `inbox` and `unread` are flags, not statuses. Stop and Notification set both. Selecting the task clears `unread` only (bold --> normal): the task stays in the inbox while I read it. `inbox` clears when I answer (UserPromptSubmit, or the dialog of a blocked task closes), or archive. Dismiss (out of the inbox, nothing to do, for FYI items) had a button and the keys Cmd+D and D until 2026-11-10, when Renaud removed them, with the backend command and his spec's mention of it. Inbox = tasks with `inbox` set, live or not, in the working column (below). Order: red, then time. This is the "close it" step of brain_fry.md.
- Only the working column is in the inbox (Renaud, 2026-11-11: "when a task is in planning, it does not appear in the inbox"). The hidden columns (later, more later, never) are out too: if planning waits, a column below it waits as well. So is next (below). Pinned or not: the column decides, the pin stays. The inbox view filters (`views.in_inbox`); events still set `inbox` and `unread` on a task in planning, so a task that moves back to working comes into the inbox with its reply, and in Dev mode with nothing selected it is selected at once (an arrival). A card outside working shows no "inbox" badge and no inbox line; its status color still says green or red. The idle close after 4 h still reads the flag, so a task in planning whose reply waits keeps its session. Until then the order had a prio step (red, then prio column, then time), now always equal, so removed.
- The next column (Renaud, 2026-11-13: "a 3rd visible column, like Next, between inbox and planning"): between working and planning, one column wide, always shown (not hidden by H). It is the coarse order that a manual order inside a column would give: "these come next". Out of the inbox like planning: a reply in a task of next waits with its green dot and comes into the inbox when the task moves back to working. Shift+Left/Right, dragging, N (a new card in the column under the cursor) and the column header all follow the list `PRIOS` (`taskfile.py`, `app.js`). No digits on its cards (only working has them). Asked first that day: a manual order inside a column (move a card above another). Replaced by next for now, since the columns hold few cards (5 open in working and 6 in planning that day) and an order is state to keep up (a place for every new or moved card). Asked again and built on 2026-11-16 (below, "Order in a column").
- "Move other projects to Next" (Renaud, 2026-11-11; to planning until 2026-11-13, then to next: those tasks are interrupted work, not plans): a button at the right end of the board bar (Organize mode only). Every task of the working column that is not in the current project (the project of the dropdown, `currentProject()`) moves to next, so that the inbox holds only that project. Archived ones moved too until 2026-11-14 ("all tasks"); since the archive folder, open tasks only (the archive is out of `actions.all_tasks`; an accepted loss), and the button's list skips them too. Hidden columns are left alone. No confirmation: the toast says how many moved and has Undo (back to working, one `prio` per task, 6 s). Disabled when nothing would move; its tooltip names the project. No key (none asked). The focus goes back to the board, so its keys keep working. Command `park` (`actions.park_others`), which chooses the tasks again; the front end's list only enables the button. Tested with pytest and a sandbox selftest (3 projects: the button, the toast, Undo, the inbox before and after).
- Pin (Renaud, 2026-11-10): "Pin", or "Unpin" on a pinned task (as Archive / Unarchive), in the Task menu and the right-click menus since 2026-11-15 (a button of the header before; see Modes, "Task menu"). A pinned task stays in the inbox until I unpin it, whatever its `inbox` flag: answering clears the flag, not the pin. The inbox is then: tasks with `inbox` set, or `pinned`, never archived ones (`views.in_inbox`).
  - Order: the tasks with `inbox` set first, in the order above; then the pinned tasks that need nothing now, in the same order. So a pinned task moves up when its agent replies, and back down after my answer.
  - Archive unpins, also the archive of my `close` prompt: the task is done, and it comes back unpinned when unarchived. An archived task cannot be pinned (the item is disabled).
  - Auto-advance skips the pinned tasks that need nothing; Cmd+Up/Down still reach them. The inbox count counts only the tasks with `inbox` set.
  - A pinned row of the inbox shows a small gray star under its status dot, and no text (Renaud, 2026-11-17: "a little star under the green or red dot not the text"; `.pin-star`). A pinned card keeps its "pinned" badge. A fork is not pinned. A pinned session still closes after 4 h without events, unless its `inbox` flag is set: the pin is about the list, not the process. No key for now.
- `live` is a third flag: a `claude` process runs for the task. Not shown (Renaud, 2026-11-10). Until then it was a filled or hollow dot next to the status color, but it changed nothing that I do: selecting a task without a process resumes it, a session that runs elsewhere has an overlay in 4a, and its status comes from the hooks wherever it runs. The backend still uses the flag (resume or not, the overlay, the close after 4 h).
- No badge on the card of a session that runs elsewhere. It said where and how ("VS Code · idle") until 2026-11-11, when Renaud had it removed: no UI marks whose only use is "runs elsewhere", since he runs his sessions only from Brain Fry. The backend still sends `elsewhere` (`views.task_json`), and the overlay of 4a still reads it, with its button "Resume here anyway".
- No done column. Archive is one action: status done, gray, hidden with the same toggle as later / more later / never. Archive does not end the `claude` process; the automatic close does that later.
- Since 2026-11-14 an archived task's file lives in `<project>/.brain_fry/archive/` (Task file, "The archive folder"), and the state that the backend pushes holds the open tasks, the archived tasks that this run touched, and every archived task only while the board asks for them: H on, or a search (App, "What the state holds"). So in Dev mode, where the hook events arrive, a push carries no old archived task. H and the search look the same as before: the archived cards come with the next push, a fraction of a second later.
- `close` archives by itself (Renaud, 2026-11-10). When I write just `close`, the agent writes down what the conversation found and ends its reply with `closed :-)` (`~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`). The Stop of that turn archives the task in place of putting it in the inbox, when the thread's prompt is `close` or `x` (case and a final `.` or `!` ignored; `x` added by Renaud, 2026-11-10) and one of the reply's last two non-empty lines holds `closed :-)` (two lines, so that a trailer may follow the mark). Both are required: an agent that asks something back before closing goes to the inbox, and the mark alone (without my prompt) archives nothing. The thread stays `pending`, so the digest still runs. As with my key, the process keeps running, and a new prompt in the session brings the task back from the archive. Code: `events.closes`. Since 2026-11-14 the Stop moves the file to the archive folder and the prompt moves it back; the digest of the archived task and its retry after a failure still run (App, "The sweep").
- Order in a column (Renaud, 2026-11-16: "i should be able to move a task up and down relative to other tasks. this order should also be visible in dev's inbox: if 2 tasks have the same status (green), the one with the higher order in org mode should appear higher in inbox, and should be selected first when auto-advance"). Before, cards were sorted by last update, and a reply moved a card to the top.
  - Each task file holds `rank`, a number: the smallest is on top (`views.rank`, `views.column_order`; the front end sorts the same way, `cardOrder` in `app.js`; the id breaks a tie). An update does not change it: only my moves do.
  - Shift+Up/Down in Organize mode moves the card under the cursor one place up or down, and the cursor follows it. In planning (two cards per row) one place in reading order: Shift+Up on the right card of a row swaps it with the left one. The neighbor is the card on screen: with a search, the hidden cards in between are passed over (the front end names the neighbor, the command `order` with `over` and `after`, `actions.place`). The card moves at once on screen, before the push.
  - One file is written per move: the rank halfway between the new neighbors. When they are closer than 0.001 (or tied), every open card of the column gets a new rank, 1 apart, in its order (ranks are near -1.8e9, where a float keeps about 7 decimals).
  - A new task lands on top of its column: a new file gets minus the time of its first save, to the microsecond (`taskfile.save`; `created` keeps the second only, and a card moved to the top in that second would stay above the new one). A card that changes column (Shift+Left/Right, a drag, "Move other projects to Next") lands on top of the new one: minus now, or 1 above the top card when that card is later than now (`actions._above`). "Move other projects to Next" keeps their order (the bottom one moves first), and its Undo puts each task back at its old rank (`prio` with `rank`). Dropping a card in its own column moves nothing.
  - Archived cards come after the open ones, the most recent first, whatever their rank: they are done, and the digits 1 to 9 of the open cards stay when H shows them. Shift+Up/Down does nothing on an archived card, and an open card does not pass them. An unarchived task comes back at its old rank.
  - The inbox follows the board inside a color: `inbox` set first, red first, then the rank (`views.inbox_order`); same for the rows of "All working". So a red task at the bottom of the board still comes first in the inbox.
  - Auto-advance (after an answer, an archive or a delete of the selected task) selects the first inbox item that needs me, no longer the item after the task that I left: of two green tasks, the higher one on the board comes first, as asked. A red task comes first too.
  - Migration: at each start, before any event, an open file without `rank` gets minus its last update (`actions.migrate_rank`), so the board shows the order that it had the day before, and new tasks stay above. An archived file without one gets its birth at its next save.
  - Decided by Claude: Shift+Up/Down as the keys (Shift+Left/Right already move a card); one place in reading order in planning (by rows, a card could not change side); a new task and a moved card on top; archived cards after the open ones; auto-advance to the first item; no drag inside a column, no menu item, no "to the top" key (none asked).
  - Tested: `test_inbox_follows_the_board_inside_a_color`, `test_a_new_task_lands_on_top_of_its_column`, `test_place_moves_a_task_above_or_below_another`, `test_place_numbers_the_column_again_when_the_gap_is_too_small`, `test_another_column_puts_the_task_on_top`, `test_migrate_rank_keeps_the_board_of_the_last_updates`, `test_park_others_keeps_the_order_of_the_moved_tasks`, `test_rank_of_a_file_without_one_is_its_birth`; a sandbox selftest (2026-11-16, window covered, synthetic keys): 8 files without rank migrated in the order of their updates, Shift+Up twice to the top, a third does nothing, Shift+Down, Hotel up in planning in reading order, Shift+Left lands on top of next, the red task first in the inbox while last on the board.
- A card shows the status color, the project label, then the short title (the title until the task has one; the title in its tooltip), then the task's digest, cut to 4 lines (all of it in its tooltip), and, while the task is in the inbox, its inbox line under a rule. Asked by Renaud on 2026-11-10: the title alone was too short. The digest stands as the description because it already exists, is rewritten at every turn, and starts with the goal. Cost: a card is about three times as tall, so a column shows fewer cards.
- No notifications (no macOS notification, no bell). The inbox is the only signal.
- Waiting for (Renaud, 2026-11-16: "One task may be blocked by another because I know it needs it. The agents may not know that but I know that and I would like to make that explicit. ... As long as the other item is not closed (meaning archived), the other one does not show up"). Built that day, choices 1a (a submenu), 2a (a badge) and the wording "Waiting for".
  - The right-click menus of an inbox row and of a card have "Waiting for ›": a submenu of the other open tasks of the working column (the rows of "All working", `st.working`, then the tasks that wait, so that a chain can be made), the project's name on the right of a task of another project. Picking B writes `waiting_for: <B's id>` in A's file (command `wait`, `actions.wait_for`). While A waits, its menus also have "Stop waiting for "B"" (`wait` with no `for`).
  - Computed, not stored: A is out of the inbox while B is an open task (`views.waits`, `views.in_inbox` with the ids of the open tasks, `views.inbox_list`). Archiving or deleting B changes nothing in A's file: A comes back at the next push with its flags as they were (a reply that came meanwhile is there, green), and in Dev mode with nothing selected it is selected (an arrival). The link stays, so unarchiving B hides A again. The front end gets `waitingFor` only while it holds A back (`views.task_json`), and shows no badge otherwise.
  - Everything that reads the inbox follows (the count, Cmd+Up/Down, Cmd+J I, auto-advance, the arrival), since they read the backend's list. Only the board shows A (Renaud, 2026-11-16: "I want the task that waits NOT to show in the inbox, only in Org mode"): not a row of "All working" either (`views.working_list`), since that list is for the tasks in flight and A is parked by my decision. Its card has a gray badge "waits for <B's short title>" (its tooltip: B's title; option 1 of the mockup, chosen that day), no "inbox" badge and no inbox line, and its right-click menu has "Stop waiting". A selected while it waits (opened from the board) is the dimmed row at the top of 1a, with the badge, as any selected task outside the list.
  - Decided by Claude: red and pinned tasks wait too ("does not show up"); one task to wait for, not a list; a chain works (A waits for B, B for C: C first, then B, then A); a loop is refused (the submenu disables a task that waits for A directly or through a chain, and `wait_for` raises), since it would hide both for good; the task itself, an archived and an unknown task are refused; an archived task cannot start to wait (the item is disabled); marking the selected inbox item selects the next item at once, as an archive does (`setWait` in `app.js`), and Cmd+Up goes back to it; a fork does not wait; a waiting task's session closes after 4 h like one without a reply (`Core._sweep`), since it may stay out of the inbox for days; no key, no item in the Task menu of the menu bar, no message to A's agent when B is archived (none asked).
  - Tested: `test_waiting_for_hides_a_task_until_the_other_is_archived`, `test_waiting_for_a_deleted_task_holds_nothing_back`, `test_red_and_pinned_tasks_wait_too`, `test_waiting_for_follows_a_chain`, `test_waiting_for_refuses_itself_an_archived_task_and_a_loop`, `test_stop_waiting_and_what_the_front_end_gets`, `test_a_fork_does_not_wait`, `test_wait_command_takes_a_task_out_of_the_inbox`; a sandbox selftest (2026-11-16, window visible): Alpha selected, right-click, Waiting for > Bravo, Bravo selected at once, Alpha not a row of "All working", its card with the badge and its menu with "Stop waiting for "Bravo"", Alpha listed but disabled in Bravo's submenu, Bravo archived, Alpha back in the inbox.

## Reply trailer and resume note (not built), auto-advance

Three additions from a review on 2026-11-09. It compared the design with my Claude Code transcripts (296 sessions, 1,217 prompts, 2026-08-27 to 11-09) and with research on interruptions and on supervising automation. The analysis and its sources are in RESEARCH.md.

Status on 2026-11-10: auto-advance is built. The reply trailer and the resume note are specified but not built: no code parses `[needs: ...]`, the hook has no SessionStart handler, `bf install` writes no `trailer_prompt.md`, and nothing asks for a resume note. The text below describes how they will work.

**Reply trailer (not built): the agent says what it needs from me**

- Every reply of an agent ends with one line: `[needs: KIND] TEXT`. KIND is a thread kind:
  - `answer`: the agent asked me a question. TEXT is the question.
  - `rule`: I must choose between options or rule on a trade-off. TEXT names the choice and its options.
  - `review`: the agent changed something that I should check. TEXT says what to check and where.
  - `fyi`: nothing is needed from me. TEXT is the outcome in a few words.

  When several apply, the first in this order wins. `blocked` still comes from the Notification hook.
- Why: the agent has the whole context of its turn, while Sonnet infers the kind from a reply cut to 8,000 characters. The line also shows in 4a, at the end of the reply. In the transcripts, only 13% of final replies had a question in their last paragraph, so most trailers will say `review` or `fyi`.
- The instruction is `~/.brain_fry/trailer_prompt.md`, written by `bf install`. The hook prints it on SessionStart, and Claude Code adds the stdout of a SessionStart hook to the model's context (documented; it does not show as a message in the terminal). SessionStart fires on startup, resume, clear, compact and fork, so the instruction comes back after a compaction. This reaches every session in a Brain Fry project, wherever it was started (Brain Fry, VS Code, a plain terminal), and changes no CLAUDE.md. On SessionStart the hook writes no event file.
- Not `--append-system-prompt`: it reaches only the sessions that Brain Fry starts. And Claude Code records the system prompt at a conversation's first request and resends that record until a compaction (`--system-prompt-snapshot`, on by default), so an edit of the text would not reach the existing sessions.
- The hook prints nothing for scripted `-p` runs, because a script may parse their output: it skips when `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT` is `sdk-cli`.
- At Stop, Brain Fry reads the last non-empty line of `last_assistant_message`. It accepts `[needs: KIND] TEXT` in any case, also inside `*` or backticks. No such line, or an unknown KIND: no trailer, and the kind comes from Sonnet as before.
- With a trailer, the thread gets its kind and question at once, and the inbox row shows the trailer instead of the start of the reply. Sonnet still writes the title and the digest; its prompt says that the trailer is the agent's own claim. When Sonnet's kind differs, the thread keeps it as `**Sonnet kind**` and the inbox row shows a small mark. These disagreements show how far each label can be trusted. That matters: research on diagnostic aids finds that a label that is right less than about 70% of the time does worse than no label (Wickens & Dixon 2007).
- The reply in the task file stays verbatim, trailer included.
- First text of `trailer_prompt.md`:

```
End every reply with one last line, and nothing after it:
[needs: KIND] TEXT
KIND is one of:
- answer: you asked me a question. TEXT is the question, in one sentence.
- rule: I must choose between options or rule on a trade-off. TEXT names the choice and its options.
- review: you changed something that I should check. TEXT says what to check and where.
- fyi: you need nothing from me. TEXT is the outcome in a few words.
If several apply, use the first in this list. If you have several questions, TEXT holds the most important one and the others go in the reply above.
```

**Resume note (not built): one line from me when I leave a task unfinished**

- When I move a task into a hidden column (later, more later, never), a one-line input opens and takes the focus: on the task's row in 1a (Dev mode), or on its card (Organize mode). Enter saves. Esc closes it without saving. Up/Down, Cmd+Up/Down, a click on another task or a mode switch close it and save what I typed.
- While the input is open, letters and Left/Right go to it, not to the Organize keys.
- No input on archive (the task is done), on answer (the agent continues and will reply), or on a move between working, next and planning (the task stays in view).
- I write the note; nothing is prefilled. Why: Leroy & Glomb 2018 found that a short plan, written when leaving an unfinished task (where I stopped, what is left), reduced the attention that stays on it and improved the next task. The move alone records no plan, and the digest is Sonnet's summary of the past, not my plan.
- The note is saved as `resume_note` in the frontmatter. It shows on the card, and under the digest in the inbox row when the task comes back. My next prompt to the task (UserPromptSubmit) removes it. Each note is also logged in `events.jsonl`, so that I can see later how often I write one and whether the habit lasts.

**Auto-advance: after I answer, the next item**

- When I send a prompt to the selected task in Dev mode, Brain Fry selects the first inbox item that needs me about 1 s later (since 2026-11-16; before, the item after the answered task, see Status, inbox, board, "Order in a column"). The trigger is that task's UserPromptSubmit event; the second lets me see that the prompt was accepted.
- The task that I answered leaves 1a at the advance (Renaud, 2026-11-10: "it should disappear"). Cmd+Up from the first inbox item selects it again: to watch it, or to press Esc when the agent goes the wrong way. It comes back in the inbox when its agent replies.
- When nothing else in the inbox needs me (pinned tasks without a reply or a block are skipped), the answered task leaves 4a all the same: nothing is selected and 4a shows a calm photo (Renaud, 2026-11-10: "I would like it to still go away even if the inbox is empty"). Until then 4a kept the task, because there was nothing better to show. Cmd+Up goes back to it. No advance for a prompt that starts with `/` (`/model` or `/compact` is not an answer); the payload's `prompt` field gives the text. Granting or refusing a permission, or answering a question of the agent, advances too, but later: the scan of Claude Code's process files sees it within 3 s, then the advance waits its second (see Hooks, "Red clears"). The `prompted` message then has `why: dialog closed`.
- No advance for a prompt that Claude Code writes itself: the result of a background agent or shell starts a turn with a UserPromptSubmit too (see Hooks, "Background work"). Found by Claude on 2026-11-10, fixed when Renaud asked: before this, the front end left the task that I was reading when its agent's result arrived. The test is `events.typed_by_me`: the payload's `source` when present (only `user` is me; the schema of 2.1.322 also lists `sdk`, `system`, `loop_wakeup`, `schedule_wakeup`, `poll_event`), else a prompt that does not start with `<agent-message` or `<task-notification`. No payload has had `source` yet (92 UserPromptSubmit in events.jsonl, 2026-11-10). Such a turn still clears `inbox`, as any prompt: the agent works again.
- No advance either when I typed in the task's terminal after its prompt arrived (a follow-up that I am typing would otherwise land in the next task's terminal), or when I selected another task within the second.
- "AA" in the heading of 1a and Task > Auto-advance in the menu bar (since 2026-11-15, see Modes, "Task menu"; before, a switch in the header) turn it off and on (Renaud, 2026-11-11: "re-do the checkbox with auto-advance, i want to be able to toggle it off / on"). On 2026-11-10 he had removed it ("It's always auto advance. That's the whole point of Brain Fry"); it came back as it was before, from commit 69249b6.
  - Off: after an answer (a prompt, or the dialog of a blocked task that closed), the task stays selected in 4a and stays at the top of 1a; bf.log says `auto-advance off: stays on <id>`. Switching it off during the second of an advance cancels that advance.
  - Not affected by the switch: Archive in Dev mode still moves to the next item (the task is done, nothing to stay on), and an arrival is still selected when nothing is selected (the calm photo shows only while nothing needs me). Both decided by Claude on 2026-11-11, as the old switch did for Archive.
  - On by default. The window remembers it (`autoAdvance` in `window.json`, see App, "Window settings"); since 2026-11-14 it survives a restart (before, it came back on at each start). No key.
  - After a click, the keyboard goes back to the selected task's terminal (4a).
  - Tested on 2026-11-11 with a sandbox selftest (window visible; real UserPromptSubmit events in the spool, one per mark that the JS logged): off --> the prompt left the task selected; on --> it advanced to the next item after 1 s; off during the second --> stayed; Archive with the switch off --> advanced at once; the focus stayed in xterm.js's textarea after each click. Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- Archive (the button or Cmd+E) of the selected task in Dev mode advances at once, with no second to wait: the task is done, so there is no agent to watch (Renaud, 2026-11-10: "remove it from inbox and move to next one"). The next item is chosen as after an answer (the first inbox item that needs me, pinned tasks that need nothing skipped; until 2026-11-16 the item after it). The archived task leaves 1a at once. When nothing else needs me, nothing is selected and 4a shows a calm photo. Cmd+Up from the first item goes back to the archived task, to unarchive it. Unarchive and the archive of Organize mode (E on a card) do not advance. Tested on 2026-11-10 with a sandbox selftest: inbox C, B, A; archive B --> A, Cmd+E in 4a's textarea --> C, archive C --> empty, Cmd+Up --> C.
- The empty 4a (no task selected, after an answer, an archive or at start, while nothing needs me): a photo that fills 4a, with no text, no button and nothing that moves (Renaud, 2026-11-10: "nothing here, just something calming"). A different one each time the screen appears, at random among the files of `web/calm/`, never the same twice in a row. It is dimmed by 15% for the dark theme and fades in (see Fades of 4a, below). The screen "No open task in <project>" of the project dropdown keeps its text and button.
- Fades of 4a (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-11: "a slower fade-in" for the photo, and "whenever I move to the next task, the Claude panel should fade out, and the next one should fade in a little more calmly"). Chosen from a local mockup with four kinds of each (plain, slow start then faster, fog that clears, a slow zoom; dip, crossfade, dip and rise, dip through fog), with sliders for the timings. He picked the plainest of each: "A1, 1 s" for the photo, B1 (the dip) with a 500 ms pause and a 1 s ease-in.
  - A change of what 4a shows in Dev mode (a select by click, key, auto-advance or arrival, an archive, the photo): the old content fades out (250 ms, ease-in), 4a stays empty for 500 ms, then the new one fades in (1 s, ease-in). The photo fades in over 1 s, ease-in-out, each time it appears, at start too; after a task it waits for the task's fade out and the pause, like a task. Leaving the photo for a task: the photo fades out like a task. Before: the swap was instant and the photo faded in over 1.2 s, ease-out.
  - Decided by Claude, listed on the mockup and not contested: only 4a fades (2a and column 3 keep the veil or the instant swap); a new switch during a fade fades the old content out from where it is, with no queue of fades; no fade where 4a was not on screen: Organize and Review mode, the switch into Dev mode itself (`openInDev`: `swapAtOnce`, `S.instant` for one frame), and the first select at start (4a showed nothing). Selecting the task already selected does nothing to a running fade.
  - A new task (Cmd+T) or a fork: no fade at all, 4a swaps at once (Renaud, 2026-11-11: "If I create a new task, there should be no fade-in for Claude Code. Claude Code should come instantly. It's just when I switch from one item in the inbox to the next one that it was too fast"). The old task's terminal, or the photo, goes at once too, and so does the overlay's fade of a switch just before. The arrival of the new task (`onState`) calls `swapAtOnce`. Decided by Claude: a fork counts as a new task (he asked for it; it arrives by the same path). The veil of 2a and column 3 stays: he named only Claude Code. Tested on 2026-11-11 with a sandbox selftest (window visible): B --> new task and A --> fork with no animation on any layer and the old terminal at `display: none` at the arrival; A --> B and new task --> A still fade; photo --> new task, the photo hidden at once (that last step ran with the window covered, which does not change this check).
  - The keyboard: the new terminal is on screen at opacity 0 from the start of the switch and gets the focus as before, so a key typed during the fade goes to the selected task, never to the old one. The fading-out terminal takes no clicks (`pointer-events: none`). The overlay ("No live session") fades in with the new task; an overlay of the old task goes at once.
  - Code: `showTerm`, `fadeOut`, `fadeIn` and `calm` in `app.js` (Web Animations API: each fade starts from the computed opacity of the running one). The end of a fade out uses the animation's `finished` promise, not `onfinish`: in a hidden page the events never fire, so a selftest could not see the old layer go to `display: none`.
  - Tested on 2026-11-11 with a sandbox selftest (three inbox tasks; window covered, so the animations were seeked by hand, see App, "selftest of an animation"): C to A, A to B, B to the photo, the photo to C, A then B 150 ms later, B again, Organize to Dev. The timings and the opacities at 0, 125, 250, 500, 760, 1000, 1250, 1500, 1760 ms matched, every faded-out layer ended hidden, no animation left. Not seen: the fades in real time, and the focus (it is set in a `requestAnimationFrame`, which a hidden page never runs). Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- Switch card (Renaud, 2026-11-11: "when I get to the Claude Code console, sometimes I don't remember what the task is about"). Cause, confirmed by him: auto-advance changes 4a while his eyes stay on 4a, so he never looks at 1a. He first asked for the title of the inbox row at the top or the bottom of 4a, all the time. Claude pushed back: the title was removed from the header on 2026-11-10 because it repeated 1a, and the selected row stays in 1a; a label that never changes is soon no longer read; the title (set once, a few words) says which task, not what it is about. Chosen from a local mockup with two choices crossed (when: in the pause with Claude Code waiting, for N s or until a key; or over Claude Code, for N s or until a key. What: the title, plus the goal line, plus the whole digest): "option 4 with the title and goal, and also some of the text I typed in the first thread ... maybe the first two lines".
  - At every switch that fades (see Fades of 4a: auto-advance, a click or a key in 1a, an arrival; not a new task or a fork, not the photo): a card over the top of 4a, from the pause on (fades in over 300 ms, ease-out, 250 ms after the switch starts). Claude Code fades in under it as before. It shows the status dot and the title, "Goal" with the goal line of the task's digest, and "Asked" with my first prompt on one line, cut after two lines.
  - It goes at my first key (a modifier alone does not count), click or scroll after it came (600 ms, ease-in), in any part of the window. The key also goes where it would go anyway (into Claude Code, mostly): the card listens in the capture phase and never stops an event. Clicks go through it (`pointer-events: none`). The key or click that made the switch does not close the card (`e.timeStamp` before `cardAt`). A new switch fades the old card out with the old task (250 ms), then shows the new one; the photo and a swap at once hide it.
  - The goal line: the line of the task's digest that starts with "Goal:", without the label (the digest prompt asks for the goal first; 67 of 74 tasks had one on 2026-11-11, the others had no digest at all), else the digest's first line (`views.goal_line`). The first prompt: the first thread's prompt, without the `<pasted_content ...>` tags around a paste and the `[Image #N]` marks, on one line, cut to 300 characters; a planned task that never started: its notes; empty until the first digest, since the title shown then is already the prompt's first line (`views.first_prompt`). Both are fields of the task in the state (`goal`, `firstPrompt`).
  - Decided by Claude: the card sits at the top of 4a, over old scrollback, never over the end of the reply or the input box; it covers the chip of the editor selection (`#sel-chip`) until my first key; the label "Asked"; no hint text on the card; a key typed into another pane or a click anywhere closes it too, so that it never stays on as a label.
  - The look (Renaud, 2026-11-13: "a small rounded pop-up ... the full width of the Claude Code panel"; it was already 12 px from each edge, with 8 px corners and a heavy shadow). From a local mockup of five looks at his 4a width (about 1230 px): "1 is perfect, but a tad larger fonts". Edge to edge and flush with the top of 4a (no top border), only the bottom corners rounded (6 px), padding 14 px 16 px, a lighter shadow; title 16 px, the Goal and Asked lines 14 px, their labels 12 px (were 15, 13, 11). Decided by Claude: one pixel more for each size.
  - Code: `showCard`, `hideCard`, `fadeCard`, `closeCard` in `app.js`, called from `showTerm`. Tested on 2026-11-11 with pytest (`test_views.py`) and a sandbox selftest (three copied tasks, window visible, animations finished by hand): no card at the first select, the card of the second task (300 ms with a 250 ms delay), Shift alone keeps it, a key closes it (600 ms), a click in 2a closes it, a switch while a card shows (250 ms out, then the new card), the start of the photo fades it out. Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
  - Why no text and no button: the old text said "Select a task in the inbox, or start one with Cmd+N", and so invited a new session at the moment when nothing needs me (the spiral of brain_fry.md). Counts and lists of the agents at work were rejected too: they give something to watch.
  - The photos: misty and foggy landscapes, portrait (4a is about 0.6 wide for 1 tall), from Wikimedia Commons, chosen by Renaud from ten candidates (he dropped the beach, the empty blue sky with the moon, and the salt lake). What made a candidate: large quiet areas (mist, fog, snow, still water), low contrast, muted colors, no sharp subject, no people or buildings. Files and credits in `web/calm/` (`CREDITS.md`: most are CC BY or CC BY-SA, which ask for that attribution when they are shipped). Landscape photos, such as the macOS wallpapers, were rejected: the crop to portrait loses most of them. Renaud added four landscape photos of his own on 2026-11-11 (the Argentine ridge, two of Les Diablerets, Creux de Champ; author and license unknown, see `CREDITS.md`). 4a shows a centered crop of about 30% of their width (40% for Creux de Champ, which is 3:2), so the needle of the Argentine and both summits of the Diablerets photo fall outside it.
  - Renaud added fourteen more on 2026-11-13, from `~/Desktop/Save_webP` (mountain photos: glaciers, ridges, ski tracks, climbers; author and license not recorded), and asked to keep the whole folder out of GitHub. So `web/calm/` is in `.gitignore` and was removed from the index (with `CREDITS.md`); the copies of the earlier commits stay in the history of GitHub. The front end no longer lists the photos: it reads `/calm.json` at start (`_calm` in `server.py`: the files of the folder named with letters, digits, `-` and `_`, in `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.png` or `.webp`; a missing folder gives `[]` and 4a stays dark). A photo dropped in the folder shows after a restart, with no code change. Done by Claude: converted to JPEG (quality 85), at most 2048 px on the long side, EXIF removed (the `PXL_` phone photos held their GPS position), the black rounded frame of the two `.avif.png` cut off, one duplicate dropped. Most are landscape, so the centered portrait crop of 4a applies to them too. Tested with pytest (the listing, a missing folder, names with a space skipped); not seen in the window: the app must be restarted.
  - Renaud added twenty-four more on 2026-11-14, from files on his Desktop (mountain photos again: snow, glaciers, seas of clouds, sunrises, hoarfrost; author and license not recorded). No code change: the folder now holds 49 photos, and `_calm` lists them all. Their EXIF holds only a date (no position, no camera), so a photo of at most 2048 px on the long side is copied as is, without a second JPEG compression (19 of them); the five larger ones are reduced to 2048 px (quality 85). None repeats an earlier photo (compared with a difference hash); two show the same place as `gross-furkahorn-sunrise.jpg`, from another spot. Names and original file names in `CREDITS.md`.
  - The same day he removed the seven photos of Wikimedia Commons, after a local mockup that showed them numbered ("remove 1-7, keep them in an archive folder in there"). They are in `web/calm/archive/`. `_calm` lists the files of `web/calm/` only, not those of a subfolder, so a photo moved to `archive/` is no longer shown (covered by `test_calm_lists_only_photos_with_plain_names`). 42 photos remain, all added by Renaud.
  - Renaud added five more on 2026-11-15, from files on his Desktop: cave paintings of the Chauvet-Pont d'Arc cave (horses, a black feline, two hands, a spotted bear and panther in red), `chauvet-*.jpg`. No code change: the folder now holds 47 photos. Four are JPEGs of at most 1600 px, copied as is; their EXIF names the rights holder ("Carole Fritz et Gilles Tosello - CNRS - Equipe Chauvet - Ministere de la Culture et de la Communication") and holds no position (the GPS block has only a version tag), so it was kept as the notice. The fifth was `Untitled.png`, a screenshot with black bars (1203 x 1089): cut to the photo (1158 x 768) and saved as JPEG at quality 85. License not recorded; details in `CREDITS.md`. The app must be restarted to show them (the page reads `/calm.json` once).
  - Two facts found on 2026-11-14. (1) 4a is no longer the portrait of 2026-11-10 (0.6 wide for 1 tall): its shape follows the splitters and the hidden columns. That day its terminal had 182 x 88 characters (`bf.log`, the line "pty ... started"), about 1440 x 1320 px, a little wider than tall; the day before, 96 x 56. At such a shape 4a shows about 82% of the width of a 4:3 photo, and 55% of a 2:1 panorama (the Argentine and the two Diablerets photos), where the portrait shape showed 30%. So landscape photos are fine now. (2) The page reads `/calm.json` once, at start. After a photo is removed or moved while the app runs, the window may still pick it: 4a then stays dark until the app is restarted. Photos added meanwhile are not picked either.
- The idle screen (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-11: "You can remove Claude Code and show the image, but I want the file explorer to show the last project, the project selector to show the last project, and the latest layout"). While the photo shows, only 4a changes. The dropdown, 2a and column 3 show the task that I left (after an answer or an archive), or at start the task that I opened last (largest `opened`, in any project, archived or not): its project, its tree, its layout (DESIGN.md, Concepts). Before, they emptied, and pane 1 showed a hint.
  - That task is not selected (`S.idle`, not `S.selected`; `wsId()` returns it). Its own tabs are the ones on screen, live: its terminals keep running, its pages stay loaded. Closing or moving one of them changes that task, as when it is selected. At start, the backend command `idle` starts its terminal tabs (fresh shells, once per run, as at a first select: `Core._restore_shells`), without starting its session and without changing `opened`.
