  - A tab opened on the idle screen (a file from 2a, a link in a terminal, Cmd+N T, B, F or E) is a project tab, which every task of the project shows (see Project tabs, built 2026-11-11). Until then a file opened there was not added to that task (Renaud: "it's just added here and stays here"): it had `idle` and was closed at the next select, and Cmd+N T and B showed the toast of a screen without a task.
  - Picking a project in the dropdown leaves the idle screen as a select does. Picking the project that it already shows does nothing (no change event); Cmd+Up goes back to the task.
  - An arrival is still selected at once (`selectArrival` does not look at `S.idle`).
  - Tested on 2026-11-11 with pytest (`idle` starts the terminal tabs without the session, once) and a sandbox selftest (window visible, `idle_test.py` in that session's scratchpad, not kept): at start the task opened last, in the other project, showed with its two files and its terminal, the dropdown on its project, the photo, no hint; a file opened there was marked `idle` and not in the task's `openFiles`; selecting the other task, then back: the file was gone; `selectNone` --> idle on the task left; an arrival was selected. Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
  - The photo shows only while nothing needs me (Renaud, 2026-11-10: "The inbox was empty and then it was not empty anymore. The new entry in the inbox did not get focus but it should"). In Dev mode with no task selected, the first inbox item that needs me (`inbox` set: a reply or a block; a quiet pinned task does not count) is selected at once, with the keyboard focus in 4a, as an advance would. Until then a reply that arrived while the photo showed only added its row to 1a. Code: `selectArrival` in `app.js`, logged as `inbox: nothing was selected --> <id>`.
    - When: at each state push (a reply, a block, also the first push at start), and when I switch to Dev mode (B, Esc on the board, the mode buttons, back from Review mode). In Organize and Review mode nothing is selected; the switch to Dev mode does it. Not on the screen "No open task in <project>": I picked that project. Not while Cmd+T, Cmd+P or the plan dialog is open, because the selection would take the keyboard from it; the next state push after it closes does it.
    - Not from the internal calls `setMode('dev', false)` (`openInDev`, a new task, a project without a task): they select a task themselves right after, and an inbox item selected in between would lose its `unread` and resume its session for nothing.
    - Tested on 2026-11-10 with a sandbox selftest (two tasks working, inbox empty, photo): Stop of the first --> selected 60 ms after the event; Organize mode, nothing selected, Stop of the second --> nothing selected; Dev mode --> the first inbox item selected, focus in xterm.js's textarea. Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
    - Known race, not handled: a state push that left the backend just before an archive (Cmd+E) still lists the archived task in the inbox, and would select it again. The window is a few milliseconds.
  - Tested on 2026-11-10 with a sandbox selftest: one inbox task, select it, `onPrompted` --> nothing selected and a photo (1280x1707, 508x820 on screen) after 1 s; Cmd+Up --> the task; Cmd+E --> another photo.
- Cmd+E and not Cmd+R (Renaud proposed Cmd+R on 2026-11-10; Cmd+A is select all): Cmd+R reloads the page of a browser tab of 3a, and E matches the E of Organize mode.
- How: when the backend applies a UserPromptSubmit, it sends a `prompted` message on `/ctl` (task id, and whether the prompt starts with `/`), before the state push. The front end takes the first inbox item that needs me, other than that task (`nextItem` in `app.js`; until 2026-11-16 the item that came after the task, else the first one). It logs each advance, and each advance that it skipped with the reason, in bf.log (logger `brain_fry.ui`). Tested on 2026-11-10 in the window: inbox C, B, A; an answer to B selected A 1.0 s later.
- Why: after an answer, 4a shows the agent at work for a median of 2 min (agent turn, in the transcripts). That moment invites three things:
  - watching the agent: tools that need constant monitoring were the strongest predictor of fatigue in the BCG survey (Bedard et al., HBR 2026; a survey, so an association, not a proven cause);
  - choosing the next item: a small decision, about 20 times a day;
  - starting a new session to fill the wait: the spiral of brain_fry.md, which ParallelPilot and METR also observed.

  Auto-advance removes that moment. It makes the switch that I already make on 75% of my prompts.
- Not for speed: in the transcripts a switch cost no measurable time (median 205 s to answer a waiting reply after a switch, 217 s after staying). It can backfire: a faster flow pushes more items through me per hour. The weekly measures of idea 10 in RESEARCH.md would show that.
- Watching is rarely needed: 15 interrupts in 1,198 turns. A permission prompt needs no watching, because the task comes back red and sorts first.

## Claude Code's process files and transcripts

- The region "Other Claude Code sessions" under the board, with drag and drop to adopt a session as a task, was built on 2026-11-10 (asked by Renaud: step in on the sessions that started in another IDE) and removed on 2026-11-11 (decided on 2026-11-10). It served only the sessions that started before `bf install` or before `bf init` of their project: hooks load when a session starts, so every newer session of a project fires them. Removed: `sessions.scan`, `sessions.adopt`, the `adopt` command, the cards, drag-to-adopt and the O key of Organize mode (about 300 lines with tests).
- Such an old session becomes a task this way: `claude --resume` in the project (it opens Claude Code's session picker), send one prompt, exit. The hook creates the task at that prompt, and `events._import_history` imports the earlier turns from the transcript (see Hooks). Not tested end to end with an old session.
- Retention, seen on 2026-11-13: `~/.claude/projects/` held 418 transcripts, the oldest last changed on 2026-08-27 (78 days before), and `~/.claude/settings.json` has no `cleanupPeriodDays`. So the cleanup after 30 days that Claude Code's docs describe had not removed them on this machine. Not investigated. The rule stays: the task file is the record.
- Transcripts, `~/.claude/projects/*/<session id>.jsonl` (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` moves `~/.claude`): `sessions.read_turns` makes one thread of kind `imported` per prompt that I typed, with the text after the last tool call of its turn as reply.
- My prompts are the user entries with `origin.kind` `human`. Transcripts from before about 2026-10 have no `origin`: there, entries flagged `isMeta`, `isCompactSummary` or `isSidechain` are skipped, and so are texts that Claude Code writes in my name ("[Request interrupted", "This session is being continued", skill bodies). IDE blocks (`<ide_opened_file>`, `<ide_selection>`) are dropped from the prompt; a slash command shows as typed (`/model sonnet`).
- Running processes: Claude Code keeps one file per process in `~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json` (internal format, seen with Claude Code 2.1.319): `pid`, `sessionId`, `cwd`, `entrypoint` (`claude-vscode`, `cli`), `name`, `status` (`idle`, `busy`, `waiting` while a dialog waits for me, with `waitingFor`), `statusUpdatedAt`. A file counts while its pid is alive. Brain Fry uses the folder only while it lists the app's own sessions (checked after 15 s); otherwise it ignores the folder and logs a warning. A process that is killed (SIGKILL) leaves its file there, with a `<pid>.<hash>.key` file next to it (seen on 2026-11-14 with 2.1.325, after a probe in a PTY): a probe that kills its `claude` removes both, after it checked that the pid is dead and that `cwd` and `startedAt` are its own.
- What the list of processes is used for: `live` of every task without a process in the app follows the list, and a blocked task whose dialog closed leaves the inbox (see Hooks). So a session that has no hooks (no SessionEnd) does not stay live forever, and a task that runs in VS Code is not resumed twice by a click. A task that runs elsewhere shows where on its card ("VS Code · idle") and in 4a. The backend reads the folder every 3 s (`Core._refresh_outside`, which also notices a change of `projects.json`).
- A task that cannot be saved during this check (2026-11-14): `Core._sync_outside` and `Core._end_blocks` go on with the other tasks. The error is logged once per task and run (`Core._not_saved`, `Core._save_failed`), and the save is tried again at each check (every 3 s). Before, one such task ended every `_refresh_outside`: the tasks after it kept a wrong `live`, no block ended, and a change of `projects.json` was not noticed. Tested in `tests/test_app_backend.py`.
- Stepping in on a session that runs in VS Code: 4a says where it runs. Close it there, then Resume. "Resume here anyway" runs two processes on one conversation, and their messages get mixed.

## Logs

Asked by Renaud on 2026-11-10: log extensively, so that Claude can review from time to time that everything went fine.

- `~/.brain_fry/logs/bf.log`, written by the app (rotates at 5 MB, keeps 5 older files) and by the `bf` subcommands (append only: two processes that rotate one file could lose lines). A line: time, level, `app[pid]` or `cli:<command>[pid]`, thread, module, message. `bf --debug` adds DEBUG lines.
- Logged: the start (versions of Brain Fry, Python, pywebview, macOS and `claude --version`, the projects); front end connected and disconnected; every command from the window (polling and file reads only at DEBUG); every applied hook event with what it changed (status, live, inbox, unread, thread kind); every change of a task field by an action, old --> new; created tasks (new, fork); digests (threads sent, duration, kinds, title; failures as warnings); each process in a PTY (pid, command, cwd, size, then exit code or signal, lifetime, bytes of output); saves and refused saves; `live` changes that come from Claude Code's process files; changes of `projects.json`; the quit.
- Errors: failed commands, exceptions with their trace, and the front end's JS errors and unhandled rejections (sent on `/ctl`, logger `brain_fry.ui`).
- Not logged: the text of prompts and replies. Task files and transcripts have them.
- `~/.brain_fry/logs/hook.log`: the hook appends one line per event that it wrote (`Stop 2762c163 --> <project>`, with `+ selection <path> 51-52` when a prompt took my selection) and each error (no jq, spool not writable). The app moves it to `hook.log.1` at start when it passes 2 MB. An event in hook.log without its line in bf.log was written but never applied.
- The hook in `~/.brain_fry/` is a copy. When it differs from the hook of the running version, a banner asks for `bf install`, which takes effect at once (Claude Code runs the file at each event).

## Split = fork

`claude --resume <id> --fork-session` starts a new session with the full history. The new task gets its own file, starts with the parent's digest and has `parent` set. This is "duplicate the main thread to specialise". The fork opens in 4a and gets the parent's prio and its tabs (file, browser, terminal; see 2a, 3a, 3b).

Tested on 2026-11-09: `claude --resume <id> --fork-session --session-id <new uuid>` works. The fork keeps the history and uses the new id, so Brain Fry knows it from the start.

## Worktrees (discussed 2026-11-10, not built)

Renaud asked whether a task should get a git worktree by default. Decision: write it down, do not build it now.

- Today no task gets a worktree. A new task runs `claude --session-id` in the project root (`actions.new_task`), a fork in its parent's `cwd` (`actions.fork_task`), a resume in the task's `cwd`. That `cwd` is set when the task is born and never updated from later events (`events.py`). The only support is passive: the hook walks up from `cwd`, so a session that Renaud starts with `claude -w` (Claude Code 2.1.322 creates `.claude/worktrees/<name>/`) still lands in its project. `.claude` is in `~/.gitignore_global`, so worktrees there are ignored by git; `.brain_fry/` is too, so a worktree holds no `.brain_fry/` and the walk-up reaches the project's.
- For: isolation (an agent does not see another agent's half-finished edits, its tests do not fail on them); a diff per task (in a worktree, "Diff vs HEAD" shows only this task's work, which v2's review needs); closing a task = merging its branch and removing its worktree, the "close it" step of brain_fry.md.
- Against a default for every task:
  - A worktree starts from HEAD, not from the working tree. On 2026-11-10 this repo had 1 commit, 28 modified files and 17 untracked ones: a worktree task would not have seen the day's work. A default would force a commit before each task, against Renaud's way of prototyping.
  - Gitignored files are missing in a worktree: `.venv` (uv rebuilds it), `.env`, data folders, checkpoints. In an ML project the agent may not find its data.
  - Many tasks write no code (quick questions, research). At 7.6 new sessions per active day, worktrees and branches pile up, and each task that writes adds a merge. Conflicts move from silent and continuous (one shared folder) to explicit at merge time: one more decision in the inbox.
  - Some projects may not be git repos.
  - SWITCHdrive syncs `.claude/` (its exclude list has only `.venv`, `node_modules`, `target`, `*_nosync`), so each worktree is uploaded (about 9 MB of tracked files for this repo). A `*_nosync` folder avoids it, but uncommitted work in it would have no backup. Minor for code repos.
- Renaud's `land` instruction (`~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`) already describes an opt-in habit: a worktree when another agent works in the main tree.
- Evidence still missing: the 67% of RESEARCH.md (two concurrent agents shared a project folder) measures how often the risk was present, not how often harm happened. Default worktrees cost something on every task, so they pay off only if collisions are frequent. Proposed count, from Claude Code's transcripts: Edit and Write tool calls carry a file path and a timestamp; count the cases where two sessions of one project edited the same file within about 30 minutes, and the turns where a test run failed while another session was editing. A proxy: it misses conflicts that span files and counts harmless overlaps, but it is much closer to harm than sharing a folder. Around 1 turn in 200: worktrees would solve a rare problem at a daily cost. Around 1 in 10: a default is justified.
- Two questions for Renaud decide it: how often do two agents that write code run in one repo at once, and is he willing to commit before starting such a task?
- If built:
  - Opt-in: a modifier in the Cmd+T picker (e.g. Option+Enter) for "new task in a worktree", and `"worktree": true` per project in `projects.json` for a project default.
  - Brain Fry creates the worktree itself (`git worktree add .claude/worktrees/<id> -b bf/<id>`) and starts `claude --session-id` there, rather than passing `-w`: as with `--session-id`, it knows the path from the start, and `--resume` must run there.
  - 2a and "Diff vs HEAD" follow the task's `cwd`, not the project root.
  - Archive: a clean worktree whose branch is merged is removed with its branch; otherwise Brain Fry asks (merge, keep, discard). Renaud's `land` steps become a Brain Fry action.
  - Open: what a fork of a worktree task gets (the parent's worktree, or a new one from the parent's branch, which needs a commit).
  - Middle path: every task starts in the main tree and moves into a worktree once it starts writing (Claude Code's EnterWorktree tool does that during a session). Brain Fry would then have to update the task's `cwd` from the hook events.

## Review mode (built 2026-11-10)

The "Git view" of the spec and the "3rd mode: review" of TODO.md: a git client in the style of SourceTree, from Renaud's notes of 2026-11-10. In SourceTree he uses the history view with "Uncommitted changes" selected: the log with its graph at the top, the staged and unstaged files at the bottom, and on the right a read-only diff where he stages or unstages hunks. He uses no stashes and uses the sidebar little. Where his notes say nothing, the choices below copy SourceTree (Mac) and leave out the actions that change many files at once under a running agent (checkout, merge, rebase). After a mockup on claude.ai (2026-11-10, https://claude.ai/artifact/PJHoKneGy5hwAdbiSnqCRv), Renaud answered the open points: the commit message goes in a dialog, single lines can be staged, discard is wanted, and 4a can be shown on demand; no amend, no search in the log, no history of one file.

Layout, numbered as in the spec:

- 1: a narrow side panel: worktrees, then local branches. Cmd+1 hides it (in Dev mode, Cmd+1 hid 2a until 2026-11-11; it now picks a tab). Cmd+Option+1 and Cmd+Option+4 do the same as Cmd+1 and Cmd+4 (2026-11-12, the keys of the columns in Dev mode).
- 2a: the log, over the whole width right of 1: graph, description (after the labels of branches, tags and worktrees), short hash, author, date. Its first row is "Uncommitted changes" of the current worktree, always present ("No changes" when the tree is clean), and selected when the mode opens.
- 2b: the files. With "Uncommitted changes" selected: the staged files over the unstaged files, with the Commit button in the header of the staged list. With a commit selected: its message, hash, author and date, then its files.
- 3b: the diff of the file selected in 2b: unified, read-only, a button on each hunk.
- The mode takes the whole window, as Organize mode does: the log needs its five columns and the diff its width. 4a is hidden by default and keeps running (see 4a on demand). The header stays (the selected task, the project dropdown), with a third mode button, "Review".

Entering and leaving:

- Cmd+Shift+G enters Review mode from Dev or Organize mode; pressed again, it goes back to the mode it came from. VS Code opens its Source Control view with Ctrl+Shift+G, but Brain Fry takes only Cmd keys, since terminals receive Ctrl. Cmd+B still goes to Organize mode, and Esc to Dev mode, as in Organize mode. `g` and `4` (see 4a on demand) join `OUR_KEYS` (app.js) and `FORWARD` (browser.py), else the keys do nothing while a browser tab has the focus. In a code editor of 3a, the key replaces CodeMirror's "find previous" (Shift+Enter in its search field still does it).
- The repository is the one that holds the selected task's `cwd` (`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`): a task in a worktree opens its worktree, and a project that is a subfolder of a repository shows the whole repository. Cmd+P (another project) changes the repository.
- No task selected (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-13: "if there's no active task, it's empty. I would like it to default to the selected project in the project dropdown"): the repository of the dropdown's project, that is the project of the idle screen's task, or the project picked with no open task. Its folder, not the idle task's `cwd`: a task in a worktree opens the main tree, and a click in panel 1 changes it. The front end sends `project:<path>` as the id of the `git.*` commands (`renderReview` in `review.js`), which `core.py` accepts for a registered project only (`Core._project_of`), as for `fs.*`. Open (2b, 3b) puts the file in the view of Dev mode as a project tab (`ownerFor`); the view is taken before the switch to Dev mode, which may select an inbox item. Tested: `test_commands_of_review_mode` (`git.state` and `git.log` with `project:<path>`, an unregistered path refused) and a sandbox selftest (window visible): the idle screen's project, then a project with no task, then Open. Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- The flow for an inbox item whose digest kind is review: select it in 1a, Cmd+Shift+G, read and stage, commit, Cmd+Shift+G, answer the agent in 4a.
- A project outside any git repository: one line of text says so. No `git init` button.

4a on demand:

- Renaud, 2026-11-10: 4a in Review mode is useful, but space is short, so it must come in and out. Cmd+4 shows it and hides it again; it is hidden by default, and the window remembers the choice (`reviewAgent` in `window.json`, see App, "Window settings").
- Shown, it takes the right of the window at full height, as in Dev mode, with the width that it has in Dev mode: its PTY keeps its size, so Claude Code does not re-render. Panels 2b and 3b give way.
- While 4a's terminal has the focus, it takes the keys, as in Dev mode: the single-letter keys of Review mode act only while one of the mode's panels has the focus. The guard that drops terminal input outside Dev mode (see Modes) lets 4a through while it is on screen in Review mode.

1, worktrees and branches:

- Worktrees (`git worktree list --porcelain`): the main tree first ("main"), then the others by folder name. Each row shows its branch or "detached at <hash>", its number of changed files, and the status dots of the tasks whose `cwd` is inside it; a click on a dot selects that task. A worktree whose folder is gone (prunable) is dimmed. A click on a row makes that worktree the current one: the first row of 2a and the lists of 2b show its changes, and the log marks its HEAD. When the mode opens, the current worktree is the one that holds the selected task's `cwd`.
- Branches: the local branches, each with its ahead/behind counts against its upstream (`%(upstream:track)`) and the worktree that has it checked out. A click selects its tip in the log.
- Read-only: no checkout, no new branch, no rename, delete, merge or rebase, and no worktree is created or removed here. Agents work in these trees, and a checkout changes files under a running agent. These actions go through a terminal of 3b or through an agent. Creating worktrees belongs to the section Worktrees, if it is built.
- No sections for stashes, remotes, tags or submodules (Renaud: "I don't need to stash anything").

2a, the log:

- Commits of all local branches, of the remote-tracking branches, and the detached HEADs of the worktrees (`git log --branches --remotes <heads> --date-order`), newest first. A toggle "Current branch" keeps only the history of the current worktree's HEAD (SourceTree's "All branches" / "Current branch"). Stashes are never shown.
- Labels before the description: local branches, remote branches, tags, and "wt: <name>" on the HEAD of each worktree. The hash has 7 characters, the author is the name only, the date is local ("10 Nov 14:03", with the year when it is not the current one).
- The graph: the backend computes the lanes (a list of open lines of descent, which each commit continues or opens), and the front end draws them in an SVG per row, one color per lane. No library.
- 1,000 commits are loaded at first, then 1,000 more when the list comes near its end. Each load asks again from the first commit (up to 20,000) and the backend computes the lanes again, so it keeps no state between pages. The graph is one small SVG per row.
- A selected commit: 2b shows its message and its changed files (`git diff-tree -r -M --name-status`, against the first parent; a root commit against the empty tree), 3b the diff of a file, with no buttons.
- When the refs change (see Refresh), the log reloads and the selected commit stays selected, by hash.

2b, the files:

- "Uncommitted changes" selected: two lists, Staged files over Unstaged files (SourceTree's order), each flat and sorted by path (the file name, then its folder in gray). Each row has a status letter: M, A, D, R (old --> new), T, `?` (untracked), U (conflict). A file that is partly staged appears in both lists.
- Line counts (Renaud, 2026-11-11): on the right of each row, before the stage button, `+15 -12` (green, red, monospace), the lines that the diff of 3b adds and removes. A side that is zero is left out (`+3`, `-40`); a binary file, a conflict, a pure rename, a change of mode only and a folder of untracked files show nothing. The same on the files of a commit. From `git diff --numstat -z` (`--cached -M` for the staged list; `diff-tree --numstat` for a commit), two more git calls per poll, for the current worktree only (`git.line_counts`, called by `git.state`; not for the counts of panel 1). An untracked file has no diff: `git._count_lines` counts its lines as git would (binary when a NUL byte is in its first 8000 bytes, no count above the 2 MB that 3b shows, none for a symlink), and reads it again only when its size or mtime changed.
- A button on each row (as VS Code's + and -) stages or unstages the whole file, and Space does it for the selected row. Each list's header has Stage all / Unstage all.
- During a merge, a rebase or a cherry-pick, a banner says so, and the conflicted files come first, in their own group. Staging one marks it resolved, as `git add` does. The resolution itself happens in the editor of Dev mode or through the agent.
- Untracked files are listed one by one (`-uall`), up to 1,000; beyond that, by folder.
- Right-click on a file: Open (switches to Dev mode, file tab in 3a), Discard changes (unstaged files only, see 3b), Copy path, Copy relative path.
- Commit (Renaud, 2026-11-10: no message area on screen, a button that opens the message): a Commit button in the header of the staged list, enabled while at least one file is staged; Cmd+Enter anywhere in the mode does the same. It opens a dialog: the branch and the worktree, the staged files, a field for the message, Cancel and Commit. Cmd+Enter in the field commits; Esc closes the dialog and keeps the draft. The backend runs `git commit -F -` with the message on stdin and `GIT_EDITOR=true`, so that no editor opens. The repository's hooks run; when the commit fails, the dialog stays open and shows their output. A commit that takes more than 60 s is stopped (for example, a signing key that waits for a passphrase). The draft is kept per worktree until the commit, in memory only. No amend (Renaud).

3b, the diff:

- Unified, 3 lines of context, two columns of line numbers, removed lines in red and added lines in green, no syntax highlighting (as SourceTree). An unstaged file shows index --> work tree (`git diff`), a staged file HEAD --> index (`git diff --cached`), a file of a commit its first parent --> the commit.
- Each hunk header has its buttons: "Discard hunk" and "Stage hunk" in an unstaged file, "Unstage hunk" in a staged file. N and P move to the next and previous hunk; Space stages or unstages the hunk at the cursor.
- Open (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-13: "one button on the top right of the file diff: Open --> opens in dev mode"): the last button of the bar of 3b, at its right end. It does what Open in the right-click menu of 2b does: Dev mode, the file in a tab of 3a, which takes the keyboard. Shown only when that menu item is enabled: not for a deleted file, a folder of untracked files, a file of a commit (its text differs from the work tree), or a file outside the task's project. No key. Code: `openable`, `openReviewFile` in `review.js`, shared with the menu. It was first named `openInDev`, which replaced app.js's `openInDev(id)` (one global scope): opening a card of the board read the file "undefined" instead of selecting the task (Renaud, 2026-11-13; renamed, and `tests/test_web_globals.py` now catches such names). The error `id.startsWith` on null in bf.log at 11:27 that day was the same bug, before Review mode had been opened in that run (checked on 2026-11-14): with `R.key` null, `relPath` gave null and the function called `openFile(null, null)`, whose `viewTabs` calls `wsProject(null)`; a selftest that calls `openFile(null, null)` gets the same message. With Review mode opened once (from 11:28 on), `relPath(id)` gave "undefined", hence the 9 reads of `.../adele/undefined` (every card click went to the project of the last Review view). `wsProject` keeps no guard for null: the caller is fixed, and a guard would only move the error. Tested with a sandbox selftest (an unstaged, an untracked and a deleted staged file; the window was covered, the editor still took the keyboard). Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted.
- Single lines (Renaud, 2026-11-10): a click on a + or - line selects it, Shift+click extends the selection, Cmd+click adds a line, Esc clears it. While a hunk has selected lines, its buttons become "Stage n lines" (or "Unstage n lines") and "Discard n lines", and Space acts on the lines. The patch keeps the hunk's context and only the selected changes, as git gui does: to stage, an unselected - line becomes context and an unselected + line is dropped; to unstage (`--reverse`), an unselected + line becomes context and an unselected - line is dropped. `git apply --recount` recounts the lines, so the counts of the @@ line do not matter. The front end sends the indexes of the lines with the hash of the hunk, so the check below applies to lines too.
- Discard (Renaud, 2026-11-10): a hunk, selected lines, or a whole file, in unstaged files only (index --> work tree); a staged change must be unstaged first. Cmd+Backspace (Brain Fry's key for Delete in 2a) or the button, then a confirmation, as in SourceTree and VS Code. The backend applies the patch to the work tree with `git apply --reverse`, after it saved that patch in `<project>/.brain_fry/discarded/<time>-<file>.patch`; the toast offers Undo, which applies it again. An untracked file goes to the Trash of macOS, as Delete in 2a does. Discard rewrites a file under a running agent, which checkout also does; it is allowed because it touches only what Renaud chose, after a confirmation, and can be undone.
- A hunk is staged as git gui does it: a patch made of the file's header and that one hunk, given to `git apply --cached` (`--reverse` to unstage). The front end sends the path and a hash of the lines of the hunk that it shows. The backend runs the diff again, looks for a hunk with the same lines (its position may have moved), and applies its own bytes. The patch is never rebuilt from the page's text: line endings (CRLF), encodings and trailing spaces must stay exact to the byte.
- If no hunk has the same lines (the agent rewrote that part of the file, or ran `git add`), nothing is applied, whether to stage, unstage or discard: a toast says that the file changed, and the diff reloads. A change elsewhere in the file does not block the hunk on screen. A discard therefore never removes lines that Renaud has not seen.
- After a stage or an unstage, the lists and the diff reload. The selection stays on the file, or moves to the next file of the same list when the file left it. The current hunk keeps its number, so it is now the next hunk.
- Whole file only, no hunks: untracked files (shown in full as added, text up to 2 MB as in 3a), files that are new in the index or deleted (a hunk of them cannot be applied alone), binary files ("Binary file."; their sizes are not shown), renames and copies, changes of mode only, submodules (the old and new commit). For these the bar of 3b has the buttons Stage file, Unstage file, Discard file (Move to Trash for an untracked file) or Mark resolved.
- The selected lines stay inside one hunk: a click in another hunk starts a new selection. They are cleared when their hunk changes.
- Text selection and copy (asked by Renaud on 2026-11-15: "ability to select and copy text from a file's diff"): pywebview injects `body { user-select: none }` (its `text_select` option, off by default), so nothing in the window could be selected. The text of the diff's lines now can (`-webkit-user-select: text` on `.ln`; this WebKit ignores the unprefixed property), not the line numbers, the signs or the hunk headers. A drag, a double click (a word) and a triple click (a line) select text as anywhere else, and leave the lines picked for staging as they were: a click whose press moved and that leaves a selection is no click, and the second click of a double click restores the picks of before the first (`R.beforeClick`). Shift+click and Cmd+click on a + or - line still pick lines, with no text selection. Cmd+C (Copy of the Edit menu) puts plain text on the pasteboard, the lines one per row, without "\ No newline at end of file" (the `copy` handler of `#rv-diffbody`); Cmd+A with the focus in the diff selects the whole diff, since nothing else on screen can be selected. Tested with a sandbox selftest (synthetic events and ranges: a copy across two hunks, a drag, a double click).
- A diff of more than 5,000 lines shows the first 5,000 and a "Show all" button.

Refresh:

- While Review mode is on screen: the status of the current worktree every 2 s, at once when a state push shows that a task of the project changed (a hook event), and after each action of the mode. The diff of the selected file is asked again at each refresh and drawn again only when its signature changes, at the same scroll position. The counts of the other worktrees (panel 1) every 10 s. Nothing runs while the mode is hidden.
- Every git call of a poll uses `--no-optional-locks`. Without it, `git status` refreshes the index and holds `index.lock` for that moment, and an agent's `git add` or `git commit` at that moment fails with "index.lock exists". Git added the option for tools that poll in the background.
- The log reloads only when the refs or the worktrees' HEADs change (`git for-each-ref`, checked at the same poll).
- A stage, an unstage or a commit that meets `index.lock` (an agent runs git) retries for 1 s, then says so in a toast.

Backend and front end:

- `git.py`: every call goes through the git CLI (`git -C <worktree>`, `-z` outputs, `-c core.quotepath=off`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, no pager), with the cleaned environment of the children (`CHILD_ENV_DROP`) and a timeout. No pygit2 or GitPython: one more dependency, and the CLI behaves exactly as the one that agents and Renaud run (worktrees, hooks, config, the lock).
- Commands on `/ctl`, with a request number: `git.state` (worktrees, branches, the status of the current worktree), `git.log` (a page), `git.commit_info`, `git.diff`, `git.stage`, `git.unstage` and `git.discard` (a file, a hunk or lines), `git.undo_discard`, `git.commit`. Git data is not part of the pushed state: the front end asks for it while the mode is on screen. `git.state` also lists, under each worktree, the tasks of the project whose folder is in it (core.py passes them).
- `web/review.js` and `web/review.css`: the four panels, the commit dialog (`#rv-dialog`), the graph, the keys of the mode. In app.js, `setMode`, `render` and the key handler call into review.js; #dev holds the panels (`#review`) beside 4a, and the classes `review` and `rv-agent` on #dev hide 1, 2a, the middle column and 4a. Keys are single letters while a panel of the mode has the focus, as in Organize mode; not while 4a or the commit dialog has it. Splitters and remembered sizes as in Dev mode.

Tests:

- pytest on a repository that a fixture builds in `tmp_path`: two branches and a merge, a second worktree, a CRLF file, an untracked file, a rename, a binary file. Checks: the lanes of the graph; staging hunk 2 of 3 leaves only that hunk in `git diff --cached`, and unstaging it restores the index; staging and unstaging 1 line of a hunk of 3 changes; a hunk whose lines changed is refused; a discard and its Undo give back the same file; a discarded untracked file goes through `files._move_to_trash` (replaced in tests); the retry on `index.lock`; every status letter of `--porcelain=v2`; a commit whose hook fails; the line counts (staged, unstaged, untracked, binary, a rename, a commit). A test of a rename must keep the file at least 50 % similar: the 1-line `b.txt` of the fixture that gains a line is seen as a delete and an add, not a rename.
- A sandbox selftest (2026-11-10): a repository with a merged branch, a tag, a worktree with its own task, 3 unstaged hunks, a staged file and an untracked one. Checked, with events sent by JS: Cmd+Shift+G in and out (the selection is kept); a click then a Shift+click select 2 lines and the buttons read "Discard 2 lines" and "Stage 2 lines"; Esc clears them; Space stages 1 line; Discard of a hunk, then Undo in the toast; Cmd+Enter opens the dialog and Cmd+Enter in it commits; a click on the other worktree shows its changes and its HEAD; Cmd+4 shows 4a at its Dev width (508 px, no resize of the PTY) and hides it; Down and Tab in 2b; a commit row shows its message, files and diff. No JS error. Screenshots looked as the mockup, after two fixes: the path in the bar of 3b disappeared when 3b was narrow (the bar now wraps), and "Graph" ran into "Description".

Built in one go on 2026-11-10 (Renaud: "go build it"), not in the five steps that this spec had planned.

Relation to other sections:

- v2's review with a diff per thread is a different thing. Review mode shows the repository: since HEAD, the changes of every agent and every turn are mixed. A diff per task or per turn could later appear as extra rows at the top of the log. With a worktree per task (see Worktrees), "Uncommitted changes" of the task's worktree is already the diff of that task.
- Renaud's `land` (`~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`) could become an action on a worktree in panel 1. Not part of this spec.

## v2

- Views. v1 ignores them, even if `projects.json` holds some.
- `bf import`: create idle tasks from all past sessions of a project at once. The region of other sessions under the board (step 8) did it one session at a time until its removal on 2026-11-11.
- Review with a diff and evidence, for each thread: the files that the turn changed, the tests that ran and their results, and a visible difference between "the agent says it is done" and "I checked". 3a's "Diff vs HEAD" shows every change since the last commit, mixed across turns and across agents. In the transcripts, while two agents ran, two of them shared a project folder 67% of the time, so a clean diff per task needs one worktree per task (see Worktrees). Sources for the diff of one turn: Claude Code's checkpoints (`file-history-snapshot` entries in the transcript, internal format), or a snapshot of the work tree at UserPromptSubmit and at Stop (`git stash create` leaves the work tree alone, but skips untracked files). Line comments on the diff go back to the agent as one prompt (as in the Claude desktop app, Conductor, Vibe Kanban).

He tests in daily use and reports what is wrong: "archive them, i have tested a lot of stuff and i report all things that bug me" (Renaud, 2026-11-14). Closed entries are in DESIGN_ARCHIVE.md. A sentence such as "Renaud has not tried it yet; the app must be restarted" in the other sections of this file, or in an entry below that stays open for another reason, is history.

- Review of 2026-11-14 (Claude; "what is missing to be feature-complete", from bf.log, hook.log, the 130 task files and the 445 prompts of events.jsonl since 2026-11-10). Claude's definition of feature-complete, four tests: the status can be trusted (no task that waits for Renaud shows as working, no thread has a wrong label); everything in his spec is built; the loop of brain_fry.md closes inside the app (take one item, load its context, decide, close); the app holds at his real volume (about 30 new tasks per day). Renaud's decision, the items that he picked: the StopFailure hook, the stale orange through `idle_prompt`, a background result that joins the open thread, the layout that survives a restart, a planned card that starts with its notes, a simple search on the board, archived tasks moved to an archive folder, a question at quit for unsaved edits, and three bug fixes (the shutdown code that rarely runs, a tab without a path read as a file, `wsProject(null)`). Built the same day: see the sections of each area. Proposed by Claude and not picked by Renaud (he did not say why; do not build them unasked): views (sets of projects; the only concept of his spec without code; Claude's argument was that "Move other projects to Next" changes prios and the way back is card by card, and that on 2026-11-13 five parked tasks of other projects held a reply that had waited 11 to 38 hours); the files that a turn changed, kept in its thread as links to "Diff vs HEAD" (`review` is the most frequent kind, 132 of 376 digested threads); renaming a task from the title of an inbox row; the prompt in gray on a new card until its first digest. Claude's recommendation, not a decision of Renaud: leave unbuilt the reply trailer (no digest failure is logged since 2026-11-10 and the kind arrives about 5 s after the reply; the accuracy of the kinds was not measured), the resume note (its trigger is a move into later, more later or never, and none of the 130 tasks is in those columns), and sessions that survive a restart or a closed window (10 of 445 turns were cut by the end of a session, in a week with 23 starts of the app). Fork is in the spec and built, with no use in the window in those 4 days.
- Seen by the agents on 2026-11-14 and not fixed (Renaud closed the conversation without asking for them): `install.py`, about lines 96-98, `install()` crashes when `~/.claude/settings.json` holds a value that is not a list for one of the hook events; `digest.py`, about line 124, a `claude -p` digest that runs at the moment of a quit is not ended, and its result is lost; the command `files.set` and `actions.set_open_files` log at INFO at every change of the open tabs (521 lines of bf.log in 4 days), unlike `grid.set` and `window.set`; `web/files.js`, about line 701, a comment still says "the grid that the window remembers"; Claude Code fired the same `<task-notification>` as 7 UserPromptSubmit events within 1 s (werewolves `ee8c522e`, 2026-11-12 11:22:00), harmless since such a prompt joins the open thread; `taskfile.py`, about line 130, a frontmatter that parses as a list or a plain string makes `meta.items()` raise AttributeError, which `_read_all` does not skip (it skips ValueError, YAMLError and TypeError), so one such file makes the read fail for its whole project (older than this day's work, the same with both YAML loaders; offered to Renaud, not asked for). The full suite passed with all the changes of the day together (287 tests after the libyaml loader and the removal of `bf consume`, `bf digest` and `bf list`). Not seen in real use yet: the migration of the archived task files at the first start, a `StopFailure` payload, the alert at quit, a planned card opened in the window, and whether running sessions pick up a newly registered hook event without a restart.

## Rejected

- Kept on purpose after the code cleanup of 2026-11-11 (six agents read every line; about 150 lines of code went, out of about 15,000; Renaud: "the rest is fine"), so a later cleanup need not ask again: `live` in `views.task_json` (only tests read it), the defensive parsing of `taskfile.loads` (hand edits, unknown keys), the `source` branch of `events.typed_by_me`, the `rename` command, `presentActionPopup` and the `_close` fallback in `app/browser.py` (a later WebKit), the ad and tracker routes of `tests/window/browser/site.py`, `CT_IMAGES` in copytab.js (a copy of `files.IMAGE_TYPES`), the four copies of the toast "Start a task first" (Project tabs may remove them), the duplicate fixtures of `test_shell_info.py`, and `raise AssertionError("unreachable")` in `git.run` (for type checkers).

- Declaring pyobjc (AppKit, Foundation, WebKit) in pyproject.toml (Renaud, 2026-11-11, in the cleanup): the code imports it directly, but it comes through pywebview's macOS dependencies, and that is enough.

- The commands `bf consume`, `bf digest` and `bf list`, removed on 2026-11-14. Renaud: "i don't need the CLI, i think. just the basics. remove: consume digest list". They ran the reader, the digest and a text list of the tasks by hand, for testing without the app; the app does the first two, and its window shows the tasks. What stays: `bf install`, `bf uninstall`, `bf ublock`, `bf init`, and `bf` alone (the window). Removed with them: `digest.digest_project`, `digest.pending_tasks`, `views.board_order`, and the test of `bf list --all`. `consume.lock` stays (Naming and files).

- Simplifications that Renaud refused on 2026-11-10 (a review of what would remove the most code; sizes are code plus tests, out of about 9,100 lines): the browser of 3a (about 2,070 lines with its spikes, 23% of the code: "this is what we ended up selecting"; a middle way, keeping the browser but dropping uBlock and the saved session cookies, about 400 lines, was not taken up), the PDF viewer (PDF.js, 5.4 MB vendored: "I want to keep the PDF viewer"), and the selection as context (about 200 lines across 7 files: "I need it"). Accepted then: one project order (see Project dropdown), the removal of the region of other sessions (done on 2026-11-11), the deletion of the spikes that are not needed, and the split of DESIGN.md.

- For PDFs (2026-11-10): WebKit's own PDF viewer in an `<iframe>` (unknown whether WKWebView in pywebview shows it; a reload would go back to page 1, because the page cannot read the plugin's position; the plugin may take Brain Fry's keys while it has the focus), and a native PDFKit view laid over the web view (a native layout under a web layout; the selftest snapshots would not show it). For the bytes of images and PDFs: base64 over `/ctl` (a third larger, and a big PDF would hold up the state and the commands).

- For the browser of 3a (researched on 2026-11-10). Renaud's Firefox inside the window: macOS cannot put another app's window inside a window, Gecko embeds only on Android, and Servo (Rust API since April 2026) has no Python bindings and fails logins (Gmail with two-factor). Streaming a Firefox window into the panel: Firefox's remote protocol gives screenshots, not a stream; ScreenCaptureKit plus forwarded input needs two permissions and handles input badly. Firefox beside Brain Fry, and an `<iframe>`: Renaud, "not an option". Chromium through Qt WebEngine: the whole app would move to pywebview's Qt backend, for the same Manifest V3 uBlock Lite. Chromium through CEF: cefpython's Mac build is a pre-release, "tested in CI only" (2026-11-03).

- For the replies in 4a (2026-11-10), to get the look and the links of the VS Code panel. Renaud: "too complex". Replacing 4a with a chat drawn in HTML over stream-json, as the VS Code extension does: weeks of work (input with slash commands and @-mentions, permission dialogs, AskUserQuestion, plan mode, interrupts, diffs), and 4a would no longer be a pure Claude Code terminal, which Renaud likes. A rendered copy of each reply (markdown-it, KaTeX) in the thread tab of 3a: about a day. Inside the terminal, the look of the panel (proportional font, inline code on a grey chip, code blocks in a box) cannot be had: xterm.js draws one monospace font, and Claude Code decides what goes in the cells. For file links: a link provider that finds `path:line` in the terminal's text (about half a day; a path that Claude Code wraps is lost), and `--append-system-prompt` asking Claude to write file references as Markdown links (untested on `--resume`, and against the CLI's own `path:line` rule).

- For Review mode (Renaud, 2026-11-10): amend, a search in the log, the history of one file. A field for the commit message always on screen under the lists: replaced by a dialog.
- For the diff of Review mode (2026-11-10): CodeMirror's merge view, which "Diff vs HEAD" uses in 3a. Its chunks come from its own diff and act on the document, while staging must apply git's own hunk, byte for byte.

- sqlite: tasks are markdown files, the app is a viewer over them.
- Sonnet through the API: needs a key and billing. `claude -p` uses the subscription.
- A TUI (Textual + tmux), the first design. Textual has no mature terminal widget (checked 2026-11-09: textual-tty 0.4.0 is pre-alpha and has no scrollback, textual-terminal is unmaintained since June 2024, Toad's shell is not a separate package). And tmux panes are rectangles, so 3b could not sit under 3a.
- Native Swift + SwiftTerm: the lightest and most Mac-like option. But the whole app would be in Swift, there is no known track record of Claude Code in SwiftTerm, the editor and diff components are weaker, and Claude can test a native window less easily.
- Electron: ships Chromium, not lightweight. Tauri: the backend would be in Rust.
- tmux as the host of the sessions, under the GUI: sessions would survive the app, but the scrollback would live in tmux, not in xterm.js.
- A separate inbox-triage agent session: 4a is the selected task's own session.
- A done column.
- A list of all tasks in Dev mode: the board does that.
- Notifications.
- `term.scrollToBottom()` each time a task comes on screen in 4a (2026-11-15). Renaud's screenshot showed 4a ending mid-history, with no input box ("the bottom of the Claude Code window is hidden"). Claude read it as a scroll position left behind (xterm.js 5.5 stops following the output after an upward scroll request, and a terminal that was `display: none` may come back scrolled up; `showTerm` and `focusTerm` never scroll; not verified) and built the change, untested. The cause was Renaud's own ctrl-o in Claude Code (its transcript view); toggling again restored the screen. The change was reverted. The sizes were checked and are fine: the window fills the screen's visible frame, 4a has 88 rows at 1410 px, and the PTY and xterm agree. For a report of this kind, ask first whether ctrl-o was toggled.
